Weight-filtered convergent complex
Yukiyoshi Nakkajima, Atsushi Shiho

Abstract




{\rm ABSTRACT}

Using log convergent topoi,
we define two fundamental filtered complexes

(E_{\rm conv},P)
and
(C_{\rm conv},P)

for the log scheme obtained by a smooth scheme
with a relative simple normal crossing divisor
over a scheme of characteristic
p>0
.
Using
(C_{\rm conv},P)
, we prove the

p
-adic purity. As a corollary of it,
we prove that
(E_{\rm conv},P)
and
(C_{\rm conv},P)

are canonically isomorphic.
These filtered complexes produce
the weight spectral sequence of
the log convergent cohomology sheaf
of the log scheme.
We also give the comparison theorem
between the projections of
(E_{\rm conv},P)
and

(C_{\rm conv},P)

to the derived category of
bounded below filtered complexes of sheaves of modules in
the Zariski topos of the log scheme and
the weight-filtered isozariskian filtered complex

(E_{\rm zar},P)\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

of the log scheme defined in
[
NS1
]
.

{\bf Contents}

§
1
. Introduction

§
2
.
Log convergent topoi and the systems of
log universal enlargements

§
3

§
4
. Log convergent linearization functors. II

§
5
. Vanishing cycle sheaves in log convergent topoi

§
6

Log convergent linearization functors
of smooth schemes with relative SNCD’s

§
7
.
(Bi)simplicial log convergent topoi

§
8
. Weight-filtered convergent complexes
and
p
-adic purity

§
9
.
The functoriality of weight-filtered convergent
and isozariskian complexes

§
10
. Boundary morphisms

§
11
.
Comparison theorem

§
12
.
Log convergent cohomologies
with compact support

References

1
Introduction

This paper is a continuation of
[
NS1
]
, a companion of

[
Sh3
]
and
[
Nak2
]
.

First we recall results in
[
NS1
]
.

Let
{\cal V}
be a complete discrete valuation ring
of mixed characteristics
(0,p)

whose residue field
\kappa
is perfect.
Let
\pi
be a nonzero element of the maximal ideal of

{\cal V}
.
Let
S
be a
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme in the sense of
[
O1
]
,
that is, a noetherian formal scheme over
{\rm Spf}({\cal V})

with the
p
-adic topology which is topologically of finite type
over
{\rm Spf}({\cal V})
.
Set
S_{n}:=\underline{\rm Spec}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}/\pi^{n}{\cal O}_{S})

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
.
Let
X
be a smooth scheme over
S_{1}
and
let
D
be a relative SNCD(=simple normal crossing divisor)
on
X/S_{1}
.
Stimulated by
[
Ka
]
and
[
Fa
]
,
we have constructed an
fs(=fine and saturated) log structure

M(D)
on
X_{\rm zar}

with morphism
M(D)\longrightarrow({\cal O}_{X},*)
in
[
NS1
]
.
If there exists the following cartesian diagram

\begin{CD}D\cap V@>{\subset}>{}>V\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\underline{\rm Spec}_{S_{1}}({\cal O}_{S_{1}}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{d}]/(x_{1}\cdots x_{a}))@>{\subset}>{}>\underline{\rm Spec}_{S_{1}}({\cal O}_{S_{1}}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{d}])\end{CD}

(
a,d\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}

a\leq d
)
for an open subscheme
V
of
X
,
where the right vertical morphism
in the diagram above is étale,
then

(M(D)|_{V}\longrightarrow({\cal O}_{V},*))=(({\cal O}_{V}^{*}x_{1}^{\mathbb{N}}\cdots x_{a}^{\mathbb{N}},*)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}({\cal O}_{V},*)).

By abuse of notation, we denote
the log scheme
(X,M(D))
by
(X,D)
.

Let
f\colon X\longrightarrow S_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S
be
the structural morphism.
In this Introduction, we assume
that the ideal sheaf
\pi{\cal O}_{S}
of

{\cal O}_{S}
has a PD-structure
\gamma

when we consider (an object of)
a (restricted) (log) crystalline site or
(that of) a (restricted) (log) crystalline topos.
Let
{\rm Crys}(X/S)
and
(X/S)_{\rm crys}

be the crystalline site of
X/(S,\pi{\cal O}_{S},\gamma)

and the crystalline topos of
X/(S,\pi{\cal O}_{S},\gamma)
defined in

[
B1
]
and
[
BO
]
, respectively.
Let
{\rm Rcrys}(X/S)
be the restricted crystalline site of

X/(S,\pi{\cal O}_{S},\gamma)
:

{\rm Rcrys}(X/S)
is a subsite of
{\rm Crys}(X/S)

whose objects are isomorphic to

(U,\mathfrak{D}_{U,\gamma}({\cal U}),[~])
’s,
where
U
is an open subscheme of
X
and

{\cal U}
is a smooth scheme over
S_{n}
for some
n

which contains
U
as a closed subscheme over
S_{n}

and
\mathfrak{D}_{U,\gamma}({\cal U})
is
the PD-envelope of
U
in
{\cal U}

over
(S_{n},\pi{\cal O}_{S_{n}},\gamma)
.
(The definition of
{\rm Rcrys}(X/S)
here
is slightly different from that in
[
B1
]

and it is a special case of the definition in
[
Tj
]
.)
Let

(X/S)_{\rm Rcrys}
be the restricted crystalline topos of

X/(S,\pi{\cal O}_{S},\gamma)
associated to
{\rm Rcrys}(X/S)
.
Let
Q^{\rm crys}_{X/S}\colon(X/S)_{\rm Rcrys}\longrightarrow(X/S)_{\rm crys}

be a morphism of topoi defined in
[
B1
]
:
for an object
E
of

(X/S)_{\rm crys}
,
Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X/S}(E)

is the natural restriction of
E
to

{\rm Rcrys}(X/S)
.
Let
{\cal O}_{X/S}
be the structure sheaf in
(X/S)_{\rm crys}
.
Then we have a natural morphism

Q^{\rm crys}_{X/S}\colon(({X/S})_{\rm Rcrys},Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X/S}({\cal O}_{X/S}))\longrightarrow(({X/S})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{X/S})

of ringed topoi.
Let
((X,D)/S)_{\rm crys}
be
the log crystalline topos of
(X,D)
over
(S,\pi{\cal O}_{S},\gamma)
defined in
[
Ka
]
and
let
{\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}
be
the structure sheaf in
((X,D)/S)_{\rm crys}
.
Let

\epsilon^{\rm crys}_{(X,D)/S}\colon(((X,D)/S)_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})\longrightarrow((X/S)_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{X/S})

be the morphism of topoi induced by
the natural morphism

(X,M(D))\longrightarrow(X,{\cal O}^{*}_{X})
of log schemes over
S_{1}
.
Let

u^{\rm crys}_{X/S}\colon((X/S)_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{X/S})\longrightarrow(X_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))

be the natural projection. Set

\overline{u}^{\rm crys}_{X/S}:=u^{\rm crys}_{X/S}\circ Q^{\rm crys}_{X/S}

as in
[
B1
]
.
Let
\tau
be the canonical filtration on a complex.
Let
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal O}_{X/S})
be the derived category of bounded below
filtered complexes of
{\cal O}_{X/S}
-modules and let

{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
be the derived category of bounded below
filtered complexes of
f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})
-modules.
In
[
NS1
]

we have defined the following four filtered complexes:

(E_{\rm crys}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P):=(R\epsilon^{\rm crys}_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),\tau)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal O}_{X/S}),

(E_{\rm zar}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P):=Ru^{\rm crys}_{X/S*}((E_{\rm crys}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P))\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})),

(C_{\rm Rcrys}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X/S}({\cal O}_{X/S})),

(C_{\rm zar}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P):=R\overline{u}^{\rm crys}_{X/S*}((C_{\rm Rcrys}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P))\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})).

The definition of
(C_{\rm Rcrys}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P)

is as follows (the definition below
is essentially the same as that in

[
NS1
]
and
[
Nak2
]
; but it is
a more general description than that).

Let
X=\bigcup_{i}X_{i}
be an open covering of
X
.
Set
X_{0}:=\coprod_{i}X_{i}
.
Let
\pi_{0}\colon X_{0}\longrightarrow X
be
the natural morphism.
Set
D_{0}:=\pi^{-1}_{0}(D)
and

(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet})_{\bullet\in{\mathbb{N}}}:={\rm cosk}_{0}^{(X,D)}(X_{0},D_{0})
.
Let
\pi\colon X_{\bullet}\longrightarrow X
be
the augmentation morphism.
Assume that there exists
a simplicial immersion

(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}_{\bullet}

into a formally log smooth
simplicial log
p
-adic formal scheme over
S
.
(If each member of the open covering of
X
is
an affine scheme, we can easily show the existence of
this immersion.)
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{\cal P}_{\bullet}
is
topologically of finite type over
S
.
For an immersion
Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}

of fine log formal schemes over
S
,
let
Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}

be the exactification of this immersion
defined in
[
Sh3
]
.
(In (
2.7
) below we shall recall the exactification.)
Then, by the universality of the exactification, we have
the simplicial exact immersion

(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}
over
S
.
Let

L^{\rm crys}_{X_{\bullet}/S}\colon\{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}\text{-modules}\}\longrightarrow\{{\cal O}_{X_{\bullet}/S}\text{-modules}\}

be the linearization functor for

{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}
-modules in
[
BO
]
.
(Note that, though the topology of

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

is not necessarily
p
-adic, the topology of
the simplicial PD envelope of the simplicial immersion

X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

is
p
-adic; we can use the theory in [loc. cit.].)
For a log formal scheme
{\cal Q}
over
S
, let us denote by

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}/S}

the log de Rham complex of relative log differential forms on

{\cal Q}/S
.
Let
P=\{P_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
be the filtration
on
\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}/S}
defined by the following formula:
for
0\leq k\leq i
,

P_{k}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}/S}:={\rm Im}(\Omega^{k}_{{\cal Q}/S}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{\cal Q}}\Omega^{i-k}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}/S}\longrightarrow\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}/S})
.
By the essentially same proof as that of
[
NS1
, (2.2.17)]
,
we can prove that the natural morphism

(1.0.1)

Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X_{\bullet}/S}L^{\rm crys}_{X_{\bullet}/S}(P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S})\longrightarrow Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X_{\bullet}/S}L^{\rm crys}_{X_{\bullet}/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S})

is injective in the category of

Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X_{\bullet}/S}({\cal O}_{X_{\bullet}/S})\text{-modules}
.
Here we need to take good care of the following related fact with this injectivity
and the following related question with it.
In
[
NS1
, (2.7.11)]
we have also proved that
the natural morphism

(1.0.2)

L^{\rm crys}_{X_{\bullet}/S}(P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S})\longrightarrow L^{\rm crys}_{X_{\bullet}/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S})

is not injective in the category of

{\cal O}_{X_{\bullet}/S}\text{-modules}
in general.
Let

L^{\rm crys}_{X_{\bullet}/S}\colon\{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}}\text{-modules}\}\longrightarrow\{{\cal O}_{X_{\bullet}/S}\text{-modules}\}

be the linearization functor for

{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}}
-modules in
[
BO
]
.
We do not know whether the natural morphism

Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X_{\bullet}/S}L^{\rm crys}_{X_{\bullet}/S}(P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}/S})\longrightarrow Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X_{\bullet}/S}L^{\rm crys}_{X_{\bullet}/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}/S})

is injective in the category of

Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X_{\bullet}/S}({\cal O}_{X_{\bullet}/S})\text{-modules}
. We are not interested in this question
because the filtration
P
on

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}/S}
is useless in general
(see (
6.2
) below for this).

Let

\pi_{{\rm Rcrys}}\colon(({X_{\bullet}/S})_{\rm Rcrys},Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X_{\bullet}/S}({\cal O}_{X_{\bullet}/S}))_{\bullet\in{\mathbb{N}}}\longrightarrow(({X/S})_{\rm Rcrys},Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X/S}({\cal O}_{X/S}))

be the natural morphism of ringed topoi (
[
NS1
, (1.6)]
).
Then, set

\displaystyle(C_{\rm Rcrys}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P):=R\pi_{{\rm Rcrys}*}(Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X_{\bullet}/S}L^{\rm crys}_{X_{\bullet}/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}),\{Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X_{\bullet}/S}L^{\rm crys}_{X_{\bullet}/S}(P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S})\}_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}).

By the essentially same proof as that of the well-definedness
of
(C_{\rm Rcrys}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P)
in
[
NS1
]
or
by the log crystalline version of
the proof of (
8.4
) below
or (
1.0.3
) below, we can prove that

(C_{\rm Rcrys}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P)
depends only on

(X,D)/(S,\pi{\cal O}_{S},\gamma)
.

In
[
NS1
, (2.7.3)]
we have proved that
there exists an isomorphism

(1.0.3)

Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X/S}(E_{\rm crys}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(C_{\rm Rcrys}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P)

by showing the following
p
-adic purity

(1.0.4)

Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X/S}R^{k}\epsilon^{\rm crys}_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X/S}a^{(k)}_{{\rm crys}*}({\cal O}_{D^{(k)}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm crys}(D/S))\quad(k\in{\mathbb{N}}),

where
D^{(k)}
is a scheme over
S_{1}

defined in
[
NS1
, (2.2.13.2)]

and will be recalled in §
6
below,

a^{(k)}\colon D^{(k)}\longrightarrow X
is the natural
morphism of schemes over
S_{1}
and

\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm crys}(D/S)\in({D^{(k)}/S})_{\rm crys}

is the crystalline orientation sheaf defined in

[
NS1
, (2.2.18)]
,
which is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{Z}}
non-canonically
(cf. §
6
below).
As a corollary of the existence of
the canonical isomorphism (
1.0.3
),
we obtain the following canonical isomorphism

(1.0.5)

(E_{\rm zar}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(C_{\rm zar}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P)

by using the formula

R\overline{u}_{X/S*}^{\rm crys}Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X/S}=Ru_{X/S*}^{\rm crys}

in
[
B1
]
.

However, unfortunately,

(1.0.6)

R^{k}\epsilon^{\rm crys}_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})\not=a^{(k)}_{{\rm crys}*}({\cal O}_{D^{(k)}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm crys}(D/S))\quad(k\in{\mathbb{N}})

in general; the natural morphism

{\cal O}_{X/S}\longrightarrow R^{0}\epsilon^{\rm crys}_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})

is not an isomorphism in general (
[
NS1
, (2.7.11)]
).
The non-equality (
1.0.6
) tells us that
we have to consider the undesirable pull-back

Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X/S}
in the isomorphism
(
1.0.4
).
To be worse, the non-equality gives us
an undesirable consequence.
Indeed, set

S_{n}:=\underline{\rm Spec}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}/\pi^{n}{\cal O}_{S})

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 2})
and endow

S_{n}
with the induced PD-structure
on
\pi{\cal O}_{S}/\pi^{n}{\cal O}_{S}
by
\gamma
.
In
[
B1
]
Berthelot has pointed out that,
for a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}Y@>{g}>{}>Z\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{1}@>{}>{}>S_{n}\end{CD}

of schemes,
there does not exist a morphism

g_{\rm Rcrys}\colon({Y/S_{1}})_{\rm Rcrys}\longrightarrow({Z/S_{n}})_{\rm Rcrys}
of topoi fitting into
the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}({Y/S_{1}})_{\rm Rcrys}@>{g_{\rm Rcrys}}>{}>({Z/S_{n}})_{\rm Rcrys}\\
@V{Q_{Y/S_{1}}}V{}V@V{}V{Q_{Z/S_{n}}}V\\
({Y/S_{1}})_{\rm crys}@>{g_{\rm crys}}>{}>({Z/S_{n}})_{\rm crys}\end{CD}

in general. As a result,
we do not know whether

(C_{\rm Rcrys}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P)
is
contravariantly functorial.
(However we know the contravariant functoriality of

(C_{\rm zar}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P)

by the canonical isomorphism (
1.0.5
).)

Let
{\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal O}_{X/S})

be the category of
filtered positively-graded graded commutative dga’s
of
{\cal O}_{X/S}
-algebras
and let
{\rm D}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal O}_{X/S}))

be the localized category of

{\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal O}_{X/S})

inverting the weakly equivalent isomorphisms
in
{\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal O}_{X/S})

(cf.
[
GM
]
).
Let

U_{\rm crys}\colon{\rm D}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal O}_{X/S}))\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal O}_{X/S})
be
the natural forgetful functor.
By the non-injectivity of the morphism
(
1.0.2
), it seems that there does not exist
a functorial filtered object

(\widetilde{C},\widetilde{P})\in{\rm D}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal O}_{X/S}))

such that

Q^{{\rm crys}*}_{X/S}U_{\rm crys}((\widetilde{C},\widetilde{P}))\simeq(C_{\rm Rcrys}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P)
.

To avoid these undesirable consequences,
we ignore torsions and
use the framework of (log) convergent topoi by Ogus
(
[
O2
]
,
[
O4
]
);
our approach in this paper turns out a success as follows.

Let
({X/S})_{\rm conv}
be the convergent topos
of
X/S
which is the relative version of
the convergent topos in
[
O2
]
.
Let
{\cal K}_{X/S}
be the isostructure sheaf in

({X/S})_{\rm conv}
.
Let
({(X,D)/S})_{\rm conv}(=({(X,D)/S})_{{\rm conv},{\rm zar}})
be the log convergent topos
of
(X,D)/S
which is the relative version of
the log convergent topos in
[
Sh2
]
.
(In §
2
below we shall define
the topoi
({X/S})_{\rm conv}
and

({(X,D)/S})_{\rm conv}
.)
Let
{\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}
be the isostructure sheaf in

({(X,D)/S})_{\rm conv}
.
Let

\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D)/S}\colon(({(X,D)/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(X,D)/S})\longrightarrow(({X/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{X/S})

be the morphism of ringed topoi forgetting the log structure along
D
.
Set
{\cal K}_{S}:={\cal O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Let

u^{\rm conv}_{X/S}\colon(({X/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{X/S})\longrightarrow({X}_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))

be the canonical projection.
Let
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S})
be the derived category of bounded below
filtered complexes of
{\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}
-modules and let

{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
be the derived category of bounded below
filtered complexes of
f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})
-modules.
In this paper we define the following four filtered complexes

(E_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P):=(R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),\tau)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{X/S}),

(E_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P):=Ru^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}((E_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P))\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S})),

(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{X/S})

and

(C_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P):=Ru^{\rm conv}_{X/S*}((C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P))\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))

in the case where
X
is separated over
S_{1}
.
In the text we shall give the definition of

(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)
,
which is analogous to that of

(C_{\rm Rcrys}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P)
.
We call
(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)

the
weight-filtered convergent complex
of
(X,D)/S
,
which is the title of this paper.

In this paper we prove the following three theorems:

Theorem 1.1

(
p
-adic purity).

Assume that
X
is separated over
S_{1}
.
Then there exists a canonical isomorphism

(1.1.1)

R^{k}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}a^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{D^{(k)}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(D/S))\quad(k\in{\mathbb{N}}),

where
\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(D/S)
is the convergent
orientation sheaf which will be defined in
§
6
below.

Theorem 1.2

(
Comparison theorem between
(E_{\rm conv},P)

and
(C_{\rm conv},P)
).

Assume that
X
is separated over
S_{1}
.
Then there exists a canonical isomorphism

(1.2.1)

(E_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P).

Consequently there exists a canonical isomorphism

(1.2.2)

(E_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(C_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P),

and
(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)
and

(C_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)
are
contravariantly functorial.

Theorem 1.3

(
Comparison theorem
between
(C_{\rm isozar},P)

and
(C_{\rm zar},P)
).

Assume that
X
is separated over
S_{1}
.
Then there exists a canonical isomorphism

(1.3.1)

(C_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(C_{\rm zar}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}.

Let
{\rm D}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{X/S}))

be the analogous localized category to

{\rm D}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal O}_{X/S}))

which is obtained by the replacement of

{\cal O}_{X/S}
by
{\cal K}_{X/S}
.
Let

U_{\rm conv}\colon{\rm D}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{X/S}))\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{X/S})
be
the natural forgetful functor.
By the construction of

(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)
,
by the derived direct image of Thom-Whitney (
[
Nav
]
)
and by the single complex of Thom-Whitney ([loc. cit.]),
we see that there exists
a functorial filtered object

(\widetilde{C},\widetilde{P})\in{\rm D}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{X/S}))

such that

U_{\rm conv}((\widetilde{C},\widetilde{P}))\simeq(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)
.
Thus we need not consider the undesirable consequences
in the log crystalline case mentioned above if we ignore torsions
and use log convergent topoi.

As in the crystalline case in
[
B1
]
,
we give a well-known remark about
(
1.1.1
) as follows.

Set
U:=X\setminus D
. Let
j\colon U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}X

be the open immersion and let
j_{\rm conv}\colon({U/S})_{\rm conv}\longrightarrow({X/S})_{\rm conv}

be the induced morphism of topoi by
j
.
We remark that

(1.3.2)

R^{k}j_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{U/S})=0\quad(\forall k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}).

In particular,

(1.3.3)

R^{k}j_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{U/S})\not\simeq a^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{D^{(k)}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(D/S))\quad(\exists k\in{\mathbb{N}})

in general ((
8.9
) below).
Thus
R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D)/S*}
is better
than
Rj_{{\rm conv}*}

in the
p
-adic case, which is different from
the
l
-adic case in
[
Fu
]
,
[
FK
]
and
[
I
]
.

Let
(X,D)
be as above or
a smooth scheme with an SNCD
over the complex number field
{\mathbb{C}}
.
Set
U:=X\setminus D
and let
j\colon U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}X

be the open immersion.
Then, as in
[
NS1
, (0.0.0.22)]
,
we can give the following translation, which fills blanks (modulo torsion) in [loc. cit.]:

(1.3.4)

/{\mathbb{C}}

(log) convergent topoi

U_{\rm an}
,
(X_{\rm an},D_{\rm an})

{((X,D)/S)}_{\rm conv}

X_{\rm an}

{(X/S)}_{\rm conv}

j_{\rm an}\colon U_{\rm an}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}X_{\rm an}
,

\epsilon_{\rm top}\colon(X_{\rm an},D_{\rm an})^{\log}\longrightarrow X_{\rm an}

\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D)/S}\colon{((X,D)/S)}_{\rm conv}\longrightarrow{(X/S)}_{\rm conv}

Rj_{{\rm an}*}({\mathbb{Q}})=R\epsilon_{{\rm top}*}({\mathbb{Q}})~

(
[
KN
]
)

R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S})

X_{\rm an}\longrightarrow X

u^{\rm conv}_{X/S}\colon({X/S})_{\rm conv}\longrightarrow{X}_{\rm zar}

{\mathbb{Q}}_{(X_{\rm an},D_{\rm an})}

{\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}

{\mathbb{Q}}_{X_{\rm an}}

{\cal K}_{X/S}

(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\rm an}/{\mathbb{C}}}(\log D_{\rm an}),\tau)

(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),\tau)

=(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\rm an}/{\mathbb{C}}}(\log D_{\rm an}),P)

=(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)

(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/{\mathbb{C}}}(\log D),P)

(C_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)

Here
(X_{\rm an},D_{\rm an})^{\log}
is
the real blow up of
(X_{\rm an},D_{\rm an})
defined
in
[
KN
]
and

\epsilon_{\rm top}

is the natural morphism of topological spaces
which is denoted by
\tau
in [loc. cit.].
(See
[
NS1
, (2.7.0.1)]
for the analogous objects
in the
l
-adic case which can be inserted
in (
1.3.4
).)

The contents of this paper are as follows.

In §
2
(resp. §
3
)
we give the definition of a log convergent topos
and the system of log universal enlargements
(resp. log convergent linearization functor)
and give basic properties of them.

In §
4
we give the compatibility of
the log convergent linearization functor with
a closed immersion of log schemes.

In §
5

we prove the Poincaré lemma of the vanishing cycle sheaf

R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D)/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{(X,D)/S}(E))

for a coherent crystal
E
of
{\cal K}_{X/S}
-modules.

In §
6
we give fundamental results for
log convergent linearization functors
of smooth schemes with relative SNCD’s.

In §
7
we review
the (bi)simplicial topoi of log convergent topoi.

In §
8

we give the definition of

(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)

and prove (
1.1
) and (
1.2
).

Let
f^{(k)}\colon D^{(k)}\longrightarrow S
be the structural morphism.
Set
f^{\rm conv}_{D^{(k)}/S}:=f^{(k)}\circ u^{\rm conv}_{D^{(k)}/S}

and
f^{\rm conv}_{(X,D)/S}:=f\circ u^{\rm conv}_{(X,D)/S}
.
In §
9

we construct the following weight spectral sequence

(1.3.5)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,h+k}

\displaystyle=R^{h-k}f^{\rm conv}_{D^{(k)}/S*}({\cal K}_{D^{(k)}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S))(-k)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow R^{h}f^{\rm conv}_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}).

In §
9
we give the contravariant functoriality of
the weight-filtered convergent and isozariskian complexes.

In §
10
we give an explicit description of
the boundary morphism of the weight spectral sequence
(
1.3.5
).

In §
11
we prove (
1.3
).
Set

f^{\rm crys}_{D^{(k)}/S}:=f^{(k)}\circ u^{\rm crys}_{D^{(k)}/S}

and

f^{\rm crys}_{(X,D)/S}:=f\circ u^{\rm crys}_{(X,D)/S}
.
As a corollary of (
1.3
),
(
1.3.5
) turns out to be canonically isomorphic to
the following weight spectral sequence

(1.3.6)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,h+k}

\displaystyle=R^{h-k}f^{\rm crys}_{D^{(k)}/S*}({\cal O}_{D^{(k)}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{{\rm crys}}(D/S))(-k)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow R^{h}f^{\rm crys}_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}},

which has been constructed in
[
NS1
]
.
As a corollary of the comparison of (
1.3.5
)
with (
1.3.6
),
if
X
is proper over
S_{1}
,
then we obtain the
E_{2}
-degeneration of (
1.3.5
)
by reducing it to that of (
1.3.6
)
which we have already obtained in
[
NS1
]
.
In the same section, as another corollary of (
1.3
),
we deduce the filtered base change theorem
(resp. filtered Künneth formula)
of
(E_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)
from
the filtered base change theorem
(resp. filtered Künneth formula) of

(E_{\rm crys}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S}),P)
which
we have already obtained in [loc. cit.].

In fact, we give the theorems above
for a truncated simplicial smooth scheme with
a truncated simplicial SNCD over
S_{1}

satisfying a certain condition.
(A split truncated simplicial smooth scheme
with a split truncated simplicial SNCD over
S_{1}

satisfies this condition.)

In §
12
we define the
filtered complex

(E_{\rm conv,c}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{X/S})

which can be considered as
the compact support version
of
(E_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)
.
We prove a filtered Poincaré duality
by using
(E_{\rm conv,c}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)

and
(E_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),P)
.

In a future paper we would like to
construct an analogous theory
for a proper SNCL(=simple normal crossing log) scheme
over a family of log points.

Notations.

(1) The log structure of a log scheme in this paper is
a sheaf of monoids in the Zariski topos.
For a log (formal) scheme
S
,

\overset{\circ}{S}
denotes
the underlying (formal) scheme of
S
and

{\cal K}_{S}
denotes
{\cal O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.
More generally, for a ringed topos
({\cal T},{\cal O}_{\cal T})
,

{\cal K}_{\cal T}
denotes

{\cal O}_{\cal T}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.
For a morphism
f\colon X\longrightarrow S
of log (formal) schemes,

\overset{\circ}{f}
denotes the underlying morphism

\overset{\circ}{X}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
of (formal) schemes.

(2) SNCD=simple normal crossing divisor.

(3) For a morphism
X\longrightarrow S
of log (formal) schemes,
we denote by

\Omega^{i}_{X/S}
(
=\omega^{i}_{X/S}
in

[
Ka
]
) the sheaf of
(formal) relative logarithmic differential forms on
X/S

of degree
i

(i\in{\mathbb{N}})
.

(4) For a commutative monoid
P
with unit element
and for a formal scheme
S

with an ideal of definition of
{\cal I}\subset{\cal O}_{S}
,
we denote by
{\cal O}_{S}\{P\}

the sheaf
\varprojlim_{n}({\cal O}_{S}[P]/{\cal I}^{n}{\cal O}_{S}[P])

in the Zariski topos
{S}_{\rm zar}
of
S
.
We denote by
\underline{\rm Spf}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}\{P\})

the log scheme whose underlying scheme is

\underline{\rm Spf}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}\{P\})

(with an ideal of definition
{\cal I}{\cal O}_{S}\{P\}
)
and whose log structure
is associated to the natural morphism

P\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{S}\{P\}
of sheaves of monoids in
S_{\rm zar}
.
When
{\cal I}=0
, we denote

\underline{\rm Spf}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}\{P\})
by

\underline{\rm Spec}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}\{P\})
.

For a log formal scheme
S

with an ideal of definition of
{\cal I}\subset{\cal O}_{S}
,
we denote by
\underline{\rm Spec}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}/{\cal I})

the log scheme whose underlying scheme is

\underline{\rm Spec}_{\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal O}_{S}/{\cal I})

and whose log structure
is the inverse image of the log structure of
S

by the natural closed immersion

\underline{\rm Spec}_{\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal O}_{S}/{\cal I})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S
.

(5)
For a morphism
f\colon X\longrightarrow S
and
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
,
we denote

\underset{(i+1)~{\rm pieces}}{\underbrace{X\times_{S}X\times_{S}\times\cdots\times_{S}X}}
by
X(i)
.

Conventions.

We use the conventions about signs in

[
NS1
]
and
[
Nak2
]
.

2
Log convergent topoi and the systems of
log universal enlargements

In this section we define the relative versions of basic notions
about the log convergent topos in
[
O4
]
and
[
Sh2
]

which is a log version of the convergent topos in
[
O2
]
.
In
[
Sh3
]
the second named author
has also developed an analogous theory.
As in
[
O2
]
,
[
Sh2
]
and
[
Sh3
]
,
we define a (pre)widening and
the log universal enlargement of it and
show basic properties of
the log universal enlargement.

Let
{\cal V}
be a complete discrete valuation ring
of mixed characteristics
(0,p)

with perfect residue field
\kappa
.
Let
S
be a fine log formal scheme
whose underlying formal scheme is a

p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme
in the sense of
[
O1
]
:

\overset{\circ}{S}
is a noetherian formal scheme
over
{\rm Spf}({\cal V})

with the
p
-adic topology
which is topologically of finite type over
{\rm Spf}({\cal V})
.
We assume that
\overset{\circ}{S}
is
flat over
{\rm Spf}({\cal V})
.
We call
S
a fine log
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme.
Let
\pi
be a non-zero element of the maximal ideal
of
{\cal V}
. Following
[
O2
]
, we denote by
S_{1}

the exact closed log subscheme of
S
defined by
the ideal sheaf
\pi{\cal O}_{S}
.
Let
Y
be a fine log scheme over
S_{1}

whose underlying scheme is of finite type
over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
.
As in
[
O4
, p. 212]
and
[
Sh2
, Definition 2.1.8]

(see also
[
Sh3
, I (2.1)]
),
we consider a quadruple
(U,T,\iota,u)
,
where
u\colon U\longrightarrow Y
is a strict morphism of
fine log schemes over
S_{1}
and

\overset{\circ}{u}\colon\overset{\circ}{U}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{Y}
is a morphism of schemes
of finite type,

T
is a fine log noetherian (not necessarily
p
-adic)
formal scheme over
S

whose underlying formal scheme
is topologically of finite type over
\overset{\circ}{S}

and
\iota\colon U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T
is
a closed immersion over
S
.
Here we have assumed the condition
“the strictness of
u
”,
which has not been assumed
in
[
Sh2
, Definition 2.1.1, 2.1.9]

and
[
Sh3
, I (2.1), (2.2)]
.
We shall use this strictness
essentially in §
5
below.
We call the
(U,T,\iota,u)

a
prewidening
of
Y/S
.
If
\overset{\circ}{T}
is
p
-adic, we say that

(U,T,\iota,u)
is a
p
-
adic

prewidening
of
Y/S
.
Let
T_{0}
be the maximal reduced log subscheme of
the exact closed log subscheme of
T

defined by
\pi{\cal O}_{T}
.
Let
\widetilde{T}
be a log formal scheme whose underlying scheme
is
\underline{\rm Spf}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{T}/(\pi{\textrm{-}}{\rm torsion}))

and whose log structure is
the pull-back of the log structure of
T
.
By imitating
[
O2
, (1.1)]
and

[
Sh2
, Definition 2.1.1, 2.1.9]
,
we obtain the notion of a widening, an exact (pre)widening
and an enlargement of
Y/S
as follows (see also
[
Sh3
, I (2.2)]
).

(1) We say that
a (
p
-adic) prewidening

(U,T,\iota,u)
of
Y/S

is a (
p
-
adic
)
widening
of
Y/S
if

\overset{\circ}{\iota}\colon\overset{\circ}{U}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{T}

is defined by an ideal sheaf of definition of
\overset{\circ}{T}
.

(2) We say that a (
p
-adic) (pre)widening

(U,T,\iota,u)
of
Y/S

is
exact
if
\iota
is exact.

(3) We say that an exact widening

(U,T,\iota,u)
of
Y/S
is
an
enlargement
of
Y/S
if
\overset{\circ}{T}
is flat over

{\rm Spf}({\cal V})
and if
T_{0}\subset U
;
the latter condition is equivalent to an inclusion

(2.0.1)

({\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{T}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{U}))^{N}\subset\pi{\cal O}_{T}\quad(\exists N\in{\mathbb{N}}).

(As noted in
[
O2
, (1.2)]
, the topology of
\overset{\circ}{T}
is
p
-adic.)

We define a
morphism of
(
p
-adic
)

(
exact
)

(
pre
)
widenings and
a morphism of enlargements
in an obvious way.
For a morphism

f\colon(U,T,\iota,u)\longrightarrow(U^{\prime},T^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},u^{\prime})
of
(
p
-adic) (exact) (pre)widenings
or enlargements of
Y/S
, we denote the morphism

T\longrightarrow T^{\prime}
of fine log (
p
-adic) formal schemes over
S

by
f\colon T\longrightarrow T^{\prime}
by abuse of notation.
As in the log crystalline case in
[
Ka
, (5.2)]
,
for a morphism
(U,T,\iota,u)\longrightarrow(U^{\prime},T^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},u^{\prime})

of exact widenings of
Y/S
, we can easily show
that the morphism
T\longrightarrow T^{\prime}
is strict by using
the strictness of the morphisms
U\longrightarrow Y
and
U^{\prime}\longrightarrow Y
.
As usual, we denote
(U,T,\iota,u)
simply by
T

if there is no risk of confusion.

By imitating
[
O2
, p. 135]
and

[
Sh2
, Definition (2.1.3)]
, we define
the log convergent site

{\rm Conv}(Y/S)(={\rm Conv}_{\rm zar}(Y/S))

as follows (see also
[
Sh3
, I (2.4)]
):
the objects of
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)

are the enlargements of
Y/S
; the morphisms
of
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
are the morphisms of enlargements of

Y/S
; for an object
(U,T,\iota,u)
of

{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
, a family

\{(U_{\lambda},T_{\lambda},\iota_{\lambda},u_{\lambda})\longrightarrow(U,T,\iota,u)\}_{\lambda}

of morphisms in
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
is defined to
be a covering family of
(U,T,\iota,u)
if

T=\bigcup_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}
is a Zariski open covering and if
the natural morphism
U_{\lambda}\longrightarrow T_{\lambda}\times_{T}U
is an isomorphism.
Let
(Y/S)_{\rm conv}

be the topos associated to the site
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
.
An object
E
in
(Y/S)_{\rm conv}

gives a Zariski sheaf
E_{T}

in the Zariski topos
T_{\rm zar}
of
T

for
(U,T,\iota,u)\in{\rm Ob}({\rm Conv}(Y/S))

and a morphism

\rho_{f}\colon f^{-1}(E_{T^{\prime}})\longrightarrow E_{T}

for a morphism

f\colon T\longrightarrow T^{\prime}
of enlargements of
Y/S
.
As in
[
O2
]
and
[
Sh2
]
,
for a prewidening
T
of
Y/S
,
we denote simply by
T

the following sheaf

h_{T}\colon{\rm Conv}(Y/S)\owns T^{\prime}\longmapsto\{\text{the morphisms $T^{\prime}\longrightarrow T$ of prewidenings
of $Y/S$}\}\in({\rm Sets})

on
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
.
Let
{\cal K}_{Y/S}
be an isostructure sheaf in

(Y/S)_{\rm conv}
defined by
the following formula

\Gamma((U,T,\iota,u),{\cal K}_{Y/S}):=\Gamma(T,{\cal O}_{T})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

for
(U,T,\iota,u)\in{\rm Ob}({\rm Conv}(Y/S))
.

Let
f\colon Y\longrightarrow S_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S

be the structural morphism.
Let
Y_{\rm zar}
be the Zariski topos of
Y
and
let

(2.0.2)

u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}\colon(Y/S)_{\rm conv}\longrightarrow Y_{\rm zar}

be a morphism of topoi
characterized by a formula

u^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S}(E)(T)=\Gamma(U,u^{-1}(E))
for a sheaf

E
in
Y_{\rm zar}
and an enlargement
T=(U,T,\iota,u)
of
Y/S
.
This morphism is
the obvious relative version of
the natural projection in

[
O2
, p. 146]
and
[
Sh2
, pp.90–91]
.
Set
f^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}:=f\circ u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}
.
The morphism (
2.0.2
) induces the following
morphism of ringed topoi:

(2.0.3)

u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}\colon((Y/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S})\longrightarrow(Y_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S})).

Let
{\cal V}^{\prime}
be another complete discrete valuation ring
of mixed characteristics
(0,p)
with perfect residue field.
Let
\pi^{\prime}
be a non-zero element of the maximal ideal
of
{\cal V}^{\prime}
. Let
{\cal V}\longrightarrow{\cal V}^{\prime}
be a morphism of
commutative rings with unit elements inducing a morphism

{\cal V}/\pi{\cal V}\longrightarrow{\cal V}^{\prime}/\pi^{\prime}{\cal V}^{\prime}
.
Let
S^{\prime}
be a fine log
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}^{\prime}
-scheme
such that
\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}
is flat over
{\cal V}^{\prime}
.
Set
S^{\prime}_{1}:=\underline{\rm Spec}_{S^{\prime}}({\cal O}_{S^{\prime}}/\pi^{\prime}{\cal O}_{S^{\prime}})
.
Let
Y^{\prime}
be a fine log scheme over
S^{\prime}_{1}
such that
the underlying structural morphism

\overset{\circ}{Y}{}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}_{1}
is of finite type.
For a commutative diagram

(2.0.4)

\begin{CD}Y^{\prime}@>{}>{}>S^{\prime}_{1}@>{\subset}>{}>S^{\prime}@>{}>{}>{\rm Spf}({\cal V}^{\prime})\\
@V{g}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
Y@>{}>{}>S_{1}@>{\subset}>{}>S@>{}>{}>{\rm Spf}({\cal V})\end{CD}

of fine log (formal) schemes, we have a natural morphism

g_{\rm conv}\colon((Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})\longrightarrow((Y/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S})

of ringed topoi.
We remark
that there is a point of difference in the nontrivial log case
from the trivial log case in

[
O1
, (2.2.1)]
:
for an enlargement
(U^{\prime},T^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},u^{\prime})
of
Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}
,
the quadruple
(U^{\prime},T^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},g\circ u^{\prime})
is
not necessarily an enlargement of
Y/S

since
g\circ u^{\prime}
is not necessarily strict.

The third statement in the following proposition
tells us that the choice of
\pi
is not important
for the log convergent cohomology of
the trivial coefficient as in the crystalline case
(
[
B1
, III Théorème 2.3.4
(Théorème d’invariance)]
).

Proposition 2.1
.

Assume that
{\cal V}^{\prime}={\cal V}
and that
the morphism
{\cal V}\longrightarrow{\cal V}^{\prime}
is the identity of
{\cal V}

(\pi^{\prime}
may be different from
\pi)
.
Assume also that
S^{\prime}=S
and
Y^{\prime}=Y\times_{S_{1}}S^{\prime}_{1}
.
Denote
g
in
(
2.0.4
)
by
i
.
Let
f^{\prime}\colon Y^{\prime}\longrightarrow S

be the structural morphism.
Then the following hold
:

(1)
The direct image
i_{{\rm conv}*}

from the category of abelian sheaves in

(Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv}
to
the category of abelian sheaves in

(Y/S)_{\rm conv}

is exact.

(2)
For a bounded below filtered complex

(E^{\bullet},P)
of
{\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S}
-modules,

(2.1.1)

Rf^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(i_{{\rm conv}*}((E^{\bullet},P)))=Rf^{\prime}{}^{\rm conv}_{Y^{\prime}/S*}((E^{\bullet},P))

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{S})
.

(3)

(2.1.2)

Rf^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}({\cal K}_{Y/S})=Rf^{\prime}{}^{\rm conv}_{Y^{\prime}/S*}({\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S})

in
D^{+}({\cal K}_{S})
.

Proof.

(1): For an object
(U,T,\iota,u)
of
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
,
set
U^{\prime}:=U\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}
, and
let
u^{\prime}\colon U^{\prime}\longrightarrow Y^{\prime}
be the projection and

\iota^{\prime}\colon U^{\prime}\longrightarrow T
the composite morphism

U^{\prime}\longrightarrow U\overset{\iota}{\longrightarrow}T
.
Then it is immediate to check that
(U^{\prime},T,\iota^{\prime},u^{\prime})

is an object of
{\rm Conv}(Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})
and that

(2.1.3)

i^{*}_{\rm conv}((U,T,\iota,u))=(U^{\prime},T,\iota^{\prime},u^{\prime}).

Hence, for an abelian sheaf
E
in

(Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime})_{\rm conv}
,

(2.1.4)

i_{{\rm conv}*}(E)_{(U,T,\iota,u)}=E_{(U^{\prime},T,\iota^{\prime},u^{\prime})}.

In particular,
i_{{\rm conv}*}
is exact.

(2) By (1) we see that

Ri_{{\rm conv}*}=i_{{\rm conv}*}
.
Thus we obtain (
2.1.1
).

(3): By (
2.1.4
) we have the following equality:

(2.1.5)

i_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})={\cal K}_{Y/S}.

Hence (
2.1.2
) follows from (
2.1.1
).
∎

For a fine log
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme
T
,
let
{\rm Coh}({\cal O}_{T})
be
the category of coherent
{\cal O}_{T}
-modules.
Let
{\rm Coh}({\cal K}_{T})
be the full subcategory
of the category of
{\cal K}_{T}
-modules
whose objects are isomorphic to
K\otimes_{\cal V}{\cal F}

for some
{\cal F}\in{\rm Coh}({\cal O}_{T})

(
[
O1
, (1.1)]
).
Following
[
O1
]
,
[
O2
]
,
[
O4
]
and
[
Sh2
]
,
we say that a
{\cal K}_{Y/S}
-module
E
is
a coherent isocrystal
if
E_{T}\in{\rm Coh}({\cal K}_{T})

for any enlargement
T
of
Y/S
and if

\rho_{f}\colon f^{*}(E_{T^{\prime}})\longrightarrow E_{T}
is an isomorphism
for any morphism
f\colon T\longrightarrow T^{\prime}
of enlargements of
Y/S
.

Next we recall the relative version of
the system of universal enlargements in
[
O2
, (2.1)]

and
[
Sh2
, Definition 2.1.22]

with an immediate generalization(=without using the
existence of an exactification of a (pre)widening).
(See
[
Sh3
, I (2.10), (2.11), (2.12)]

for a slightly different formulation using the existence of the exactification;
we recall it in (
2.7
) and
(
2.8
) below.)

Let
(U,T,\iota,u)
be
an enlargement of
Y/S
.
We say that
(U,T,\iota,u)
is
an enlargement of
Y/S

with radius

\leq|\pi|

if
{\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{T}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{U})\subset\pi{\cal O}_{T}
.
The following is a log version of
[
O2
, (2.1)]

and an immediate generalization of

[
Sh2
, Definition 2.1.22, Lemma 2.1.23]

for the case
n=1
in [loc. cit.],
which is a log version of
[
O1
, (2.3)]
and
[
O2
, (2.1)]
:

Proposition 2.2
.

Let
T=(U,T,\iota,u)
be
a
(p
-adic
)

(
pre
)
widening of

Y/S
. Then there exists an enlargement

T_{1}=(U_{1},T_{1},\iota_{1},u_{1})
of
Y/S

with radius
\leq|\pi|

with a morphism
T_{1}\longrightarrow T
of

(p
-adic
)

(
pre
)
widenings of
Y/S

satisfying the following universality
:

any morphism
`T:=(`U,`T,`\iota,`u)\longrightarrow T

of
(p
-adic
)

(
pre
)
widenings of
Y/S

from an enlargement of
Y/S

with radius
\leq|\pi|

factors through
a unique morphism

`T\longrightarrow T_{1}
of enlargements of
Y/S
.

Proof.

Because the question is local, we may assume
that there exists an exactification of
\iota

(cf.
[
Ka
, (5.6)]
), that is,
we may have the following commutative diagram

(2.2.1)

\begin{CD}U@>{\subset}>{}>T^{\prime\prime}\\
\Big\|@V{}V{}V\\
U@>{\subset}>{}>T,\end{CD}

where the upper horizontal morphism is
an exact closed immersion and
the right vertical morphism is log étale.
In this case, (
2.2
) follows from
the relative version of

[
Sh2
, Definition 2.1.22, Lemma 2.1.23]

(cf.
[
O1
, (2.3)]
,
[
O2
, (2.1)]
).
For the completeness of this paper, we give
the construction of
(U_{1},T_{1},\iota_{1},u_{1})
as follows.

Set
\overset{\circ}{\widetilde{T^{\prime\prime}}}:=\underline{\rm Spf}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime\prime}}({\cal O}_{T^{\prime\prime}}/(\pi{\textrm{-}}{\rm torsion}))

and
\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime\prime}:=\overset{\circ}{U}\times_{\overset{\circ}{T}}\overset{\circ}{\widetilde{T^{\prime\prime}}}
.
Let
{\cal I}^{\prime\prime}
be the defining ideal sheaf of the immersion

\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime\prime}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\widetilde{T^{\prime\prime}}}
.
Take a formal blow up of
\overset{\circ}{\widetilde{T^{\prime\prime}}}
with respect to
the ideal sheaf
{\cal I}^{\prime\prime}+\pi{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{\widetilde{T^{\prime\prime}}}}
.
Let
\overset{\circ}{B}(U,T)
be the
p
-adic completion of
this formal blowing up and let
\overset{\circ}{B}(U,T)\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T^{\prime\prime}}

be the natural morphism.
Endow
\overset{\circ}{B}(U,T)
with
the pull-back of the log structure of
T^{\prime\prime}
.
Let
B(U,T)
be the resulting log formal scheme.
Let
T_{1}
be the maximal log open formal subscheme of

B(U,T)
such that

{\cal I}^{\prime\prime}{\cal O}_{B(U,T)}\subset\pi{\cal O}_{B(U,T)}
.
Set
U_{1}:=U\times_{T}T_{1}
.
Then we have a natural immersion

\iota_{1}\colon U_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T_{1}
and
a natural morphism
u_{1}\colon U_{1}\longrightarrow Y
.
The quadruple
(U_{1},T_{1},\iota_{1},u_{1})

is a desired enlargement of
Y/S

with radius
\leq|\pi|
.
∎

Now, as in
[
O1
]
,
[
O2
]
and
[
Sh2
]
,
by replacing the ideal sheaf

{\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{\widetilde{T}{}^{\prime\prime}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{U^{\prime\prime}})

by
({\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{\widetilde{T}{}^{\prime\prime}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{U^{\prime\prime}}))^{n}

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
in (
2.2
),
we have the inductive system
\{T_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

(
T_{n}=(U_{n},T_{n},\iota_{n},u_{n})
).
We call
\{T_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

the
system of the universal enlargements

of
(U,T,\iota,u)
.
By using the condition (
2.0.1
) and
by the definition of
T_{n}
,
we have the following equality

(2.2.2)

h_{T}=\varinjlim_{n}h_{T_{n}}

as in the proof of

[
O2
, (2.1)]
and
[
Sh2
, Lemma 2.1.23]
.
We denote the log formal scheme
T_{n}

by
{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T)
.
By abuse of notation, we also denote
the enlargement
(U_{n},T_{n},\iota_{n},u_{n})

by
{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T)
.
(In
[
O2
]

(the log structures of
Y
and
T
are trivial there),
Ogus has distinguished
T_{Y,n}(T):=(U_{n},T_{n},\iota_{n},u_{n})

from
T_{U,n}(Y):=T_{n}
.)

Remark 2.3
.

Let the notations be as above.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{T}
is an affine formal scheme

{\rm Spf}(A)
and that
\iota
is an exact closed immersion.
Let
I\subset A
be the defining ideal of
\overset{\circ}{\iota}
.
Let
f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}

(r\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})

be a system of generators of
I
.
For
\underline{m}:=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{r})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r}
, set

|\underline{m}|:=\sum_{j=1}^{r}m_{j}
and

f^{\underline{m}}:=f^{m_{1}}_{1}\cdots f^{m_{r}}_{r}
.
For
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
and

\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r}

with
|\underline{m}|=n
,
let
t_{\underline{m}}
be independent variables.
Then, in the proof of
[
O1
, (2.3)]
, Ogus has given
the following description:

(2.3.1)

\Gamma({\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T),{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T)})=(A[t_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r},|\underline{m}|=n]/((f^{\underline{m}}-\pi t_{\underline{m}})+(p{\textrm{-}}{\rm torsion})))^{\widehat{}},

where the upper
\,\,\widehat{~}\,\,
on the right hand side
means the
p
-adic completion.

The following is a relative version of
[
Sh2
, Lemma 2.1.25]

which is a log version of
[
O1
, (2.4.4)]

(see also
[
Sh3
, I (2.15)]
):

Proposition 2.4
.

Let
(U,T,\iota,u)
be a
(p
-adic
)
prewidening of
Y/S
.
Let
\widehat{T}
be the formal completion of
T
along
U

(\widehat{T}
is, by definition, the log scheme whose underlying scheme
is the formal completion of
\overset{\circ}{T}
along
\overset{\circ}{U}

and whose log structure is
the pull-back of that of
T)
.
Then the natural morphism

{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(\widehat{T})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T)

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})

of enlargements of
Y/S
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

(We need an additional slight care to

[
Sh2
, Lemma 2.1.25]

since
\iota
is not necessarily exact.)
The question is local.
Let the notations be as in the proof of (
2.2
).
Let
\overset{\circ}{\widehat{T}{}^{\prime\prime}}
be the formal completion of

\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime\prime}
along
U
.
Endow
\overset{\circ}{\widehat{T}{}^{\prime\prime}}
with
the pull-back of the log structure of
T^{\prime\prime}

and let
\widehat{T}{}^{\prime\prime}
be the resulting log formal scheme.
Then we have to prove that
the natural morphism

{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(\widehat{T}{}^{\prime\prime})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T^{\prime\prime})

is an isomorphism, which is proved by
the same proof as that of
[
Sh2
, Lemma 2.1.25]

using the description (
2.3.1
):
we can easily construct the inverse morphism

{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T^{\prime\prime})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(\widehat{T}{}^{\prime\prime})

of the morphism
{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(\widehat{T}{}^{\prime\prime})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T^{\prime\prime})

since the topology of
{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T^{\prime\prime})
is
p
-adic.
∎

Lemma 2.5
.

Let
u\colon U\longrightarrow Y
be a strict morphism of
fine log schemes over
S_{1}

such that
\overset{\circ}{u}
is of finite type.
Let
T
be a fine log formal scheme over
S

whose underlying formal scheme is a
noetherian formal scheme
which is topologically of finite type over
S
.
Let
\iota\colon U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T
be a
(
not necessarily closed
)

immersion over
S
.
Let
`T
be a log open formal subscheme of
T

such that
`\overset{\circ}{T}
contains
\overset{\circ}{U}

as a closed subscheme. Then the enlargement

{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(`T)
is independent of the choice of
the fine log open formal subscheme
`T
of
T
.

Proof.

By using (
2.4
), the proof is the same as that of
[
B1
, I Proposition 4.2.1]
.
∎

Definition 2.6
.

We call the quadruple
(U,T,\iota,u)
in
(
2.5
) a
quasi-prewidening

of
Y/S
. We define a morphism of quasi-prewidenings
of
Y/S
in an obvious way. We often denote

(U,T,\iota,u)
simply by
T
.
We denote
{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(`T)
by

{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T)
and call

\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

the
system of the universal enlargements
of

(U,T,\iota,u)
.

We recall the following(=the exactification)
which has been proved in
[
Sh3
]
.

Proposition 2.7

(
[
Sh3
, Proposition 2.10]
)
.

Let
T
be a fine log formal scheme.
Let
{\cal C}^{\rm ex}_{\rm hom}

(
resp.
{\cal C})

be the category of homeomorphic exact immersions

(
resp. the category of immersions
)
of
fine log formal schemes over
T
.
Let
\iota
be the inclusion functor

{\cal C}^{\rm ex}_{\rm hom}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal C}
.
Then
\iota
has a right adjoint functor

(\quad)^{\rm ex}\colon{\cal C}\longrightarrow{\cal C}^{\rm ex}_{\rm hom}
.

Because we need the following notations later,
we recall the construction of
(\quad)^{\rm ex}
.
By the universality, the problem is local.
Let
Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}
be an object
of
{\cal C}
.
We may assume that the immersion above is closed and
that it has a global chart
(P\longrightarrow Q)
such that the morphism

P^{\rm gp}\longrightarrow Q^{\rm gp}
is surjective.
Let
P^{\rm ex}
be the inverse image of

Q
by the morphism
P^{\rm gp}\longrightarrow Q^{\rm gp}
.
Then we have a fine log formal
{\cal V}
-scheme

{\cal Q}^{{\rm loc,ex}}:={\cal Q}\widehat{\times}_{\underline{\rm Spf}_{T}({\cal O}_{T}\{P\})}\underline{\rm Spf}_{T}({\cal O}_{T}\{P^{\rm ex}\})

over
{\cal Q}
with an exact immersion

Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}^{{\rm loc,ex}}
.
(Recall
\underline{\rm Spf}_{T}
in the
Notations
(4).)
We take the formal completion of

{\cal Q}^{{\rm loc,ex}}
along
Y
, which
we denote by
{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
.
Since
\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}=\overset{\circ}{Y}

and

M_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},x}/{\cal O}^{*}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},x}=M_{Y,x}/{\cal O}^{*}_{Y,x}

(x\in\overset{\circ}{Y})
,
we see that the morphism
Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}

is a desired morphism over
T
.
By the construction above, we see that the natural morphism

{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}\longrightarrow{\cal Q}
is affine.

Remark 2.8
.

We also recall the formulation in

[
Sh3
, I (2.10), (2.11), (2.12)]
.
Let the notations be as in (
2.2
),
before (
2.3
) and in (
2.5
).
Let
\iota^{\rm ex}\colon U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T^{\rm ex}

be the exactification of
\iota
.
Set

T^{\rm ex}:=(U,T^{\rm ex},\iota^{\rm ex},u)

by abuse of notation.
Then
T_{n}=(T^{\rm ex})_{n}

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
.
Indeed
we can obtain
T_{n}
by replacing
(`T)^{\prime\prime}
with
T^{\rm ex}
.

The following is a relative log version of

[
O2
, Lemma 2.4]
(with an additional assumption)
and
[
Sh2
, Lemma 2.1.26]
(see also
[
Sh3
, I (2.16)]
):

Lemma 2.9
.

Let the notations be as in
(
2.5
)
.
Let
(U^{\prime},T^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},u^{\prime})\longrightarrow(U,T,\iota,u)
be a morphism
of quasi-prewidenings of
Y/S
such that
U^{\prime}=U\times_{T}T^{\prime}
.
Then the natural morphism

{\mathfrak{T}}_{U^{\prime},n}(T^{\prime})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T)\times_{\widetilde{T}}\widetilde{T^{\prime}}

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
is an isomorphism.
If
T^{\prime}\longrightarrow T
is flat, then the natural morphism

{\mathfrak{T}}_{U^{\prime},n}(T^{\prime})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T)\times_{T}T^{\prime}

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of

[
O2
, Lemma 2.4]
and
[
Sh2
, Lemma 2.1.26]
.
∎

Let the notations be as in
(
2.5
) and (
2.6
).
Set
T_{n}:={\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T)
.
Let
\beta_{n}\colon T_{n}\longrightarrow T

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})

be the natural morphism.
Because
h_{`T}=\varinjlim_{n}h_{T_{n}}

is independent of the choice of
`T
,
we can set

(2.9.1)

h_{T}=h_{`T}

on
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
.
Set also
(Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T}:=(Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{`T}
.
Let
E
be a sheaf in
(Y/S)_{\rm conv}
.
Set
E_{T}={\cal H}{\it om}_{(Y/S)_{\rm conv}}(h_{T},E)

as in
[
O2
, p. 140]
.
By (
2.9.1
) and (
2.2.2
),
we see that
E_{T}=\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n*}(E_{T_{n}})

as in [loc. cit.].
Let
\overset{\to}{T}
be the associated topos
to the site defined in
[
Sh2
, Definition 2.1.28]
,
which is the log version of the site in
[
O2
, §3]
.
An object of this site is
a log open formal subscheme
V_{n}
of
T_{n}

for a positive integer
n
; let
V_{m}
be
a log open formal subscheme of
T_{m}
;
for
n>m
,
{\rm Hom}(V_{n},V_{m}):=\emptyset
,
and, for
n\leq m
,

{\rm Hom}(V_{n},V_{m})

is the set of morphisms
V_{n}\longrightarrow V_{m}
’s of
log formal subschemes over the natural morphism
T_{n}\longrightarrow T_{m}
.
A covering of
V_{n}
is a Zariski open covering of
V_{n}
.
Let
\overset{\to}{T}
be the topos associated to this site.
Let
\phi_{n}\colon T_{n}\longrightarrow T_{n+1}
be the natural morphism
of enlargements of
Y/S
.
Then an object
{\cal F}
in
\overset{\to}{T}

is a family
\{({\cal F}_{n},\psi_{n})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
of pairs,
where
{\cal F}_{n}
is a Zariski sheaf in
(T_{n})_{\rm zar}

and
\psi_{n}\colon\phi^{-1}_{n}({\cal F}_{n+1})\longrightarrow{\cal F}_{n}

is a morphism of Zariski sheaves in
(T_{n})_{\rm zar}
.
Let
{\cal O}_{\overset{\to}{T}}=\{({\cal O}_{T_{n}},\phi_{n}^{-1})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

(resp.
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}=\{({\cal K}_{T_{n}},\phi_{n}^{-1})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
)
be the structure sheaf (resp. isostructure sheaf) of
\overset{\to}{T}
,
where
\phi_{n}^{-1}
is the natural pull-back morphism

\phi_{n}^{-1}({\cal O}_{T_{n+1}})\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{T_{n}}
.
(resp.
\phi_{n}^{-1}({\cal K}_{T_{n+1}})\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{T_{n}}
).
Following
[
O2
, p. 141]
,
we say that an
{\cal O}_{\overset{\to}{T}}
-module

{\cal F}
is
coherent

if
{\cal F}_{n}\in{\rm Coh}({\cal O}_{T_{n}})

for all
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

and
we say that
{\cal F}
is
admissible
(resp.
crystalline
)
if the natural morphism

{\cal O}_{T_{n}}\otimes_{\phi^{-1}_{n+1}({\cal O}_{T_{n+1}})}\phi^{-1}_{n+1}({\cal F}_{n+1})\longrightarrow{\cal F}_{n}

is surjective (resp. isomorphic).
As in [loc. cit.], we can give the definitions of
a coherent
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}
-module,
an admissible
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}
-module
and a crystalline
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}
-module.
For an object
E
of

(Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T}
and
for a morphism
T^{\prime}\longrightarrow T
of quasi-prewidenings of
Y/S
,
then we have an associated object

{\cal E}^{\prime}=\{{\cal E}^{\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

({\cal E}^{\prime}_{n}:=E_{T^{\prime}_{n}})
in
\overset{\to}{T^{\prime}}
.
Following
[
O2
, p. 147]
, we say that
a
{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{T}
-module
E
is

admissible
(resp.
crystalline
)
if
{\cal E}^{\prime}
is coherent and admissible (resp. crystalline) for any morphism
T^{\prime}\longrightarrow T

of quasi-prewidenings.
In this crystalline case we say that
E
is a crystal of

{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{T}
-module.
Let
\beta\colon\overset{\to}{T}\longrightarrow T_{\rm zar}

be the natural morphism of topoi
which is denoted by
\gamma
in
[
Sh2
, p. 55]

(=the log version of
\gamma
in
[
O1
, p. 141]
).

Let
{\cal Z}
,
{\cal Z}^{\prime}
and
T

be fine log noetherian formal schemes over
S

whose underlying formal schemes are
topologically of finite type over
S
.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{T}
is
p
-adic
and that
\overset{\circ}{T}
is flat
over
{\rm Spf}({\cal V})
.
Let
{\cal Z}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Z}^{\prime}
be a closed immersion
of fine log formal schemes over
S
.
Let
T\longrightarrow{\cal Z}^{\prime}
and
let
u\colon T\otimes_{\cal V}({\cal V}/\pi)\longrightarrow{\cal Z}

be morphisms over
S
.
Following
[
O1
, p. 784]
,
we say that
(T,u)
is an
enlargement
of
{\cal Z}/S

in
{\cal Z}^{\prime}

with radius

\leq|\pi|

if the following diagram

\begin{CD}T\otimes_{\cal V}({\cal V}/\pi)@>{\subset}>{}>T\\
@V{u}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\cal Z}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal Z}^{\prime}\end{CD}

is commutative.
We define a morphism of
enlargements of
{\cal Z}/S
in
{\cal Z}^{\prime}

with radius
\leq|\pi|
in an obvious way.

The following is a log version of
[
O1
, (2.3)]
:

Proposition 2.10
.

There exists an enlargement

({\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal Z},1}({\cal Z}^{\prime}),u_{\mathfrak{T}})

of
{\cal Z}/S
in
{\cal Z}^{\prime}
with radius
\leq|\pi|

such that, for any enlargement
(T,u)

of
{\cal Z}/S
in
{\cal Z}^{\prime}
with radius

\leq|\pi|
,
there exists a unique morphism

(T,u)\longrightarrow({\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal Z},1}({\cal Z}^{\prime}),u_{\mathfrak{T}})

such that the morphism
T\longrightarrow{\cal Z}^{\prime}
factors through

T\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal Z},1}({\cal Z}^{\prime})
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of
[
O1
, (2.3)]
and (
2.2
).
∎

As in
[
O1
, (2.5)]
, replacing

{\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{{\cal Z}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\cal Z})

by
\{{\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{{\cal Z}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\cal Z})^{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
,
we obtain the system

\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal Z},n}({\cal Z}^{\prime})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

of enlargements of
{\cal Z}/S
in
{\cal Z}^{\prime}
.
We call
\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal Z},n}({\cal Z}^{\prime})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

the
system of the universal enlargements
of
{\cal Z}/S
in
{\cal Z}^{\prime}
.

Proposition 2.11
.

The following hold
:

(1)
Let the notations be as above.
Let
\widehat{\cal Z}{}^{\prime}
be the formal completion of
{\cal Z}^{\prime}

along
{\cal Z}
.
Then the natural morphism

{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal Z},n}(\widehat{\cal Z}^{\prime})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal Z},n}({\cal Z}^{\prime})
is an isomorphism.

(2)

Let
{\cal Z}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Z}^{\prime\prime}
be an immersion
of fine log noetherian formal schemes over
S

whose underlying formal schemes are
topologically of finite type over
S
.
Let
{\cal Z}^{\prime}
be a log open formal subscheme of
{\cal Z}^{\prime\prime}

such that
\overset{\circ}{\cal Z}{}^{\prime}
contains
\overset{\circ}{\cal Z}

as a closed formal subscheme. Then the enlargement

{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal Z},n}({\cal Z}^{\prime})

(n\in{\mathbb{N}})

is independent of the choice of
the fine log open formal subscheme
{\cal Z}^{\prime}
of
{\cal Z}^{\prime\prime}
.

(
We denote
{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal Z},n}({\cal Z}^{\prime})

by
{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal Z},n}({\cal Z}^{\prime\prime})
.
)

Proof.

(1): The proof of (1) is the same as that of
(
2.4
) and
[
Sh2
, Lemma 2.1.25]
.

(2): (2) follows from (1) as in (
2.5
).
∎

Next we would like to prove (
2.14
) below.
To prove this, we need the following:

Lemma 2.12

(Weak fibration theorem (cf.
[
B3
, (1.3.2)]
))
.

Let
Z
be a fine log scheme over
S_{1}
such that

\overset{\circ}{Z}
is of finite type over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
.
Let

(2.12.1)

\begin{CD}Z@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal U}^{\prime}\\
\Big\|@V{}V{g}V\\
Z@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal U}\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram over
S
,
where the horizontal morphisms are
closed immersions into
fine log noetherian
(
not necessarily
p
-adic
)

formal schemes over
S
whose underlying formal schemes
are topologically of finite type over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Let
g^{\rm ex}\colon{\cal U}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}\longrightarrow{\cal U}^{\rm ex}

be the morphism obtained by
(\ref{cd:zuup})
.
Then the following hold
:

(1)
Assume that

\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{\rm ex}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal U}{}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal U}{}^{\rm ex}
is étale.
Let
{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}(g)\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal U}^{\prime})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal U})

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})

be the induced morphism by
g
.
Then the natural morphism

{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal U})}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}(g)_{*}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal U}^{\prime})})

is an isomorphism.

(2)
If

\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{\rm ex}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal U}{}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal U}{}^{\rm ex}

is formally smooth,
then

{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}(g)_{*}({\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal U}^{\prime})})

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
is a flat

{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal U})}
-algebra.

Proof.

In the proof of
[
B3
, (1.3.1)]

(resp.
[
B3
, (1.3.2)]
: Théorème de fibration faible),
Berthelot has essentially proved (1)
(resp. an analogous theorem to (2))
in the terms of rigid analytic spaces. Here we also give a proof of (1) (resp. (2))
for the completeness of this paper.

(1): The question is local; we may assume that the
horizontal closed immersions in (
2.12.1
) have global charts
and that
\overset{\circ}{\cal U}{}^{\prime}
and
\overset{\circ}{\cal U}

are affine.
By replacing
{\cal U}
with
{\cal U}^{\rm ex}

and
{\cal U}^{\prime}
with
{\cal U}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}
,
respectively,
we may assume that the horizontal closed immersions are exact
and that
\overset{\circ}{g}
is étale.
We may also assume that the immersions
induce isomorphisms

\overset{\circ}{Z}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\cal U}

and

\overset{\circ}{Z}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\cal U}{}^{\prime}

as topological spaces ((
2.4
)).

Set
A^{\prime}:=\Gamma({\cal U}^{\prime},{\cal O}_{{\cal U}^{\prime}})

and
A:=\Gamma({\cal U},{\cal O}_{\cal U})
.
Let
I\subset A
be the defining ideal of
the closed immersion
Z\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal U}
.
Since a chart of the log structure of
{\cal U}
gives
those of
{\cal U}^{\prime}
and
Z
, we see that
the underlying scheme of

Z\times_{\cal U}{\cal U}^{\prime}
is equal to

\overset{\circ}{Z}\times_{\overset{\circ}{\cal U}}\overset{\circ}{\cal U}{}^{\prime}
.
The natural morphism

\overset{\circ}{Z}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{Z}\times_{\overset{\circ}{\cal U}}\overset{\circ}{\cal U}{}^{\prime}

is a closed immersion and étale,
and hence an open immersion by
[
EGA IV-4
, (17.9.1)]
.
Consequently the morphism above is an
isomorphism of schemes.
Hence
IA^{\prime}
is the defining ideal of
the closed immersion

Z\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal U}^{\prime}
and
the morphism
A/I\longrightarrow A^{\prime}/IA^{\prime}
is an
isomorphism.
Let the notations be as in (
2.3
).
Then, by (
2.3.1
),
it suffices to prove that the morphism

(2.12.2)

A[t_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r},|\underline{m}|=n]/(f^{\underline{m}}-\pi t_{\underline{m}},\pi^{l})\longrightarrow A^{\prime}[t_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r},|\underline{m}|=n]/(f^{\underline{m}}-\pi t_{\underline{m}},\pi^{l})

is an isomorphism for any
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
.
However this is clear because
the morphism (
2.12.2
) is étale,
because
I
is nilpotent in

A[t_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r},|\underline{m}|=n]/(f^{\underline{m}}-\pi t_{\underline{m}},\pi^{l})

and because
the reduction mod
I
of the morphism
(
2.12.2
) is an isomorphism.

(2):
Let
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}^{r}_{S}

(r\in{\mathbb{N}})

be the formal affine space of
relative dimension
r
over
S

(
\overset{\circ}{\widehat{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{r}_{S}=\underline{\rm Spf}_{\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal O}_{S}[[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]])
;
the log structure of
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}^{r}_{S}
is,
by definition, the pull-back of
the log structure of
S
).
By (1) and (
2.4
), we may assume that

{\cal U}^{\prime}={\cal U}\times_{S}\hat{\mathbb{A}}^{r}_{S}
.
The zero-section

S\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\hat{\mathbb{A}}^{r}_{S}

induces the closed immersion

{\cal U}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal U}^{\prime}
.
For
\underline{m}:=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{r})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r}
, set

x^{\underline{m}}:=x^{m_{1}}_{1}\cdots x^{m_{r}}_{r}
.
Let
\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal U},n}({\cal U}^{\prime})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

be the system of the universal enlargements
of the closed immersion

{\cal U}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal U}^{\prime}
.
Set

\widetilde{\cal U}:=\underline{\rm Spf}_{\cal U}({\cal O}_{\cal U}/(\pi{\textrm{-}}{\rm torsion}))
.
Then

(2.12.3)

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal U},n}({\cal U}^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=({\cal O}_{{\cal U}^{\prime}}[t_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r},|\underline{m}|=n]/((x^{\underline{m}}-\pi t_{\underline{m}})+(p{\textrm{-}}{\rm torsion})))^{\widehat{}}

\displaystyle=({\cal O}_{\widetilde{\cal U}}[[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]][t_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r},|\underline{m}|=n]/(x^{\underline{m}}-\pi t_{\underline{m}}))^{\widehat{}}.

Set
B_{n}:=({\cal O}_{\widetilde{\cal U}}[[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]][t_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r},|\underline{m}|=n]/(x^{\underline{m}}-\pi t_{\underline{m}}))^{\widehat{}}
.
Since

B_{n}\mod\pi^{k}\quad(k\in{\mathbb{N}})

is a free
{\cal O}_{\widetilde{\cal U}}/\pi^{k}{\cal O}_{\widetilde{\cal U}}
-modules

(x^{\underline{l}}t_{\underline{m}}^{\underline{e}}

(|\underline{l}|<n
,

|\underline{m}|=n
,
\underline{e}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r})

form a basis), since

\overset{\circ}{\widetilde{\cal U}}
is noetherian
and since

B_{n}

is separated for the
\pi
-adic topology,
the sheaf of rings

B_{n}

is a flat

{\cal O}_{\widetilde{\cal U}}
-module by

[
Bou
, Chapitre III §5, Théorème 1]
.
Let
\widetilde{\cal U}{}^{\wedge}

be the
p
-adic completion of
\widetilde{\cal U}
.
Then

{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}(g)_{*}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal U},n}({\cal U}^{\prime})})

is a flat

{\cal O}_{\widetilde{\cal U}{}^{\wedge}}={\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal U},n}({\cal U})}
-module
for any
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
.
By the universality, we have the following natural morphism

(2.12.4)

\alpha_{n}\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal U}^{\prime})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal U})\times_{\widetilde{\cal U}{}^{\wedge}}{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal U},n}({\cal U}^{\prime}).

We claim that the following induced morphism

(2.12.5)

\alpha_{n}^{*}\colon{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal U})\times_{\widetilde{\cal U}{}^{\wedge}}{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal U},n}({\cal U}^{\prime})}\longrightarrow\alpha_{n*}({\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal U}^{\prime})})

is an isomorphism.
(Though the proof for this claim is essentially the same as the proof of
[
O1
, (2.6.2)]
,
we give the proof for this claim. See also (
2.13
) (2) below.)
We may assume that

\overset{\circ}{\cal U}
is an affine formal scheme
{\rm Spf}(A)
.
Let
y_{1},\ldots,y_{q}

(q\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})

be a system of generators of the defining ideal sheaf
of the closed immersion
Z\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal U}
.
Then

(2.12.6)

{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal U})}=({\cal O}_{\cal U}[s_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{q},|\underline{m}|=n]/((y^{\underline{m}}-\pi s_{\underline{m}})+(p{\textrm{-}}{\rm torsion})))^{\widehat{}}.

The ideal

(y_{1},\ldots,y_{q},x_{1},\ldots,x_{r})
in

\Gamma({\cal U}^{\prime},{\cal O}_{{\cal U}^{\prime}})
gives the defining ideal sheaf
of the closed immersion
Z\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal U}^{\prime}
.
Set
(z_{1},\ldots,z_{q+r})=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{q},x_{1},\ldots,x_{r})
.
Then

(2.12.7)

{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal U}^{\prime})}=({\cal O}_{{\cal U}^{\prime}}[u_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{q+r},|\underline{m}|=n]/((z^{\underline{m}}-\pi u_{\underline{m}})+(p{\textrm{-}}{\rm torsion})))^{\widehat{}}.

Set
A^{\prime}:=A[[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]]
and

C:=A^{\prime}[u_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{q+r},|\underline{m}|=n]/((z^{\underline{m}}-\pi u_{\underline{m}})+(p{\textrm{-}}{\rm torsion})),

D:=A[s_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{q},|\underline{m}|=n]/((y^{\underline{m}}-\pi s_{\underline{m}})+(p{\textrm{-}}{\rm torsion}))

and

E:=A^{\prime}[t_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r},|\underline{m}|=n]/((x^{\underline{m}}-\pi t_{\underline{m}})+(p{\textrm{-}}{\rm torsion})).

Then
C
is of finite type over
D\otimes_{A}E
.
The ring
C
is integral over
D\otimes_{A}E
.
Indeed, express
z^{\underline{m}}=y^{\underline{k}}x^{\underline{l}}

such that
|\underline{k}|+|\underline{l}|=|\underline{m}|=n
.
Then

\displaystyle\pi^{n}u^{n}_{\underline{m}}

\displaystyle=(z^{\underline{m}})^{n}=y^{n{\underline{k}}}x^{n{\underline{l}}}=y_{1}^{nk_{1}}\cdots y_{q}^{nk_{q}}x_{1}^{nl_{1}}\cdots x_{r}^{nl_{r}}

\displaystyle=(\pi^{k_{1}}s_{(n,0,\ldots,0)}^{k_{1}})\cdots(\pi^{k_{q}}s_{(0,\ldots,0,n)}^{k_{q}})(\pi^{l_{1}}t_{(n,0,\ldots,0)}^{l_{1}})\cdots(\pi^{l_{r}}t_{(0,\ldots,0,n)}^{l_{r}})

\displaystyle=\pi^{n}s_{(n,0,\ldots,0)}^{k_{1}}\cdots s_{(0,\ldots,0,n)}^{k_{q}}t_{(n,0,\ldots,0)}^{l_{1}}\cdots t_{(0,\ldots,0,n)}^{l_{r}}.

Hence
u^{n}_{\underline{m}}=s_{(n,0,\ldots,0)}^{k_{1}}\cdots s_{(0,\ldots,0,n)}^{k_{q}}t_{(n,0,\ldots,0)}^{l_{1}}\cdots t_{(0,\ldots,0,n)}^{l_{r}}
in
C

and this tells us that
C
is integral over
D\otimes_{A}E
.
Consequently
C
is finite over
D\otimes_{A}E

and

\widehat{C}=(D\otimes_{A}E)^{\widehat{}}\otimes_{D\otimes_{A}E}C
.
By this equality we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{C}\otimes_{\cal V}K=(D\otimes_{A}E\otimes_{\cal V}K)^{\widehat{}}\otimes_{(D\otimes_{A}E)\otimes_{\cal V}K}(C\otimes_{\cal V}K)=(D\otimes_{A}E)^{\widehat{}}\otimes_{\cal V}K

since
(D\otimes_{A}E)\otimes_{\cal V}K=C\otimes_{\cal V}K
.
This shows that the morphism (
2.12.5
)
is an isomorphism. We complete the proof of (2).
∎

Remark 2.13
.

(1) Let the notations be as in
[
O1
, (2.6.2)]
. We find a claim
that there exists a map

\alpha\colon T_{I^{(n)}_{Z}}(Y)\longrightarrow T_{I^{n}_{Z}}(Y)

in [loc. cit.]. However, since
I^{n}_{Z}
contains
I^{(n)}_{Z}
,
the source and the target of
\alpha
in [loc. cit.] should be in reverse, that is, there exists a map

\alpha\colon T_{I^{n}_{Z}}(Y)\longrightarrow T_{I^{(n)}_{Z}}(Y)
.

(2) Though we find a claim
in the proof of
[
O1
, (2.12)]

that “It suffices to prove this for
n=1
.”,
the reduction for the case of any
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}

to the case
n=1
seems impossible: in the notation of
the proof of [loc. cit.], we have to consider
the structure sheaf of
T_{Z,n}(Y(1))
.
Note that, though the natural morphism

T_{Z}(Y(1))\longrightarrow T_{Z}(Y)\times_{Y}T_{Y}(Y(1))

is an isomorphism
as claimed in [loc. cit.],
while the natural morphism

T_{Z,n}(Y(1))\longrightarrow T_{Z,n}(Y)\times_{Y}T_{Y,n}(Y(1))\quad(n\geq 2)

is not an isomorphism in general.
In conclusion, we do not know whether the claim in [loc. cit.]
that
p_{i}\colon T_{Z,n}(Y(1))\longrightarrow T_{Z,n}(Y)
is flat is right;
by (
2.12
) (2),
we know only that
p_{i*}({\cal K}_{T_{Z,n}(Y(1))})

is a flat
{\cal K}_{T_{Z,n}(Y)}
-algebra.

Proposition 2.14
.

Let
Y/S_{1}
be as in the beginning of this section.
Let
\iota_{Y}\colon Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}

be an immersion into
a formally log smooth log noetherian formal scheme over
S

which is topologically of finite type over
S
.
Set
{\cal Y}(1):={\cal Y}\times_{S}{\cal Y}
.
Let
\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y}(1))\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

be the system of the universal enlargements of
the diagonal immersion

Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}(1)
.
Let

p_{i,n}\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y}(1))\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})

(i=1,2)

be the natural morphism induced by the

i
-th projection

{\cal Y}(1)\longrightarrow{\cal Y}
.
Then
p_{i,n*}({\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y}(1))})

(i=1,2)
is a flat

{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})}
-algebra.

Proof.

Let
{\cal Y}^{\rm ex}
and
{\cal Y}(1)^{\rm ex}

be the exactifications of

\iota_{Y}
and the diagonal immersion

Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}(1)
, respectively.
Let

p_{1},p_{2}\colon{\cal Y}(1)^{\rm ex}\longrightarrow{\cal Y}^{\rm ex}

be the induced morphism
by the first and the second projections

{\cal Y}(1)\longrightarrow{\cal Y}
, respectively.
As in
[
Ka
, (6.6)]
, we see that
the morphisms
\overset{\circ}{p}_{1}
and
\overset{\circ}{p}_{2}

are formally smooth over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Now (
2.14
) follows from (
2.12
) (2).
∎

3
Log convergent linearization functors. I

Let
S
and
Y/S_{1}
be as in §
2
.
Let
\iota_{Y}\colon Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}

be an immersion into
a formally log smooth
log noetherian
p
-adic formal scheme over
S

which is topologically of finite type over
S
.
Let
{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})
be
the quasi-prewidening

(Y,{\cal Y},\iota_{Y},{\rm id}_{Y})
of
Y/S
.
Let

\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

(
{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y}):=(Y_{n},{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y}),Y_{n}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y}),Y_{n}\longrightarrow Y)
,

Y_{n}:=Y\times_{\cal Y}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})
)
be the system of the universal enlargements of

{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})
.
We call
\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

the
system of the universal enlargements of

\iota_{Y}
for short. By (
2.8
)
we obtain the equality

{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})={\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y}^{\rm ex})
.
Let

((Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})},{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})})

be the localized ringed topos
of
((Y/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S})

at
{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})

and let

j_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}\colon((Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})},{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})})\longrightarrow((Y/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S})

be the natural morphism of the ringed topoi and let

(3.0.1)

\displaystyle\varphi^{*}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}\colon

\displaystyle\{\text{the category of coherent crystals of }{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}\text{-modules}\}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\{\text{the category of coherent crystals of }{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}\text{-modules}\}

be a natural functor which is
a relative log version (with
\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
)
of the functor in
[
O2
, p. 147]
.
We also have the following natural functor

(3.0.2)

\varphi_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})*}\colon((Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})},{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})})\longrightarrow({\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}_{\rm zar},{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})})

by setting
\varphi_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})*}(E)(U):=E(U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y}))
.
For a coherent crystal of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}
-module

{\cal E}
, set

(3.0.3)

L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}):=j_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})*}\varphi^{*}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}({\cal E})

by abuse of notation (cf.
[
O2
, p. 152]
,
[
Sh2
, p. 95]
)
(“UE” is the abbreviation of the universal enlargement).

Definition 3.1
.

(1) We call the functor

(3.1.1)

L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}\colon\{{\rm coherent}~{\rm crystals}~{\rm of}~{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}{\textrm{-}}{\rm modules}\}\longrightarrow\{{\cal K}_{Y/S}\text{-}{\rm modules}\}

the
log convergent linearization functor with respect to

\iota_{Y}
for coherent crystals of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}
-modules.

(2) Let

\beta_{n}\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\longrightarrow{\cal Y}

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
be the natural morphism.
Let
\beta^{\rm ex}_{n}\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\longrightarrow{\cal Y}^{\rm ex}

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
be also the natural morphism.
Let
{\cal Z}
be
{\cal Y}
or
{\cal Y}^{\rm ex}

and let

\gamma_{n}\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\longrightarrow{\cal Z}

be
\beta_{n}
or
\beta^{\rm ex}_{n}
.
We call the composite functor

(3.1.2)

L^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}:=L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}\circ\gamma_{n}^{*}\colon\{{\rm coherent}~{\cal K}_{\cal Z}{\textrm{-}}{\rm modules}\}\longrightarrow\{{\cal K}_{Y/S}\text{-}{\rm modules}\}

the
log convergent linearization functor
with respect to

\iota_{Y}

for coherent

{\cal K}_{\cal Z}
-
modules
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
T=(U,T,\iota,u)
be
an exact quasi-
(
pre
)
widening of

Y/S
and let
\{T_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be
the system of the universal enlargements of
T
.
Let
\beta_{n}\colon T_{n}\longrightarrow T
be the natural morphism.
Then, for each
n
,
the pull-back functor

\beta^{*}_{n}\colon\{{\rm coherent}~{\cal K}_{T}{\textrm{-}}{\rm modules}\}\longrightarrow\{{\rm coherent}~{\cal K}_{T_{n}}{\textrm{-}}{\rm modules}\}

is exact.

Proof.

We may assume that
\iota
is an exact closed immersion.
Let the notations be as in (
2.3
).
We may assume that
\overset{\circ}{T}
is
an affine formal scheme
{\rm Spf}(A)

over
{\cal V}
.
Set
A_{n}:=(A[t_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{k},|\underline{m}|=n]/(f^{\underline{m}}-\pi t_{\underline{m}}))^{\widehat{}}
.
Then
\Gamma(T_{n},{\cal O}_{T_{n}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}=A_{n}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.
It suffices to prove that, for a finitely generated

A
-module
E
,

{\rm Tor}_{1}^{A}(A_{n},E\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})=0.

In fact, we claim that
\pi\cdot{\rm Tor}_{1}^{A}(A_{n},E)=0
.
(This implies
p\cdot{\rm Tor}_{1}^{A}(A_{n},E)=0
, and hence

{\rm Tor}_{1}^{A}(A_{n},E\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})=0
.)
Indeed,
let
\iota\colon A\longrightarrow A_{n}
be the natural inclusion
and let
\rho\colon A_{n}\longrightarrow A
be
a morphism of
A
-modules defined by
\rho(1_{A})=\pi

and
\rho(t_{\underline{m}})=f^{\underline{m}}
.
Then
\rho\circ\iota=\pi\times{\rm id}_{A}

and
\iota\circ\rho=\pi\times{\rm id}_{A_{n}}
.
The two morphisms
\iota
and
\rho
induces
the following two zero morphisms;

\iota_{*}\colon 0={\rm Tor}_{1}^{A}(A,E)\longrightarrow{\rm Tor}_{1}^{A}(A_{n},E),\quad\rho_{*}\colon{\rm Tor}_{1}^{A}(A_{n},E)\longrightarrow{\rm Tor}_{1}^{A}(A,E)=0,

respectively, and we have two relations

\iota_{*}\circ\rho_{*}=\pi
and
\rho_{*}\circ\iota_{*}=\pi
.
Hence
\pi\cdot{\rm Tor}_{1}^{A}(A_{n},E)=0
.
We finish the proof of (
3.2
).
∎

Corollary 3.3
.

Let the notations be as in
(
3.2
)
.
The category of coherent crystals of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}
-modules
is an abelian category. The kernel and the cokernel in
this category
are equal to those in the category of
coherent
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}
-modules.

Proof.

For each
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
,
the system of the universal enlargements of
T_{m}
is

\{T_{1},T_{2},\ldots,T_{m-1},T_{m},T_{m},T_{m},\ldots\}.

Let
\iota_{m,m^{\prime}}\colon T_{m^{\prime}}\longrightarrow T_{m}

(m^{\prime}\leq m)

be the natural morphism.
Because
T_{m}

(\forall m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
is
an exact enlargement of
Y/S
,
the pull-back functor

\iota_{m,m^{\prime}}^{*}\colon\{\text{coherent ${\cal K}_{T_{m}}$-modules}\}\longrightarrow\{\text{coherent ${\cal K}_{T_{m^{\prime}}}$-modules}\}

is exact by (
3.2
).
Now (
3.3
) follows from easy calculations.
∎

Remark 3.4
.

If a quasi-prewidening
(U,T,\iota,u)
of
Y/S

is not exact, the obvious analogue of
(
3.2
) does not hold in general.
Indeed, set
S:=({\rm Spf}({\cal V}),{\cal V}^{*})

and let
Y

(resp.
{\cal Y}
)
be the following log scheme over
S_{1}
(resp.
S
):

Y=({\rm Spec}(({\cal V}/\pi)[x]),({\mathbb{N}}\owns 1\longmapsto x\in({\cal V}/\pi)[x])^{a})

(resp.
{\cal Y}=({\rm Spf}({\cal V}\{x\}),({\mathbb{N}}\owns 1\longmapsto x\in{\cal V}\{x\})^{a})
.
Consider the case where
U=Y
,

T:={\cal Y}(i):=\underset{(i+1)~{\rm pieces}}{\underbrace{{\cal Y}\times_{S}{\cal Y}\times_{S}\times\cdots\times_{S}{\cal Y}}}
,

\iota
is the diagonal embedding

Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}(i)

(i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})

and
u={\rm id}_{Y}
.
Then we claim that
the pull-back functor

\{{\rm coherent}~{\cal K}_{T}{\textrm{-}}{\rm modules}\}\longrightarrow\{{\rm coherent}~{\cal K}_{T^{\rm ex}}{\textrm{-}}{\rm modules}\}

is not exact.
Indeed, set

A_{i}=\varprojlim_{n}{\cal V}\{u^{\pm 1}_{1},\ldots u^{\pm 1}_{i},x_{0},x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}\}/I^{n}

(I:=(x_{1}-u_{1}x_{0},\ldots,x_{i}-u_{i}x_{0},u_{1}-1,\ldots,u_{i}-1))
.
It is easy to check that

T^{\rm ex}=({\rm Spf}(A_{i}),({\mathbb{N}}\owns 1\longmapsto x_{0}\in A_{i})^{a})
.
We have a natural isomorphism

A_{i}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal V}\{x_{0}\}[[u_{1}-1,\ldots u_{i}-1]]
.
Set
K\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}\}:=K\otimes_{\cal V}{\cal V}\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}\}
.
Consider the exact sequence

(3.4.1)

0\longrightarrow K\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}\}\overset{x_{i}\times}{\longrightarrow}K\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}\}

and consider

K\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}\}
as
K\{x_{0},\ldots,x_{i}\}/(x_{0})

with natural
K\{x_{0},\ldots,x_{i}\}
-module.
Apply the tensorization

\otimes_{K\{x_{0},\ldots,x_{i}\}}(A_{i}\otimes_{\cal V}K)
to (
3.4.1
).
Then we have the following sequence

(3.4.2)
