0\longrightarrow K\otimes_{\cal V}{\cal V}[[u_{1}-1,\ldots,u_{i}-1]]\overset{0}{\longrightarrow}K\otimes_{\cal V}{\cal V}[[u_{1}-1,\ldots,u_{i}-1]],

which is not exact.
Thus we have shown that the claim is right.
In fact, set
B_{n}:=\Gamma(T_{n},{\cal O}_{T_{n}})

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
.
Then

(\ref{eqn:xel})\otimes_{K\{x_{0},\ldots,x_{i}\}}(B_{n}\otimes_{\cal V}K)
is not exact because

(B_{n}/(x_{0}))\otimes_{\cal V}K\not=0
and

(\ref{eqn:xel})\otimes_{K\{x_{0},\ldots,x_{i}\}}(B_{n}\otimes_{\cal V}K)

is equal to the following sequence

0\longrightarrow(B_{n}/(x_{0}))\otimes_{\cal V}K\overset{0}{\longrightarrow}(B_{n}/(x_{0}))\otimes_{\cal V}K.

Proposition 3.5
.

If the morphism
\overset{\circ}{\cal Y}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
is affine,
then the functor
L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}
in
(
3.1.1
)

is right exact.

Proof.

Let
T:=(U,T,\iota,u)
be an object of

{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
.
Then we have the natural immersion

(\iota,\iota_{Y}\circ u)\colon U\longrightarrow T\times_{S}{\cal Y}
over
S
.
We may assume that
\overset{\circ}{T}{}
is affine.
Let

\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

be the system of the universal enlargements
of this immersion.
Then we have the following diagram
of the inductive systems of enlargements of
Y/S
:

(3.5.1)

\begin{CD}\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}@>{\{p_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}>{}>\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\\
@V{\{p^{\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}V{}V\\
T.\end{CD}

Let
{\cal E}=\{{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

be a coherent crystal of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}
-modules.
Then, as in the proof of
[
O2
, (5.4)]
and

[
Sh2
, Theorem 2.3.5]
,
we have

(3.5.2)

L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E})_{T}=\varprojlim_{n}p^{\prime}_{n*}p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n})

by the definition of
L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}
((
3.0.3
)).

Let

(3.5.3)

{\cal E}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\cal E}\longrightarrow{\cal E}^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow 0\quad({\cal E}^{\prime}=\{{\cal E}^{\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty},{\cal E}^{\prime\prime}=\{{\cal E}^{\prime\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty})

be an exact sequence of coherent crystals of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}
-modules.
Set
E_{n}:=\Gamma({\mathfrak{T}}_{n},p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}_{n}))
,

E^{\prime}_{n}:=\Gamma({\mathfrak{T}}_{n},p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}^{\prime}_{n}))

and
E^{\prime\prime}_{n}:=\Gamma({\mathfrak{T}}_{n},p_{n}^{*}({\cal E}^{\prime\prime}_{n}))
.
We have to prove that

(3.5.4)

\varprojlim_{n}{E}^{\prime}_{n}\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}{E}_{n}\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}{E}^{\prime\prime}_{n}\longrightarrow 0

is exact.

Because the morphism

\overset{\circ}{T}{}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{\cal Y}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}{}
is affine and
because the integralization of a log structure is affine
(
[
Ka
, (2.7)]
),
the underlying scheme of
T\times_{S}{\cal Y}
is affine.
Because
\overset{\circ}{\cal Y}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
is affine,
it is separated: the diagonal immersion

\overset{\circ}{\cal Y}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal Y}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{\cal Y}

is a closed immersion.
Factorize this immersion into the following composite morphism:

\overset{\circ}{\cal Y}\longrightarrow({\cal Y}\times_{S}{\cal Y})^{\circ}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal Y}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{\cal Y}
.
Then, since

({\cal Y}\times_{S}{\cal Y})^{\circ}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal Y}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{\cal Y}

is affine, it is separated.
Hence the morphism

\overset{\circ}{\cal Y}\longrightarrow({\cal Y}\times_{S}{\cal Y})^{\circ}

is a closed immersion.
Because
T=(T\times_{S}{\cal Y})\times_{{\cal Y}\times_{S}{\cal Y}}{\cal Y}
,
the morphism
T\longrightarrow T\times_{S}{\cal Y}
is a closed immersion.
Consequently the immersion

U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T\times_{S}{\cal Y}
is closed,
and
{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}
is also affine.

We have the following sequence of projective systems
of coherent crystals of

\{\Gamma({\mathfrak{T}}_{n},{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
-modules:

\{{E}^{\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\longrightarrow\{{E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\longrightarrow\{{E}^{\prime\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\longrightarrow 0.

Because
{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}
is affine,
the sequence

E^{\prime}_{n}\longrightarrow E_{n}\longrightarrow E^{\prime\prime}_{n}\longrightarrow 0
for each
n

is exact. Set
F_{n}:={\rm Ker}(E_{n}\longrightarrow E^{\prime\prime}_{n})

and
G_{n}:={\rm Ker}(E^{\prime}_{n}\longrightarrow E_{n})
.
Since the sequences

(3.5.5;
n
)

0\longrightarrow F_{n}\longrightarrow E_{n}\longrightarrow E^{\prime\prime}_{n}\longrightarrow 0

and

(3.5.6;
n
)

0\longrightarrow G_{n}\longrightarrow E^{\prime}_{n}\longrightarrow F_{n}\longrightarrow 0

are exact,
the families
\{F_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
and

\{G_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
are coherent crystals of

\{\Gamma({\mathfrak{T}}_{n},{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
-modules by
(
3.3
).
For a coherent crystal
\{H_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
of

\{\Gamma({\mathfrak{T}}_{n},{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
-modules,

R^{1}\varprojlim_{n}H_{n}=0
by the log version of
[
O2
, (3.8)]

which follows from the proof of [loc. cit.].
Hence the sequences
\varprojlim_{n}
(
3.5.5;

n

) and

\varprojlim_{n}
(
3.5.6;

n

) are exact.
Thus we have proved the exactness of (
3.5.4
).

We finish the proof of (
3.5
).
∎

Let
i
and
m
be nonnegative integers.
Set

{\cal Y}(i):=\underset{(i+1)~{\rm pieces}}{\underbrace{{\cal Y}\times_{S}{\cal Y}\times_{S}\times\cdots\times_{S}{\cal Y}}}
.
Let
{\cal Y}_{m}(i)

be the
m
-th infinitesimal neighborhood of
the diagonal
{\cal Y}
in
{\cal Y}(i)
defined in

[
Ka
, (5.8)]
.

Let
p_{j}(i)\colon{\cal Y}_{m}(i)\longrightarrow{\cal Y}

(1\leq j\leq i+1)
be the
j
-th projection.
Let
p_{ij}\colon{\cal Y}_{m}(2)\longrightarrow{\cal Y}_{m}(1)
be
the
(i,j)
-th projection
(1\leq i<j\leq 3)
.

For an object
(U,T,\iota,u)
of
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
,
set
U_{j}(i):=U\times_{\iota_{Y}\circ u,{\cal Y},p_{j}(i)}{\cal Y}_{m}(i)
.
Then we have immersions

\Delta(i):=\Delta_{m}(i)\colon U\overset{\iota\times(\iota_{Y}\circ u)}{\longrightarrow}T\times_{S}{\cal Y}\overset{{\rm id}_{T}\times{\rm diag}.}{\longrightarrow}T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i)

and

\iota_{j}(i)\colon U_{j}(i)\overset{\iota\times{\rm id}_{{\cal Y}_{m}(i)}}{\longrightarrow}T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i).

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
{\cal E}:=\{{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be
a coherent crystal of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}
-modules.
Then there exists a family
\{\nabla^{i}\}_{i=0}^{\infty}

of natural morphisms

\nabla^{i}\colon L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Y}/S})\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal Y}/S})

which makes
\{(L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Y}/S}),\nabla^{i})\}_{i=0}^{\infty}

a complex of
{\cal K}_{Y/S}
-modules.

Proof.

(The following proof is the log version of that of
[
O2
, (5.4)]
.)
Let the notations be as in the proof of (
3.5
).
Let
u_{j}(i)\colon U_{j}(i)\longrightarrow Y

(i\in{\mathbb{N}})

be the composite morphism

U_{j}(i)\overset{\rm 1st~proj.}{\longrightarrow}U\overset{u}{\longrightarrow}Y
.
Then, for
1\leq j\leq i+1
,
the following two diagrams

\begin{CD}U@>{({\rm id}_{U},{\rm diag}.\circ\iota_{Y}\circ u)}>{}>U_{j}(i)\\
@V{\Delta(i)}V{}V@V{}V{\iota_{j}(i)}V\\
T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i)=T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i)\end{CD}

and

\begin{CD}U_{j}(i)@>{{\rm 1st~proj.}}>{}>U\\
@V{\iota_{j}(i)}V{}V@V{}V{\Delta(0)}V\\
T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i)@>{{\rm id}_{T}\times p_{j}(i)}>{}>T\times_{S}{\cal Y}\end{CD}

give us the following two morphisms

\Delta_{j}(i)\colon(U,T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i),\Delta(i),u)\longrightarrow(U_{j}(i),T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i),\iota_{j}(i),u_{j}(i))

and

p_{j}(i)\colon(U_{j}(i),T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i),\iota_{j}(i),u_{j}(i))\longrightarrow(U,T\times_{S}{\cal Y},\Delta(0),u)

of prewidenings of
Y/S
, respectively.
Hence we have the following natural morphisms of
log formal schemes for
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
:

\Delta_{j,n}(i)\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i))\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{U_{j}(i),n}(T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i)),

p_{j,n}(i)\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{U_{j}(i),n}(T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i))\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T\times_{S}{\cal Y})={\mathfrak{T}}_{n}.

Set
\delta_{j}(i):=({\rm id}_{U},{\rm diag}.\circ\iota_{Y}\circ u)\colon U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}U_{j}(i)
.
Let
{\cal I}_{U}
and
{\cal I}_{U_{j}(i)}
be
the defining ideal sheaves of the immersions

\Delta(i)\colon U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i)
and

\iota_{j}(i)\colon U_{j}(i)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i)
, respectively.
We have the following commutative diagram:

(3.6.1)

\begin{CD}U@>{\subset}>{}>T\times_{S}U@>{\rm proj.}>{}>U@>{\iota_{Y}\circ u}>{}>{\cal Y}\\
@V{\bigcap}V{\delta_{j}(i)}V@V{\bigcap}V{}V@V{\bigcap}V{}V@V{}V{{\rm diag}.}V\\
U_{j}(i)@>{\subset}>{}>T\times_{S}U_{j}(i)@>{\rm proj.}>{}>U_{j}(i)@>{\rm proj.}>{}>{\cal Y}_{m}(i),\end{CD}

where each square in this diagram is cartesian.
Let
{\cal J}
be the defining ideal sheaf of
the diagonal immersion
{\cal Y}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}_{m}(i)
.
Set
{\cal J}(T):={\cal J}{\cal O}_{T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i)}
.
Because each square in (
3.6.1
) is cartesian,

{\cal J}(T){\cal O}_{U_{j}(i)}
is the defining ideal sheaf of
the immersion
U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}U_{j}(i)
.
Hence
{\cal I}_{U}={\cal I}_{U_{j}(i)}+{\cal J}(T)
.
Since
{\cal J}(T)^{m+1}=0
,

{\cal I}_{U}^{n+m}\subset{\cal I}_{U_{j}(i)}^{n}
.
Therefore we have
the following natural morphism of log formal schemes

(3.6.2)

h_{j,n+m,n}(i)\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{U_{j}(i),n}(T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i))\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n+m}(T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i))\quad(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})

as in the proof of
[
O2
, (5.4)]
using [loc. cit., (2.5)].
Then
\Delta_{j,n+m}(i)\circ h_{j,n+m,n}(i)

(resp.
h_{j,n+m,n}(i)\circ\Delta_{j,n}(i)
)
is the transition morphism

{\mathfrak{T}}_{U_{j}(i),n}(T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i))\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{U_{j}(i),n+m}(T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i))

(resp.
{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i))\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n+m}(T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i))
).
As stated in [loc. cit., (2.5)],
we have the following isomorphism of inductive objects:

(3.6.3)

\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i))\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U_{j}(i),n}(T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i))\}_{n=1}^{\infty}.

We identify

\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U_{j}(i),n}(T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i))\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

with

\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(i))\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

by this isomorphism.
For a morphism

\alpha\colon T^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})

of quasi-prewidenings of
Y/S
,
let
\alpha_{n}\colon T^{\prime}_{n}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})
be
the induced morphism by
\alpha
.
Set
{\cal E}_{T^{\prime}_{n}}:=\alpha^{*}_{n}({\cal E}_{n})
.
Let
{\cal I}_{1}
be the defining ideal sheaf of
the immersion

{\cal Y}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}_{1}(1)
.
Then there exists a functorial isomorphism

(3.6.4)

{\cal I}_{1}\simeq\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Y}/S}

(
[
Ka
, (5.8.1)]
,
[
Sh1
, Proposition 3.2.5]
).
By using this fact,
we see that
{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}_{m}(1)}
is
a locally free
{\cal O}_{\cal Y}
-module of finite rank.
By (
2.9
)
we have the following isomorphism

(3.6.5)

\displaystyle\epsilon^{m}\colon

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}_{m}(1)}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\varprojlim_{n}{\cal E}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}}=\varprojlim_{n}p_{2,n}(1)^{*}{\cal E}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}}=\varprojlim_{n}{\cal E}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U_{2}(1),n}(T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(1))}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longleftarrow}\varprojlim_{n}{\cal E}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(1))}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\varprojlim_{n}{\cal E}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U_{1}(1),n}(T\times_{S}{\cal Y}_{m}(1))}=\varprojlim_{n}p_{1,n}(1)^{*}{\cal E}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}}

\displaystyle=(\varprojlim_{n}{\cal E}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}_{m}(1)}.

Set
{\mathfrak{E}}:=\varprojlim_{n}({\cal E}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}})
and

\nabla^{0}(x):=\epsilon^{1}(1\otimes x)-x\otimes 1

for a local section
x
of
{\mathfrak{E}}
.
The morphism
\epsilon^{1}~{\rm mod}~{\cal I}_{1}

is the identity.
Thus
\nabla^{0}
induces the following morphism

(3.6.6)

\nabla^{0}\colon{\mathfrak{E}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Y}/S}.

By (
3.5.2
) we obtain the following equality:

(3.6.7)

L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E})_{T}=\varprojlim_{n}p^{\prime}_{n*}({\cal E}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}}).

Hence we have the following morphism

(3.6.8)

\displaystyle\nabla^{0}\colon L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E})_{T}=\varprojlim_{n}p^{\prime}_{n*}({\cal E}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}p^{\prime}_{n*}({\cal E}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Y}/S}

\displaystyle=L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Y}/S})_{T}.

The morphism
\nabla^{0}
induces
\nabla^{i}

(i\in{\mathbb{N}})
.

The rest is to prove that

\nabla^{i+1}\circ\nabla^{i}=0

(i\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
As in
[
B1
, II Théorème 4.2.11]

(cf.
[
BO
, Proposition 2.11, Construction 2.14]
),
we can check
\nabla^{1}\circ\nabla^{0}=0

by the usual cocycle condition of
\{\epsilon^{m}\}_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}
.
Hence
\nabla^{i+1}\circ\nabla^{i}=0

(\forall i\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
∎

The following is a relative version of

[
O2
, (5.4)]
and
[
Sh2
, Theorem 2.3.5 (2)]
,
though the formulation of it is rather different from them:

Theorem 3.7

(
Log convergent Poincaré lemma).

Let
E
be a coherent crystal of
{\cal K}_{Y/S}
-modules.
Set
{\cal E}_{n}:=E_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})}
and

{\cal E}:=\{{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
.
Then the natural morphism

(3.7.1)

E\longrightarrow L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}/S})

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

Set
{\mathfrak{T}}:={\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})=(Y,{\cal Y},\iota,{\rm id}_{Y})
and

{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}:={\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0})
.
Following
[
O2
, p. 152]
and

[
Sh2
, p. 95]
, set

\Omega^{i}_{{\mathfrak{T}}/S}(E):=j_{{\mathfrak{T}}*}(j^{*}_{\mathfrak{T}}(E)\otimes_{{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{\mathfrak{T}}}\varphi^{*}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}}({\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Y}/S}))\quad(i\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Let
T
be an enlargement of
Y/S
with a morphism

T\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}
of prewidenings.
Then, by (
2.2.2
),
the morphism
T\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}
factors through

T\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}
for some
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Hence
(\varphi_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}}^{*}({\cal E}))_{T}={\cal K}_{T}\otimes_{{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}}}E_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}}=E_{T}
.
On the other hand,

j_{\mathfrak{T}}^{*}(E)_{(T\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}})}=E_{T}
.
Thus

\displaystyle\Omega^{i}_{{\mathfrak{T}}/S}(E)

\displaystyle=j_{{\mathfrak{T}}*}(\varphi^{*}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}}({\cal E})\otimes_{{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}}}\varphi^{*}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}}({\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Y}/S}))

\displaystyle=j_{{\mathfrak{T}}*}\varphi^{*}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Y}/S})=L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Y}/S}).

Moreover, by the proof of

[
O2
, (5.4)]
and
[
Sh2
, Theorem 2.3.5 (2)]

and (
3.6
),

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{T}}/S}(E)=L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}/S})

as complexes.
Hence, by the proof of

[
O2
, (5.4)]
and
[
Sh2
, Theorem 2.3.5 (3)]
,

E=L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}/S})

in
D^{+}({\cal K}_{Y/S})
.
∎

The following has been essentially proved
in
[
O2
, (6.6)]
and
[
Sh2
, Corollary 2.3.8]
:

Proposition 3.8
.

Let the notations be as in
(
3.7
)
.
Set
Y_{n}:=Y\times_{\cal Y}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})=Y\times_{\widehat{\cal Y}}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}(\widehat{\cal Y})
,
where
\widehat{\cal Y}
is
the formal completion of
{\cal Y}
along
Y
.
Let

\beta_{n}\colon Y_{n}\longrightarrow Y
be
the projection and identify

{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})_{\rm zar}
with

Y_{n,{\rm zar}}
.
Then

(3.8.1)

Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(E)=\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n*}({\cal E}_{n}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}/S})

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))
.

Proof.

(The proof is the same as that of
[
Sh2
, Corollary 2.3.4]

which is the log version of
[
O2
, Corollary 4.4]
.)
By (
3.7.1
) we have the following isomorphism

(3.8.2)

Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(E)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}/S})).

By the obvious relative version of

[
Sh2
, Corollary 2.3.4]
which is the log version of

[
O2
, Corollary 4.4]
,
for a coherent crystal
F
in

(Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{\mathfrak{T}}
,

j_{{\mathfrak{T}}*}(F)
is
u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}
-acyclic.
Hence the right hand side of (
3.8.2
)
is equal to

u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal E}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}/S})
.

By the relative version of
the commutativity of the diagram of topoi
in
[
Sh2
, p. 91]
which is a log version of that
of topoi in
[
O2
, p. 147]
, we have

(\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n*}){\varphi}_{\overset{\to}{{\mathfrak{T}}}*}=u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}j_{{\mathfrak{T}}*}
.
Hence, for a
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{{\mathfrak{T}}}}
-module

{\cal F}=\{{\cal F}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
, we have the following:

u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}({\cal F})=u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}j_{{\mathfrak{T}}*}{\varphi}^{*}_{\overset{\to}{{\mathfrak{T}}}}({\cal F})=(\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n*}){\varphi}_{\overset{\to}{{\mathfrak{T}}}*}{\varphi}^{*}_{\overset{\to}{{\mathfrak{T}}}}({\cal F})=\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n*}({\cal F}_{n}).

We complete the proof of (
3.8
).
∎

4
Log convergent linearization functors. II

In this section we prove that
log convergent linearization functors are
compatible with closed immersions of log schemes.
This is the log convergent version of the compatibility of
log crystalline linearization functors with closed immersions of log schemes
in
[
NS1
, (2.2)]
.

Let
{\cal V}
,
\pi
,
S
and
S_{1}

be as in §
2
.

The following is the log convergent version of

[
NS1
, (2.2.6)]
whose proof is
much simpler than that of [loc. cit.]:

Lemma 4.1

(
cf.
[
NS1
, (2.2.6)]
).

Let
\iota_{Y}\colon Y_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}Y_{2}
be
a closed immersion of
fine log schemes over
S_{1}

whose underlying schemes are of finite type
over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}
.
Let
T_{j}:=(U_{j},T_{j},\iota_{j},u_{j})

(j=1,2)
be a
(
pre
)
widening of
Y_{j}/S
.
Let
{\iota}_{U}\colon U_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}U_{2}
and

\iota_{T}\colon T_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T_{2}

be closed immersions
of fine log formal schemes over
S

whose underlying formal schemes
are noetherian formal schemes
which are topologically of finite type over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Assume that
{\iota}_{U}
and
\iota_{T}

induce a morphism

(\iota_{U},\iota_{T})\colon(U_{1},T_{1},\iota_{1},u_{1})\longrightarrow(U_{2},T_{2},\iota_{2},u_{2})

of
(
pre
)
widenings of
Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
.
Let

\iota^{\rm loc}_{\rm conv}\colon(Y_{1}/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T_{1}}\longrightarrow(Y_{2}/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T_{2}}

be the induced morphism of topoi by
\iota_{Y}
and

(\iota_{U},\iota_{T})
.
Let
(U,T,\iota,u)
be a
(
pre
)
widening of
Y_{2}/S

over
(U_{2},T_{2},\iota_{2},u_{2})
.
Then
\iota^{{\rm loc}*}_{\rm conv}((U,T,\iota,u))=(U\times_{U_{2}}U_{1},T\times_{T_{2}}T_{1},\iota\times_{\iota_{2}}\iota_{1},u\times_{u_{2}}u_{1})

as sheaves.

Proof.

We prove (
4.1
) for the case of widenings.
It is evident that the closed immersion

U\times_{U_{2}}U_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T\times_{T_{2}}T_{1}

is defined by an ideal sheaf of definition of

T\times_{T_{2}}T_{1}
.
Hence

(U\times_{U_{2}}U_{1},T\times_{T_{2}}T_{1},\iota\times_{\iota_{2}}\iota_{1},u\times_{u_{2}}u_{1})

is a widening of
Y_{1}/S
.
It is straightforward to see that

\iota^{{\rm loc}*}_{\rm conv}((U,T,\iota,u))=(U\times_{U_{2}}U_{1},T\times_{T_{2}}T_{1},\iota\times_{\iota_{2}}\iota_{1},u\times_{u_{2}}u_{1})
.
∎

Let
\iota_{Y,Z}\colon Z\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}Y

be a closed immersion of fine log
schemes over
S_{1}
.
Assume that there exists the following cartesian diagram

(4.1.1)

\begin{CD}Z@>{\iota_{\cal Z}}>{}>{\cal Z}\\
@V{\iota_{Y,Z}}V{}V@V{}V{\iota_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z}}}V\\
Y@>{\iota_{\cal Y}}>{}>{\cal Y},\end{CD}

where
\iota_{\cal Z}
and
\iota_{\cal Y}

are closed immersions into
fine log
p
-adic formal schemes over
S
and

\iota_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z}}
is a closed immersion of
fine log
p
-adic formal schemes over
S
.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{\cal Y}
and
\overset{\circ}{\cal Z}

are noetherian formal schemes which
are topologically of finite type over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Then we have the natural following immersion
by the universality of the exactification:

(4.1.2)

\iota_{{\cal Y}^{\rm ex},{\cal Z}^{\rm ex}}\colon{\cal Z}^{\rm ex}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}^{\rm ex}.

Set
{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z}):=(Z,{\cal Z},\iota_{\cal Z},{\rm id}_{Z})

and

{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y}):=(Y,{\cal Y},\iota_{\cal Y},{\rm id}_{Y})
.
Let

\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

and

\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

be the systems of the universal enlargements
of
\iota_{\cal Z}
and
\iota_{\cal Y}

with natural morphisms

g_{{\cal Y},n}\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\longrightarrow{\cal Y}

and

g_{{\cal Z},n}\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})\longrightarrow{\cal Z}
,
respectively.
Let

g_{{\cal Y}^{\rm ex},n}\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\longrightarrow{\cal Y}^{\rm ex}

and

g_{{\cal Z}^{\rm ex},n}\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})\longrightarrow{\cal Z}^{\rm ex}

be also the natural morphisms.
Because the diagram (
4.1.1
) is cartesian,
we have the following equality by (
2.9
):

(4.1.3)

{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})={\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\times_{\widetilde{\cal Y}}\widetilde{\cal Z}.

By the universality of the exactification,
we see that the following equality holds
:

(4.1.4)

{\cal Y}^{\rm ex}\times_{\cal Y}{\cal Z}={\cal Z}^{\rm ex}.

By using this equality and (
2.9
),
we obtain the following equality:

(4.1.5)

{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})={\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\times_{\widetilde{\cal Y}{}^{\rm ex}}\widetilde{\cal Z}{}^{\rm ex}.

Set
Z_{n}:=Z\times_{\cal Z}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})
and

Y_{n}:=Y\times_{\cal Y}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})
.
Let
\iota_{{\cal Z},n}

and
\iota_{{\cal Y},n}

be the natural closed immersions

Z_{n}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})

and

Y_{n}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})
, respectively.
Let
\iota_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z},n}\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})
be also the natural closed immersion.
Let
u_{Z,n}
and
u_{Y,n}
be the first projections

Z_{n}\longrightarrow Z
and
Y_{n}\longrightarrow Y
.
Let
\star
be nothing or
{\rm ex}
.
Let

g^{*}_{{\cal Z}^{\star}}\colon\{{\cal K}_{{\cal Z}^{\star}}\text{-modules}\}\longrightarrow\{{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z)}}\text{-modules}\}

and

g^{*}_{{\cal Y}^{\star}}\colon\{{\cal K}_{{\cal Y}^{\star}}\text{-modules}\}\longrightarrow\{{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y)}}\text{-modules}\}

be the natural pull-backs obtained by

\{g_{{\cal Z}^{\star},n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
and

\{g_{{\cal Y}^{\star},n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
, respectively.
Let

\overset{\to}{\iota}{}_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z}*}\colon\{{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}\text{-modules}\}\longrightarrow\{{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}\text{-modules}\}

and

{\iota}_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z},n*}\colon\{{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})}\text{-modules}\}\longrightarrow\{{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})}\text{-modules}\}\quad(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})

be the natural direct images.

Lemma 4.2

(
cf.
[
NS1
, (2.2.9)]
).

(1)

Let
{\cal E}
be a quasi-coherent
{\cal O}_{{\cal Z}^{\star}}
-module.
For the quasi-coherent
{\cal K}_{{\cal Z}^{\star}}
-module

{\cal E}\otimes_{\cal V}K
,
the following diagram

(4.2.1)

\begin{CD}\{{\cal K}_{{\cal Z}^{\star}}\text{-modules}\}@>{g^{*}_{{\cal Z}^{\star}}}>{}>\{{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}\text{-modules}\}\\
@V{\iota_{{\cal Y}^{\star},{\cal Z}^{\star}*}}V{}V@V{}V{\overset{\to}{\iota}{}_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z}*}}V\\
\{{\cal K}_{{\cal Y}^{\star}}\text{-modules}\}@>{g^{*}_{{\cal Y}^{\star}}}>{}>\{{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}\text{-modules}\}\end{CD}

is commutative, that is, the natural morphism

g_{{\cal Y}^{\star}}^{*}\iota_{{\cal Y}^{\star},{\cal Z}^{\star}*}({\cal E}\otimes_{\cal V}K){\longrightarrow}\overset{\to}{\iota}_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z}*}g^{*}_{{\cal Z}^{\star}}({\cal E}\otimes_{\cal V}K)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

(The proof is the same as that of
[
NS1
, (2.2.9)]
.)
Since the morphisms

{\cal Z}\longrightarrow{\cal Y}
and

{\cal Z}^{\rm ex}\longrightarrow{\cal Y}^{\rm ex}

are affine, (
4.2
) immediately follows
from (
4.1.3
), (
4.1.5
) and
the affine base change theorem (
[
EGA II
, (1.5.2)]
).
∎

Lemma 4.3

(
cf.
[
NS1
, (2.2.10)]
).

Let

\iota^{\rm loc}_{\rm conv}\colon(Z/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})}\longrightarrow(Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})}

be the natural morphism of topoi.
Then the following diagram

(4.3.1)

\begin{CD}\{\text{coherent crystals of }{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}\text{-modules}\}@>{\varphi_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}^{*}}>{}>\\
@V{\overset{\to}{\iota}{}_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z}*}}V{}V\\
\{\text{coherent crystals of }{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}\text{-modules}\}@>{\varphi_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}^{*}}>{}>\end{CD}

\begin{CD}\{\text{coherent crystals of }{\cal K}_{Z/S}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}\text{-modules}\}\\
@V{}V{\iota^{\rm loc}_{{\rm conv}*}}V\\
\{\text{coherent crystals of }{\cal K}_{Y/S}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}\text{-modules}\}\end{CD}

is commutative.

Proof.

Let
{\cal E}=\{{\cal E}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

be a coherent crystal of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}
-modules.
Let
T=(U,T,\iota,u)
be an enlargement of
Y/S

with a morphism

h_{{\cal Y},n}\colon T\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})

of enlargements of
Y/S
.
Let
\iota^{\prime}\colon U\times_{Y_{n}}{Z_{n}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T\times_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})

and
u^{\prime}\colon U\times_{Y_{n}}{Z_{n}}\longrightarrow Z

be the natural morphisms.
By abuse of notation,
we denote the enlargement

(U\times_{Y_{n}}{Z_{n}},T\times_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z}),\iota^{\prime},u^{\prime})

simply by

T\times_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})
.
Then we have the natural morphism

h_{{\cal Z},n}\colon T\times_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})
.
By (
4.1
) we have the following:

(4.3.2)

\displaystyle\iota_{{\rm conv}*}^{\rm loc}\varphi_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})}^{*}({\cal E}_{n})(T)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})}^{*}({\cal E}_{n})(T\times_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z}))

\displaystyle=\Gamma(T\times_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z}),h^{*}_{{\cal Z},n}({\cal E}_{n})).

On the other hand,

(4.3.3)

\varphi_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})}^{*}{\iota_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z},n*}}({\cal E}_{n})(T)=\Gamma(T,h^{*}_{{\cal Y},n}{\iota_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z},n*}}({\cal E}_{n})).

Since the morphism

\iota_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z},n}\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})

is a closed immersion, in
particular, an affine morphism,
the affine base change theorem tells us
that the right hand sides of
(
4.3.2
) and (
4.3.3
) are the same.
This completes the proof of (
4.3
).
∎

Lemma 4.4

(
cf.
[
NS1
, (2.2.11)]
).

Assume that
\widetilde{\cal Y}={\cal Y}
and
\widetilde{\cal Z}={\cal Z}
.
Then the following diagram of topoi

(4.4.1)

\begin{CD}(Z/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}@>{j_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}}>{}>(Z/S)_{\rm conv}\\
@V{\iota_{{\rm conv}}^{\rm loc}}V{}V@V{}V{\iota_{\rm conv}}V\\
(Y/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}@>{j_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}}>{}>(Y/S)_{\rm conv}\\
\end{CD}

is commutative.

Proof.

Let
(U,T,\iota,u)
be an object of
{\rm Conv}({Y/S})
.
Let
\widehat{T}
be the formal completion of
T

along the closed log subscheme

U\times_{Y}Z
of
U

(we endow
\widehat{T}
with
the pull-back of the log structure of
T
).
Let
\hat{\iota}
be the closed immersion

U\times_{Y}Z\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\widehat{T}
and
let
u_{Z}\colon U\times_{Y}Z\longrightarrow Z
be the second projection.
We claim that

(4.4.2)

\displaystyle\iota^{*}_{\rm conv}((U,T,\iota,u))=(U\times_{Y}Z,\widehat{T},\hat{\iota},u_{Z})

as a sheaf in
({Z/S})_{\rm conv}
.
Indeed, let
(U^{\prime},T^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},u^{\prime})

be an object of
{\rm Conv}({Z/S})

fitting into the following commutative diagram over
S
:

(4.4.3)

\begin{CD}Z@<{u^{\prime}}<{}<U^{\prime}@>{\iota^{\prime}}>{}>T^{\prime}\\
@V{\iota_{Y,Z}}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{g}V\\
Y@<{u}<{}<U@>{\iota}>{}>T.\end{CD}

Obviously we have the following commutative
diagram

(4.4.4)

\begin{CD}U^{\prime}@>{\iota^{\prime}}>{}>T^{\prime}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{g}V\\
U\times_{Y}Z@>{\subset}>{}>T.\end{CD}

Let
{\cal J}
(resp.
{\cal J}^{\prime}
) be
the ideal sheaf of
the lower horizontal closed immersion (resp.
\iota^{\prime}
) in (
4.4.4
).
Then the commutative diagram (
4.4.4
) tells us that
the natural morphism

g^{*}({\cal O}_{T})\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{T^{\prime}}
induces a morphism

g^{*}({\cal J})\longrightarrow{\cal J}^{\prime}
.
Since
{\cal O}_{T^{\prime}}=\varprojlim_{n}{\cal O}_{T^{\prime}}/{\cal J}^{\prime}{}^{n}
,
we have a natural morphism

g^{*}({\cal O}_{\widehat{T}})\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{T^{\prime}}
and
hence a morphism

T^{\prime}\longrightarrow\widehat{T}
of log formal schemes.
Now it is easy to check that our claim is right.

Let
(T\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y}))^{\widehat{}}

and
(\widehat{T}\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z}))^{\widehat{}}

be the formal completions of
T\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})

and
\widehat{T}\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})

along
U\times_{Y}Y_{n}
and
U\times_{Y}Z_{n}=(U\times_{Y}Z)\times_{Z}Z_{n}
,
respectively.
Then, by (
2.9.1
) and (
4.4.2
), we obtain the following equalities:

(4.4.5)

\displaystyle j_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}^{*}\iota^{*}_{\rm conv}((U,T,\iota,u))

\displaystyle=(U\times_{Y}Z,\widehat{T},\hat{\iota},u_{Z})\times\varinjlim_{n}(Z_{n},{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z}),\iota_{{\cal Z},n},u_{Z,n})

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{n}(U\times_{Y}Z_{n},(\widehat{T}\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z}))^{\widehat{}}),

where we omit to write
the closed immersion

U\times_{Y}Z_{n}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(\widehat{T}\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z}))^{\widehat{}}
and
the natural morphism
U\times_{Y}Z_{n}\longrightarrow Z
in the last object in (
4.4.5
).
Let
(T\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z}))^{\widehat{}}

be the formal completion of

T\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})

along
U\times_{Y}Z_{n}
.
There exists a natural morphism

(\widehat{T}\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z}))^{\widehat{}}\longrightarrow(T\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z}))^{\widehat{}}
.
On the other hand, there exists a natural morphism

(T\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z}))^{\widehat{}}\longrightarrow\widehat{T}
, which induces a natural morphism

(T\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z}))^{\widehat{}}\longrightarrow(\widehat{T}\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z}))^{\widehat{}}
.
We can easily see that the two morphisms
are inverse each other.
Hence

(4.4.6)

j_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}^{*}\iota^{*}_{\rm conv}((U,T,\iota,u))=\varinjlim_{n}(U\times_{Y}Z_{n},(T\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z}))^{\widehat{}})

by (
4.4.5
).
On the other hand, by (
4.1
),

(4.4.7)

\displaystyle\iota_{\rm conv}^{{\rm loc}*}j_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}^{*}((U,T,\iota,u))

\displaystyle=\iota_{\rm conv}^{{\rm loc}*}[(U,T,\iota,u)\times\varinjlim_{n}(Y_{n},{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y}),\iota_{{\cal Y},n},u_{Y,n})]

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{n}(U\times_{Y}Y_{n}\times_{Y}Z,(T\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y}))^{\widehat{}}\times_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})).

By (
4.1.3
) and the assumption,
we have the following equalities:

\displaystyle Z_{n}

\displaystyle=Z\times_{\cal Z}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z})=Z\times_{\cal Z}\widetilde{\cal Z}\times_{\widetilde{\cal Y}}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})=Z\times_{\cal Z}{\cal Z}\times_{\cal Y}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})

\displaystyle=Z\times_{Y}Y\times_{\cal Y}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})=Z\times_{Y}Y_{n}.

Hence
U\times_{Y}Z_{n}=U\times_{Y}Y_{n}\times_{Y}Z
,
and

(4.4.8)

\displaystyle(T\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z}))^{\widehat{}}

\displaystyle=(T\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})\times_{{\cal Y}}{\cal Z})^{\widehat{}}

\displaystyle=(T\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y}))^{\widehat{}}\times_{\cal Y}{\cal Z}

\displaystyle=(T\times_{S}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y}))^{\widehat{}}\times_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y,n}({\cal Y})}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z,n}({\cal Z}).

By (
4.4.6
), (
4.4.7
) and (
4.4.8
),
we have

j_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}^{*}\iota^{*}_{\rm conv}=\iota_{\rm conv}^{{\rm loc}*}j_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{Y}({\cal Y})}^{*}.

Therefore the diagram (
4.4.1
) is commutative.
∎

Corollary 4.5

(
cf.
[
NS1
, (2.2.12)]
).

Assume that
\widetilde{\cal Y}={\cal Y}
and
\widetilde{\cal Z}={\cal Z}
.
Then there exists a canonical
isomorphism of functors

(4.5.1)

L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}\circ\overset{\to}{\iota}_{{\cal Y},{\cal Z}*}\longrightarrow\iota_{{\rm conv}*}\circ L^{\rm UE}_{Z/S}

for coherent crystals of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{Z}({\cal Z})}
-modules.
Set
L^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}:=L^{\rm UE}_{Y/S}\circ\{g^{*}_{{\cal Y}^{\star},n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

and
L^{\rm conv}_{Z/S}:=L^{\rm UE}_{Z/S}\circ\{g^{*}_{{\cal Z}^{\star},n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
.
Then there also exists the following canonical
isomorphism of functors

(4.5.2)

L^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}\circ\iota_{{\cal Y}^{\star},{\cal Z}^{\star}*}\longrightarrow\iota_{{\rm conv}*}\circ L^{\rm conv}_{Z/S}

for the coherent
{\cal K}_{{\cal Z}^{*}}
-modules.

Proof.

The former statement of
(
4.5
) immediately follows from
(
4.3
) and (
4.4
).
The latter follows from the former and (
4.2
).
∎

5
Vanishing cycle sheaves in log convergent topoi

In this section we study several properties of
the morphisms forgetting the log structures
of log convergent topoi.
See
[
NS1
, (2.3)]
for the analogues
in the log crystalline case.

Let the notations be as in §
2
.
Let
M
be the log structure of
Y
.
Let
N
be a fine sub log structure of
M

on
\overset{\circ}{Y}
.
Set
Y_{M}:=(\overset{\circ}{Y},M)(=Y)

and
Y_{N}:=(\overset{\circ}{Y},N)
.
The inclusion
N\subset M
induces
a natural morphism

(5.0.1)

\epsilon_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S}\colon Y_{M}\longrightarrow Y_{N}

of log schemes over
S_{1}
.
The morphism
\epsilon_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S}

induces the following morphism of
log convergent topoi:

(5.0.2)

\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S}\colon(Y_{M}/S)_{\rm conv}\longrightarrow(Y_{N}/S)_{\rm conv}.

For simplicity of notations, we denote

\epsilon_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S}
and
\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S}

simply by
\epsilon
and
\epsilon_{\rm conv}
for the time being.
Let

(U_{M},T_{M},\iota_{M},u_{M}):=((\overset{\circ}{U},M_{U}),(\overset{\circ}{T},M_{T}),\iota_{M},u_{M})

be an object of the log convergent site

{\rm Conv}(Y_{M}/S)
.
Set
N_{U}:=\overset{\circ}{u}{}^{*}_{M}(N)

and
U_{N}:=(\overset{\circ}{U},N_{U})
.
Then we easily see that
N_{U}\subset M_{U}
since

u_{M}
is strict.
We have the natural morphism

u_{N}\colon U_{N}\longrightarrow Y_{N}

of log schemes over
S_{1}
.

Definition 5.1
.

(1) We call the morphism

\epsilon_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S}
in
(
5.0.1
)
the
morphism of log schemes over

S_{1}

forgetting the structure

M\setminus N
,
and call the morphism

\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S}
in
(
5.0.2
) the
morphism of
log convergent topoi forgetting the structure

M\setminus N
.
When
N
is trivial,
we call
\epsilon_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S}
and

\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S}
the

morphism forgetting the log structure
of
Y/S
.

(2) (See
[
NS1
, (2.1)]
for the definition
of the fs(=fine and saturated) log structure
M(D)
of
a relative SNCD
D
on a smooth scheme
X
over
S_{1}
.)
When
\overset{\circ}{Y}
is a smooth scheme
X
over
S_{1}
,

M=M({D\cup Z})
and
N=M(Z)
,
where
D
and
Z
are transversal relative
SNCD’s on
X/S_{1}
, we call

\epsilon_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S}

and
\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S}

the
morphism of ringed topoi
forgetting the log structure along

D
and denote them by

\epsilon_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S}
and

\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S}
, respectively.

The morphism
\epsilon_{\rm conv}=\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S}
also induces a morphism

(5.1.1)

\epsilon_{\rm conv}\colon((Y_{M}/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{M}/S})\longrightarrow((Y_{N}/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{N}/S})

of ringed topoi.
Let
u^{\rm conv}_{Y_{L}/S}\colon((Y_{L}/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{L}/S})\longrightarrow(Y_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))

(L:=M,N)

be the projection in (
2.0.3
).
Then we have the following equality:

(5.1.2)

u^{\rm conv}_{Y_{N}/S}\circ\epsilon_{\rm conv}=u^{\rm conv}_{Y_{M}/S}.

As in
[
NS1
, (2.3.0.3)]
,
we assume that, locally on
\overset{\circ}{Y}
,
there exists a finitely generated commutative monoid
P

with unit element such that
P^{\rm gp}
has no
p
-torsion
and that there exists a chart
P\longrightarrow N
.

Next we define the localizations of

\epsilon_{\rm conv}
’s in (
5.0.2
) and
(
5.1.1
).
Let

T_{M}=(U_{M},T_{M},\iota_{M},u_{M})=((\overset{\circ}{U},M_{U}),(\overset{\circ}{T},M_{T}),\iota_{M},u_{M})

be an exact widening of
Y_{M}/S
.
Then we have an isomorphism

M_{T}/{\cal O}_{T}^{*}\overset{\overset{\iota^{*}_{M}}{\sim}}{\longrightarrow}M_{U}/{\cal O}_{U}^{*}

on
U_{\rm zar}=T_{\rm zar}
.
Set
N_{U}:=\overset{\circ}{u}{}^{*}_{M}(N)

and
U_{N}:=(\overset{\circ}{U},N_{U})
.
Let
N_{T}
be the inverse image of

N_{U}/{\cal O}_{U}^{*}
by the morphism

M_{T}\longrightarrow M_{U}/{\cal O}_{U}^{*}
.
Then, as in
[
NS1
, (2.3.1)]
,

N_{T}
is shown to be fine.
Set
T_{N}:=(\overset{\circ}{T},N_{T})
.
Let
u_{N}\colon U_{N}\longrightarrow Y_{N}

(resp.
\iota_{N}\colon U_{N}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T_{N}
)
be the induced morphism
by
\overset{\circ}{u}_{M}
(resp.
\overset{\circ}{\iota}_{M}
).
Set
T_{N}:=(U_{N},T_{N},\iota_{N},u_{N})
.
Then we have a morphism

(5.1.3)

\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T}\colon(Y_{M}/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T_{M}}\longrightarrow(Y_{N}/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T_{N}}

of topoi and a morphism

(5.1.4)

\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T}\colon((Y_{M}/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T_{M}},{\cal K}_{Y_{M}/S}|_{T_{M}})\longrightarrow((Y_{N}/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T_{N}},{\cal K}_{Y_{N}/S}|_{T_{N}})

of ringed topoi.

Lemma 5.2

(
cf.
[
NS1
, (2.3.3)]
).

Let the notations be as above.
Then the functor

\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T*}
is exact.

Proof.

Let
\phi_{N}\colon(U^{\prime}_{N},T^{\prime}_{N},\iota^{\prime}_{N},u^{\prime}_{N})\longrightarrow T_{N}=(U_{N},T_{N},\iota_{N},u_{N})

(
U^{\prime}_{N}=(\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime},N_{U^{\prime}})
,

T^{\prime}_{N}=(\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime},N_{T^{\prime}})
)
be a morphism in
(Y_{N}/S)_{\rm conv}
.
Let
\phi_{U}\colon\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{U}
and

\phi\colon\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}

be the underlying morphisms of
(formal) schemes obtained by
\phi_{N}
.
Set
M_{U^{\prime}}:=\phi_{U}^{*}(M_{U})
,
M_{T^{\prime}}:=\phi^{*}(M_{T})
,
U^{\prime}_{M}:=(\overset{\circ}{U}{}^{\prime},M_{U^{\prime}})
and

T^{\prime}_{M}:=(\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime},M_{T^{\prime}})
.
Let
\iota^{\prime}_{M}\colon{U}^{\prime}_{M}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T^{\prime}_{M}

and
u^{\prime}_{M}\colon U^{\prime}_{M}\longrightarrow Y_{M}

be the natural morphisms.
Let

\phi_{M}\colon(U^{\prime}_{M},T^{\prime}_{M},\iota^{\prime}_{M},u^{\prime}_{M})\longrightarrow(U_{M},T_{M},\iota_{M},u_{M})

be the natural morphism.
Then
(U^{\prime}_{M},T^{\prime}_{M},\iota^{\prime}_{M},u^{\prime}_{M};\phi_{M})

is an object of
(Y_{M}/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T_{M}}
.
Let
T_{M}\times_{T_{N}}T^{\prime}_{N}

be the fiber product of

T_{M}
and
T^{\prime}_{N}
over
T_{N}
in
the category of fine log formal schemes.
By the same proof as that of
[
NS1
, (2.3.3)]
, we have

(5.2.1)

T_{M}\times_{T_{N}}T^{\prime}_{N}=T^{\prime}_{M}.

We also have

(5.2.2)

U_{M}\times_{U_{N}}U^{\prime}_{N}=U^{\prime}_{M}.

By the formula (
5.2.1
) and
(
5.2.2
),
(\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T})^{*}(U^{\prime}_{N},T^{\prime}_{N},\iota_{N}^{\prime},u_{N}^{\prime};\phi_{N})

is represented by

(U^{\prime}_{M},T^{\prime}_{M},\iota^{\prime}_{M},u^{\prime}_{M};\phi_{M})
.
Therefore,
for an object
E
in

(Y_{M}/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T_{M}}
, we have

(5.2.3)

\displaystyle\Gamma((U^{\prime}_{N},T^{\prime}_{N},\iota^{\prime}_{N},u^{\prime}_{N};\phi_{N}),(\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T})_{*}(E))

\displaystyle={\rm Hom}_{(Y_{M}/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T_{M}}}((\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T})^{*}(U^{\prime}_{N},T^{\prime}_{N},\iota^{\prime}_{N},u^{\prime}_{N};\phi_{N}),E)

\displaystyle=E(U^{\prime}_{M},T^{\prime}_{M},\iota^{\prime}_{M},u^{\prime}_{M};\phi_{M}).

Using this formula, we see that the functor

\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T*}
is exact.
∎

Lemma 5.3

(
cf.
[
NS1
, (2.3.4)]
).

Let the notations be as above.
Then the following diagram of
topoi is commutative
:

(5.3.1)

\begin{CD}(Y_{M}/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T_{M}}@>{j_{T_{M}}}>{}>(Y_{M}/S)_{\rm conv}\\
@V{\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T}}V{}V@V{}V{\epsilon_{\rm conv}}V\\
(Y_{N}/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T_{N}}@>{j_{T_{N}}}>{}>(Y_{N}/S)_{\rm conv}.\\
\end{CD}

The obvious analogue of

(
5.3.1
)
for ringed topoi

((\widetilde{Y_{L}/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{L}/S})

(L=M,N)
also holds.

Proof.

By (
5.2.1
) and (
5.2.2
),

(\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T})^{*}(T_{N})=T_{M}
.
The rest of the proof
is the same as that of
[
NS1
, (2.3.4)]
.
∎

From now on, assume that
\overset{\circ}{T}
is affine.

Lemma 5.4

(
cf.
[
NS1
, (2.3.5)]
).

Let
E
be an admissible sheaf of

{\cal K}_{Y_{M}/S}|_{T_{M}}
-modules in

(Y_{M}/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T_{M}}
.
Then the canonical morphism

(5.4.1)

\epsilon_{{\rm conv}*}j_{T_{M}*}(E)\longrightarrow R\epsilon_{{\rm conv}*}j_{T_{M}*}(E)

is an isomorphism in the derived category

D^{+}({\cal K}_{Y_{N}/S})
.

Proof.

By the proof of
[
Sh2
, Proposition 2.3.3]
which is
a log version of
[
O2
, (4.3)]
,
R^{q}j_{T_{M}*}(E)=0

(q>0)
.
By (
5.2.3
),

(\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T})_{*}(E)
is an admissible sheaf of

{\cal K}_{Y_{N}/S}|_{T_{N}}
-modules.
Hence, by the proof of [loc. cit.] again,

R^{q}j_{T_{N}*}((\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T})_{*}(E))=0

(q>0)
.
Therefore we obtain the following equalities:

\displaystyle\epsilon_{{\rm conv}*}j_{T_{M}*}(E)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{lemm:ymjeps})}{=}~j_{T_{N}*}(\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T})_{*}(E)=Rj_{T_{N}*}(\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T})_{*}(E)~\overset{(\ref{lemm:efstex})}{=}~R(j_{T_{N}}\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{T})_{*}(E)

\displaystyle~\overset{(\ref{lemm:ymjeps})}{=}~R(\epsilon_{\rm conv}j_{T_{M}})_{*}(E)=R\epsilon_{{\rm conv}*}Rj_{T_{M}*}(E)=R\epsilon_{{\rm conv}*}j_{T_{M}*}(E).

∎

Corollary 5.5

(
cf.
[
NS1
, (2.3.6)]
).

Let the notations be as above.
Let
{\cal Y}_{\cal M}:=(\overset{\circ}{\cal Y},{\cal M})
be
a formally log smooth log noetherian
p
-adic formal scheme
over
S

which is topologically of finite type over
S
.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{\cal Y}
is affine.
Let
\iota_{\cal M}\colon Y_{M}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}_{\cal M}

be an immersion over
S
.
Let
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\cal M}

be a prewidening

(Y_{M},{\cal Y}_{\cal M},\iota_{\cal M},{\rm id}_{Y_{M}})
.
Let
{\cal E}
be a coherent crystal of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{\cal M}}
-modules.
Let
L^{\rm UE}_{Y_{M}/S}({\cal E})
be
the log convergent linearization of

{\cal E}
with respect to
\iota_{\cal M}
.
Then the canonical morphism

(5.5.1)

\epsilon_{{\rm conv}*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y_{M}/S}({\cal E})\longrightarrow R\epsilon_{{\rm conv}*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y_{M}/S}({\cal E})

is an isomorphism in
D^{+}({\cal K}_{Y_{N}/S})
.

Proof.

(
5.5
) immediately follows from
(
5.4
) and the definition of

L^{\rm UE}_{Y_{M}/S}({\cal E})

((
3.0.3
)).
∎

Lemma 5.6

(
cf.
[
NS1
, (2.3.7)]
).

Let the notations and the assumption be as in
(
5.5
)
.
Let
{\cal N}
be a fine sub log structure of
{\cal M}

such that
{\cal Y}_{\cal N}:=(\overset{\circ}{\cal Y},{\cal N})

is also formally log smooth over
S
.
Let

(5.6.1)

\begin{CD}Y_{M}@>{\iota_{\cal M}}>{}>{\cal Y}_{\cal M}\\
@V{\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S}}V{}V@V{}V{\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{\cal Y},{\cal M},{\cal N})/S}}V\\
Y_{N}@>{\iota_{\cal N}}>{}>{\cal Y}_{\cal N}\\
\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram whose horizontal morphisms
are closed immersions.
For
L=M
or
N
and
{\cal L}={\cal M}
or
{\cal N}
,
set
{\mathfrak{T}}_{\cal L}:=((\overset{\circ}{Y},L),(\overset{\circ}{\cal Y},{\cal L}),(\overset{\circ}{Y},L)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(\overset{\circ}{\cal Y},{\cal L}),{\rm id}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},L)})
.
Let
\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal L},n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be
the system of the universal enlargements
of
\iota_{\cal L}

with the following natural commutative diagram
for
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}

({\star}={\rm ex}
or nothing
):

(5.6.2)

\begin{CD}{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal M},n}@>{g^{\star}_{{\cal M},n}}>{}>{\cal Y}^{\star}_{\cal M}\\
@V{h_{n}}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal N},n}@>{g^{\star}_{{\cal N},n}}>{}>{\cal Y}^{\star}_{\cal N}.\\
\end{CD}

Assume that the underlying
morphism
\overset{\circ}{h}_{n}\colon\overset{\circ}{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal M},n}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal N},n}
of
h_{n}

is the identity of
\overset{\circ}{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal M},n}

for any
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
.
Let
L^{\rm UE}_{Y_{L}/S}

(
resp.
L^{\rm conv}_{Y_{L}/S})

be the log convergent linearization functor
for coherent crystal of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{\cal L}}
-modules

(
resp. coherent
{\cal K}_{{\cal Y}^{\star}_{\cal L}}
-modules
)
.
Then there exist natural isomorphisms

(5.6.3)

L^{\rm UE}_{Y_{N}/S}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\epsilon_{\rm conv*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y_{M}/S}

and

(5.6.4)

L^{\rm conv}_{Y_{N}/S}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\epsilon_{\rm conv*}L^{\rm conv}_{Y_{M}/S}

of functors. Moreover, the functors

(
5.6.3
)
and
(
5.6.4
)

are functorial with respect to integrable connections

(
cf.
(
3.6
)
)
.

Proof.

Let
\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}}\colon(Y_{M}/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal M},n}}\longrightarrow(Y_{N}/S)_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal N},n}}

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})

be the localized
morphism of topoi
forgetting the log structure.
Using the formula (
5.2.3
),
we can immediately check that

(\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}})_{*}\varphi_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal M},n}}^{*}=\varphi_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal N},n}}^{*}
.
By (
5.3
)
we obtain the following commutative diagram:

(5.6.5)

\begin{CD}\{\text{coherent crystals of }{\cal K}_{{\cal Y}^{\star}_{\cal M}}\text{-modules}\}@>{\{\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}_{{\cal M},n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}>{}>\{\text{coherent crystals of }{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{\cal M}}\text{-modules}\}\\
\Big\|\Big\|\\
\{\text{coherent crystals of }{\cal K}_{{\cal Y}^{\star}_{\cal N}}\text{-modules}\}@>{\{\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}_{{\cal N},n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}>{}>\{\text{coherent crystals of }{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{\cal N}}\text{-modules}\}\end{CD}

\begin{CD}@>{\varphi^{*}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{\cal M}}}>{}>\{\text{coherent crystals of }{\cal K}_{Y_{M}/S}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal M}}}\text{-modules}\}\\
@V{\{(\epsilon_{\rm conv}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{n}})_{*}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}V{}V\\
@>{\varphi^{*}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{\cal N}}}>{}>\{\text{coherent crystals of }{\cal K}_{Y_{N}/S}|_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\cal N}}}\text{-modules}\}\end{CD}

\begin{CD}@>{j_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{M*}}}>{}>\{\text{crystals of }{\cal K}_{Y_{M}/S}\text{-modules}\}\\
@V{\epsilon_{\rm conv*}}V{}V\\
@>{j_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{N*}}}>{}>\{\text{crystals of }{\cal K}_{Y_{N}/S}\text{-modules}\}.\end{CD}

Hence we have the isomorphisms
(
5.6.3
) and (
5.6.4
).

Because the commutative diagram (
5.6.5
) is contravariantly functorial,
the desired functoriality
follows from the construction
\nabla^{0}

in the proof of (
3.6
).
∎

Definition 5.7
.

For a coherent crystal
E
of
{\cal K}_{Y_{M}/S}
-modules,
we call
R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S*}(E)

the
vanishing cycle sheaf
of
E

along

M\setminus N
. We call

R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S*}({\cal K}_{Y_{M}/S})
the

vanishing cycle sheaf
of
Y_{M}/S

along

M\setminus N
.
If
N
is trivial, we omit the word
“along
M\setminus N
”.

The following theorem is a main result in this section:

Theorem 5.8

(Poincaré lemma of a vanishing cycle sheaf
(cf.
[
NS1
, (2.3.10)]
))
.

Let the notations be as in
(
5.5
)
.
Let
E
be a coherent crystal of

{\cal K}_{Y_{N}/S}
-modules and let

({\cal E},\nabla)
be the
{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{\mathfrak{T}}_{\cal M}}
-module
with integrable connection
(
3.6.6
)

corresponding to

\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S}(E):

\nabla\colon{\cal E}\longrightarrow{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Y}_{\cal M}/S}
.
Assume that we are given
the commutative diagrams
(
5.6.1
)

and
(
5.6.2
)

and that
\overset{\circ}{h}_{n}
is the identity for
any
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
.
Then there exists a canonical isomorphism

(5.8.1)

R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S}(E))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}L^{\rm UE}_{Y_{N}/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}_{\cal M}/S})

in
D^{+}({\cal K}_{Y_{N}/S})
.

Proof.

By (
3.7.1
) we have

\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S}(E)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}L^{\rm UE}_{Y_{M}/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}_{\cal M}/S})
.
Applying
R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S*}
to
both hand sides of the above and
using (
5.5
) and (
5.6
),
we obtain the following:

\displaystyle R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S*}(\epsilon^{{\rm conv}*}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S}(E))

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y_{M}/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}_{\cal M}/S})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longleftarrow}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(\overset{\circ}{Y},M,N)/S*}L^{\rm UE}_{Y_{M}/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}_{\cal M}/S})

\displaystyle=L^{\rm UE}_{Y_{N}/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}_{\cal M}/S}).

∎

6
Log convergent linearization functors
of smooth schemes with relative SNCD’s

In this section we show several properties of
log convergent linearization functors
of a smooth scheme with a relative SNCD.
We recall and generalize
several notions in
[
NS1
]
and
[
Nak2
]
.

Let
S
be a
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme
in the sense of
[
O1
]
.
Set
S_{1}:=\underline{\rm Spec}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}/\pi{\cal O}_{S})
.
Let
f\colon X\longrightarrow S_{1}
be a smooth scheme
with a relative SNCD
D
on
X

over
S_{1}
.
Let
Z
be a relative SNCD on

X
over
S_{1}
which
intersects
D
transversally over
S_{1}
.
Let

(6.0.1)

(X,D)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}`{\cal R}\quad{\rm and}\quad(X,Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal R}

be immersions into
formally log smooth log noetherian
p
-adic formal schemes over
S

such that
\overset{\circ}{`{\cal R}}=\overset{\circ}{\cal R}
.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{\cal R}
is topologically of finite type
over
S
.
Set
{\cal P}:=(\overset{\circ}{\cal R},M_{`{\cal R}}\oplus_{{\cal O}^{*}_{\cal R}}M_{{\cal R}})
.
Assume that
{\cal P}
is log smooth.
Then we have a natural closed immersion

(X,D\cup Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
and
the following commutative diagram:

(6.0.2)

\begin{CD}(X,D\cup Z)@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}\\
@V{\epsilon_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S_{1}}}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
(X,Z)@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal R}.\end{CD}

Let
(X,D\cup Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\rm ex}

(resp.
(X,Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal R}^{\rm ex}
)
be the exactification of the upper immersion
(resp. the lower immersion) in (
6.0.2
).
Let
(X,D)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(`{\cal R})^{\rm ex}

be the exactification of the first immersion in
(
6.0.1
).
Let
{\cal M}^{\rm ex}
(resp.
{\cal N}^{\rm ex}
) be
the pull-back of the log structure of

(`{\cal R})^{\rm ex}

(resp.
{\cal R}^{\rm ex}
)
by the morphism

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}\longrightarrow(`{\cal R})^{\rm ex}

(resp.
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}\longrightarrow{\cal R}^{\rm ex}
).
Then we have the following equality:

(6.0.3)

M_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}={\cal M}^{\rm ex}\oplus_{{\cal O}^{*}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}}}{\cal N}^{\rm ex}.

Endow
\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}

with the pull-back of the log structure of
{\cal R}^{\rm ex}

and let
{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
be the resulting log formal scheme.
Then we have the natural exact immersion

(X,Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}

and we see that

{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}=(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex},{\cal N}^{\rm ex})
.
We have the following commutative diagram

(6.0.4)

\begin{CD}(X,D\cup Z)@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}^{\rm ex}\\
@V{\epsilon_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S}}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
(X,Z)@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}\\
\Big\|@V{}V{}V\\
(X,Z)@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal R}^{\rm ex}.\end{CD}

If the former immersion (resp. the latter immersion)
in (
6.0.1
)
have a chart
(P_{1}\longrightarrow Q_{1})
(resp.
(P_{2}\longrightarrow Q_{2})
),
we denote by
P^{\rm ex}_{1}
(resp.
P^{\rm ex}_{2}
)
the inverse image of
Q_{1}
(resp.
Q_{2}
)
by the morphism
(P^{\rm gp}_{1}\longrightarrow Q^{\rm gp}_{1})

(resp.
(P^{\rm gp}_{2}\longrightarrow Q^{\rm gp}_{2})
).
We set

{\cal P}^{{\rm loc,ex}}:={\cal P}\times_{{\rm Spf}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\{P_{1}\oplus P_{2}\})}{\rm Spf}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\{P^{\rm ex}_{1}\oplus P^{\rm ex}_{2}\})

and

{\cal R}^{{\rm loc,ex}}:={\cal R}\times_{{\rm Spf}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\{P_{2}\})}{\rm Spf}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\{P^{\rm ex}_{2}\})
,
respectively.
Let
{\cal Q}^{\rm loc,ex}
be
the fine log formal
{\cal V}
-scheme
whose underlying formal scheme is

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm loc,ex}}
and
whose log structure is the pull-back of
the log structure of
{\cal R}^{{\rm loc,ex}}
.
Then
{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
(resp.
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
)
is the formal completion of

{\cal Q}^{{\rm loc,ex}}
(resp.
{\cal P}^{{\rm loc,ex}}
) along
X
.

For a nonnegative integer
i
and an integer
k
,
set

(6.0.5)

P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}_{k}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}=\begin{cases}0&(k<0),\\
{\rm Im}(\Omega^{k}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i-k}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S}\longrightarrow\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S})&(0\leq k\leq i),\\
\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}&(k>i).\end{cases}

Then we have a filtration

P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}:=\{P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

on
\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}
.

Lemma 6.1
.

(1)
The following formulas hold
:

(6.1.1)

\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}/S}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}=\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S},\quad\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}/S}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}=\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S}\quad(i\in{\mathbb{N}}).

(2)
Assume that
the two horizontal immersions in
(
6.0.2
)

are exact.
Set

(6.1.2)

P^{{\cal P}/{\cal R}}_{k}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}/S}=\begin{cases}0&(k<0),\\
{\rm Im}(\Omega^{k}_{{\cal P}/S}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}\Omega^{i-k}_{{\cal R}/S}\longrightarrow\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}/S})&(0\leq k\leq i),\\
\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}/S}&(k>i).\end{cases}

Then

(6.1.3)

P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}=P^{{\cal P}/{\cal R}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}.

Proof.

(1): To prove the first formula in (
6.1.1
),
we have only to prove
that
\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}/S}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}=\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}
.
Because we have a natural morphism

\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}/S}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}
,
the question is local.
We may assume that the immersions

(X,D)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}`{\cal R}

and

(X,Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal R}

have charts
(P_{1}\longrightarrow Q_{1})
and
(P_{2}\longrightarrow Q_{2})
, respectively.
Let the notations be after (
6.0.4
).
Because the natural morphism

{\cal P}^{\rm loc,ex}\longrightarrow{\cal P}
is log étale,
we have the following by
[
Ka
, (3.12)]
:

(6.1.4)

\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm loc,ex}/S}=\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}/S}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm loc,ex}}.

Furthermore, we may assume that
there exists a lift
({\cal X},{\cal D}\cup{\cal Z})
of

(X,D\cup Z)
over
S
such that
the exact immersion

(X,D\cup Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\rm loc,ex}

factors through

(X,D\cup Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}({\cal X},{\cal D}\cup{\cal Z})
.
By the proof of
[
NS1
, (2.1.4)]
,
we may assume that

{\cal P}^{\rm loc,ex}=({\cal X},{\cal D}\cup{\cal Z})\widehat{\times}_{S}\widehat{{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{c}
for some
c\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}

étale locally.
In this case we obtain the following equality:

\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm loc,ex}/S}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm loc,ex}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}=\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}
.
Hence we obtain the first formula in
(
6.1.1
).
Similarly we obtain the second formula
in (
6.1.1
) by the argument above.

(2):
Because we have a natural morphism

P^{{\cal P}/{\cal R}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}\longrightarrow P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}
,
the question is local.
By the assumption,
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
is
the formal completion of

{\cal P}

along
X
with the pull-back of
the log structure of
{\cal P}
.
By (1) we see that

\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}/S}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}=\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}

(i\in{\mathbb{N}})

and

\Omega^{j}_{{\cal Q}/S}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}=\Omega^{j}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S}

(j\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
Since
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
is the formal completion of

{\cal P}
along
X
,

\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}
is an exact functor.
Hence, for
0\leq k\leq i
, we obtain the following equality:

(6.1.5)

{\rm Im}(\Omega^{k}_{{\cal P}/S}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}\Omega^{i-k}_{{\cal Q}/S}\longrightarrow\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}/S})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}={\rm Im}(\Omega^{k}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i-k}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S}\longrightarrow\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}).

This is nothing but the equality (
6.1.3
).
∎

Remark 6.2
.

Consider the case where the horizontal immersions in
(
6.0.2
) are not exact. Let

P^{{\cal P}/{\cal R}}
be the filtration
on
\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}/S}
defined by
the formula (
6.1.2
).
It is natural to ask whether
the following equalities

(6.2.1)

P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}=P^{{\cal P}/{\cal R}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}},\quad P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal R}^{\rm ex}}=P^{{\cal P}/{\cal R}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}

hold.
However neither of these does not hold in general.
Indeed, consider the case

X={\rm Spec}(\kappa[t])
,
Z=\emptyset
,
D=\{t=0\}
,

{\cal P}=({\rm Spf}({\cal V}\{x,y\}),\{x=0\}\cup\{y=0\})

and consider the immersion

(X,D)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}

defined by
x\mapsto t,y\mapsto t
.
Then

{\cal R}=(\overset{\circ}{{\cal P}},{\cal O}_{\cal P}^{*})
,

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}=({\rm Spf}({\cal V}\{x\}[[u-1]]),\{x=0\})

and

{\cal Q}={\cal Q}^{\rm ex}=(\overset{\circ}{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}},{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}^{*})
.
By the definition of

P^{{\cal P}/{\cal R}}
, we see that

P^{{\cal P}/{\cal R}}_{1}\Omega^{2}_{{\cal P}/S}={\rm Im}(\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}/S}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal R}/S}\longrightarrow\Omega^{2}_{{\cal P}/S})

is generated by

xd\log x\wedge d\log y
and
yd\log x\wedge d\log y
.
Hence the image of the following natural morphism

P^{{\cal P}/{\cal R}}_{1}\Omega^{2}_{{\cal P}/S}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{2}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}

is generated by
xd\log x\wedge d\log u
.
On the other hand,
set
{\cal U}={\cal Q}
or
{\cal R}
.
Then

P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal U}^{\rm ex}}_{1}\Omega^{2}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}={\rm Im}(\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal U}^{\rm ex}/S}\longrightarrow\Omega^{2}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S})=\Omega^{2}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}.

Since
\langle xd\log x\wedge d\log u\rangle\not=\Omega^{2}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}
,
neither of the formulas in (
6.2.1
)
does not hold.

In this way, we think that
the filtration

P^{{\cal P}/{\cal R}}

is not good when

(X,D)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(\overset{\circ}{{\cal P}},{\cal M})

is not exact
and we do not consider

P^{{\cal P}/{\cal R}}
any more in this paper.

For a finitely generated monoid
Q

and a set
\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\}

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
of generators of
Q
,
we say that
\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\}
is
minimal
if
n
is minimal.
Let
Y=(\overset{\circ}{Y},M_{Y})
be
an fs(=fine and saturated) log (formal) scheme.
Let
y
be a point of
\overset{\circ}{Y}
.
Let
m_{1,y},\ldots,m_{r,y}
be local sections of
M_{Y}
around
y

whose images in
M_{Y,y}/{\cal O}_{Y,y}^{*}

form a minimal set of generators of
M_{Y,y}/{\cal O}_{Y,y}^{*}
.
Let
D(M_{Y})_{i}

(1\leq i\leq r)

be the local closed subscheme of

\overset{\circ}{Y}
defined by the ideal sheaf generated by
the image of
m_{i,y}
in
{\cal O}_{Y}
.
For a positive integer
k
,
let
D^{(k)}(M_{Y})
be the disjoint union of
the
k
-fold intersections of different
D(M_{Y})_{i}
’s.
Assume that

(6.2.2)

M_{Y,y}/{\cal O}^{*}_{Y,y}\simeq{\mathbb{N}}^{r}

for any point
y
of
\overset{\circ}{Y}

and for some
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
depending on
y
.
It is easy to show that

{\rm Aut}({\mathbb{N}}^{r})\simeq{\mathfrak{S}}_{r}

(see
[
Nak2
, §4]
for the proof of this).
Consequently

{\rm Aut}(M_{Y,y}/{\cal O}^{*}_{Y,y})\simeq{\mathfrak{S}}_{r}
.
Hence the scheme
D^{(k)}(M_{Y})
is
independent of the choice of
m_{1,y},\ldots,m_{r,y}

and it is globalized.
We denote this globalized scheme
by the same symbol
D^{(k)}(M_{Y})
.
Set
D^{(0)}(M_{Y}):=\overset{\circ}{Y}
.
Let
c^{(k)}\colon D^{(k)}(M_{Y})\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{Y}

be the natural morphism.

As in
[
D1
, (3.1.4)]
and
[
NS1
, (2.2.18)]
,
we have an orientation sheaf
\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(k)}(D(M_{Y}))

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})
associated to the set of
D(M_{Y})_{i}
’s.
We have the following equality

(6.2.3)

c^{(k)}_{*}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(k)}(D(M_{Y}))=\bigwedge^{k}(M_{Y}^{\rm gp}/{\cal O}_{Y}^{*})

as sheaves of abelian groups on
\overset{\circ}{Y}
.

The following has been proved in
[
Nak2
]
:

Proposition 6.3

(
[
Nak2
, (4.3)]
)
.

Let
g\colon Y\longrightarrow Y^{\prime}
be a morphism of fs log schemes
satisfying the condition
(
6.2.2
)
.
Assume that, for each point
y\in\overset{\circ}{Y}

and for each member
m
of the minimal generators
of
M_{Y,y}/{\cal O}^{*}_{Y,y}
, there exists
a unique member
m^{\prime}
of the minimal generators of

M_{Y^{\prime},\overset{\circ}{g}(y)}/{\cal O}^{*}_{Y^{\prime},\overset{\circ}{g}(y)}

such that
g^{*}(m^{\prime})\in m^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}}
.
Then there exists a canonical morphism

g^{(k)}\colon D^{(k)}(M_{Y})\longrightarrow D^{(k)}(M_{Y^{\prime}})
fitting
into the following commutative diagram of schemes
:

(6.3.1)

\begin{CD}D^{(k)}(M_{Y})@>{g^{(k)}}>{}>D^{(k)}(M_{Y^{\prime}})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\overset{\circ}{Y}@>{\overset{\circ}{g}}>{}>\overset{\circ}{Y}{}^{\prime}.\end{CD}

When
Y=(X,D)
, then
we denote
D^{(k)}(M_{Y})
by
D^{(k)}
and

\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(k)}(D(M_{Y}))
by

\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(D/S_{1})
.
The sheaf
\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(D/S_{1})
is
extended to an abelian sheaf

\varpi^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)

in the log convergent topos

(\widetilde{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S})_{\rm conv}

since, for an object

(U,T,\iota,u)\in{\rm Conv}({(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S})
,
we have the pull-back of the sheaf

\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(D/S_{1})
to
\overset{\circ}{U}

by
u

and since the closed immersion

\iota\colon\overset{\circ}{U}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{T}

is a homeomorphism of topological spaces.
If
Z=\emptyset
, then we denote

\varpi^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)

by

\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(D/S)
.

Definition 6.4
.

We call

\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(D/S_{1})\text{ (resp. }\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(D/S),\text{ }\varpi^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))

the
zariskian orientation sheaf

(resp. the
convergent orientation sheaf
,
the
log convergent orientation sheaf
) of

D^{(k)}/S_{1}
(resp.
D^{(k)}/S
,

(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S
).

Let
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
be two fine log structures
on
\overset{\circ}{Y}
such that

M_{Y}=M_{1}\oplus_{{\cal O}^{*}_{Y}}M_{2}
.
Assume that the condition (
6.2.2
)
is satisfied for
M_{1}
.
Let
m_{1,y},\ldots,m_{r,y}
be
local sections of
M_{1}
around
y

whose images in
M_{1,y}/{\cal O}_{Y,y}^{*}

form a minimal set of generators of
M_{1,y}/{\cal O}_{Y,y}^{*}
.
Set
Y_{i}:=(\overset{\circ}{Y},M_{i})

(i=1,2)
.
Endow
D^{(k)}(M_{1})
with the pull-back of
the log structure of
M_{2}
and denote
the resulting log scheme by
(D^{(k)}(M_{1}),M_{2})

by abuse of notation.
Let
b^{(k)}\colon(D^{(k)}(M_{1}),M_{2})\longrightarrow(\overset{\circ}{Y},M_{2})

be the natural morphism.
By abuse of notation, we denote by the same symbol

b^{(k)}
the underlying morphism

D^{(k)}(M_{1})\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{Y}
.

Now we come back to the situation
(
6.0.1
) (and (
6.0.2
)).

Lemma 6.5
.

The log scheme

(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex},{\cal M}^{\rm ex})

satisfies the condition
(
6.2.2
)
.
In particular
D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})

is well-defined.

Proof.

Identify the points of
X
with the image of those
in
\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}
.
Because

(X,D)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex},{\cal M}^{\rm ex})

is exact and closed,
we have an equality

{\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{x}/{\cal O}^{*}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex},x}=M(D)_{x}/{\cal O}^{*}_{D,x}

for a point
x\in X
. Hence (
6.5
) is clear.
∎

By abuse of notation, denote
by
(D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}),{\cal N}^{\rm ex})

the log formal scheme whose underlying formal scheme
is
D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})
and
whose log structure is the pull-back of
{\cal N}^{\rm ex}

by the morphism

D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}
.

Set
Z|_{D^{(k)}}:=Z\times_{X}D^{(k)}
.
The scheme

Z|_{D^{(k)}}
is a relative SNCD on
D^{(k)}/S_{1}
.
Let

a^{(k)}\colon(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})\longrightarrow(X,Z)
and

b^{(k)}:(D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}),{\cal N}^{\rm ex})\longrightarrow{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}

be the morphisms induced by the natural closed immersions.

Let the middle objects in the following table
be the prewidenings obtained by
the left immersions over
S
and
let the right objects be the system
of the universal enlargements of
these prewidenings:

(X,D\cup Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}

T

\overset{\to}{T}:=\{T_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

(X,Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}

T_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}

\overset{\to}{T}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}:=\{T_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}),{\cal N}^{\rm ex})

T^{(k)}

\overset{\to}{T}{}^{(k)}:=\{T^{(k)}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

Let
g_{n}\colon T_{n}\longrightarrow{\cal P}
,

g^{\rm ex}_{n}\colon T_{n}\longrightarrow{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
,

g_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},n}\colon T_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},n}\longrightarrow{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
,
and

g^{(k)}_{n}\colon T^{(k)}_{n}\longrightarrow(D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}),{\cal N}^{\rm ex})

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})

be the natural morphisms.
Let
c^{(k)}_{n}\colon T^{(k)}_{n}\longrightarrow T_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},n}

be the morphism induced by

b^{(k)}\colon(D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}),{\cal N}^{\rm ex})\longrightarrow{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
.

Lemma 6.6

(
cf.
[
NS1
, (2.2.16) (1)]
)
.

The natural morphism

T^{(k)}_{n}\longrightarrow T_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},n}\times_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}(D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}),{\cal N}^{\rm ex})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

By the universality of the universal enlargement,
this is a local question.
Let
x
be a point of
X
.
By taking a local exactification

(X,D\cup Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\rm loc,ex}

of the immersion
(X,D\cup Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
,
we may assume that the immersion

(X,D\cup Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
is exact.
Hence, by
[
NS1
, (2.1.5)]
,
we may assume that
there exist a formally smooth formal scheme
{\cal X}

and transversal relative SNCD’s

{\cal D}
and
{\cal Z}
on
{\cal X}
such that

{\cal P}=({\cal X},{\cal D}\cup{\cal Z})

and such that there exist exact immersions

(X,D)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}({\cal X},{\cal D})

and
(X,Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}({\cal X},{\cal Z})
over
S
.
We may assume that
{\cal X}
is affine
(say
{\cal X}={\rm Spf}(A)
) and
may have the following cartesian diagrams

(6.6.1)

\begin{CD}{\cal D}\cup{\cal Z}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal X}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{g}V\\
\underline{\rm Spf}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{d^{\prime}}\}/(x_{1}\cdots x_{s}))@>{\subset}>{}>\underline{\rm Spf}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{d^{\prime}}\}),\end{CD}

(6.6.2)

\begin{CD}X@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal X}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{g}V\\
\underline{\rm Spf}_{S_{1}}({\cal O}_{S_{1}}\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{d^{\prime}}\}/(x_{d+1},\ldots,x_{d^{\prime}}))@>{\subset}>{}>\underline{\rm Spf}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{d^{\prime}}\})\end{CD}

for some
s\leq d^{\prime}
, where
g
is an étale morphism.
Let
{\cal D}_{1\cdots r}\subset{\cal D}
be a
closed subscheme defined by an equation

x_{1}=\cdots=x_{r}=0
.
Set
e:=d^{\prime}-d
.
For
\underline{m}:=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{e})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{e}
, set

x^{\underline{m}}:=x^{m_{1}}_{d+1}\cdots x^{m_{e}}_{d^{\prime}}
.
For
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
and

\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{e}

with
|\underline{m}|=n
,
let
t_{\underline{m}}
be independent variables.
Then, by (
2.3.1
),

{\cal O}_{T_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},n}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal X}}{\cal O}_{{\cal D}_{1\cdots r}}=(A[t_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r},|\underline{m}|=n]/((x^{\underline{m}}-\pi t_{\underline{m}})+(p{\textrm{-}}{\rm torsion})))^{\widehat{}}/(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}).

Set
D_{1\cdots r}:={\cal D}_{1\cdots r}\times_{\cal X}X
.
On the other hand,
the structure sheaf of the
n
-th universal log enlargement
of the closed immersion
D_{1\cdots r}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal D}_{1\cdots r}
is

\displaystyle({\cal O}_{{\cal D}_{1\cdots r}}[t_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{k},|\underline{m}|=n]/((x^{\underline{m}}-\pi t_{\underline{m}})+(p{\textrm{-}}{\rm torsion})))^{\widehat{}}

\displaystyle=(A[t_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{k},|\underline{m}|=n]/((x^{\underline{m}}-\pi t_{\underline{m}})+(p{\textrm{-}}{\rm torsion})))^{\widehat{}}/(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r})

\displaystyle={\cal O}_{T_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},n}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal X}}{\cal O}_{{\cal D}_{1\cdots r}}.

Furthermore it is immediate to see
that there exists a natural isomorphism

T_{n}^{(k)}\simeq T_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},n}\times_{({\cal X},{\cal Z})}({\cal D}^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}),{\cal N}^{\rm ex})

as log formal schemes. Hence we can complete the proof.
∎

Set

(X_{n},D_{n}\cup Z_{n}):=(X,D\cup Z)\times_{\cal P}T_{n}
,

(X_{n},Z_{n}):=(X,Z)\times_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}T_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},n}

and

(D^{(k)}_{n},Z_{n}|_{D^{(k)}_{n}}):=(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})\times_{(D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}),{\cal N}^{\rm ex})}T^{(k)}_{n}

(n\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
As usual, we denote the left representable objects
in the following table
by the right ones for simplicity of notation:

((X_{n},D_{n}\cup Z_{n})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T_{n})\in((X,D\cup Z)/S)_{\rm conv}

T_{n}

((X_{n},Z_{n})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},n})\in((X,Z)/S)_{\rm conv}

T_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},n}

((D^{(k)}_{n},Z_{n}|_{D^{(k)}_{n}})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T^{(k)}_{n})\in((D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)})/S})_{\rm conv}

T^{(k)}_{n}

Let
a^{(k)}_{\rm conv}:((D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)})/S})_{\rm conv}\longrightarrow((X,Z)/S)_{\rm conv}

be the morphism of topoi induced by the
morphism

a^{(k)}
.

Let

j_{T}:((X,D\cup Z)/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T}\longrightarrow((X,D\cup Z)/S)_{\rm conv}

j_{T_{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}:((X,Z)/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T_{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}\longrightarrow((X,Z)/S)_{\rm conv}

j_{T^{(k)}}:((D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S)_{\rm conv}|_{T^{(k)}}\longrightarrow((D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S)_{\rm conv}

be localization functors and let

\varphi^{*}:\{{\rm coherent~crystals~of~}{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}{\rm-modules}\}\longrightarrow

{\varphi}^{*}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}:\{{\rm coherent~crystals~of~}{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\rm-modules}\}\longrightarrow

\varphi^{(k)*}:\{{\rm coherent~crystals~of~}{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}{}^{(k)}}{\rm-modules}\}\longrightarrow

\{{\rm coherent~crystals~of~}{\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}|_{T}{\rm-modules}\}

\{{\rm coherent~crystals~of~}{\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}|_{T_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\rm-modules}\}

\{{\rm coherent~crystals~of~}{\cal K}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S}|_{T^{(k)}}{\rm-modules}\}

be the morphisms of pull-backs
defined in (
3.0.1
).
Let
h_{n}
be
g_{n}
or
g^{\rm ex}_{n}
.
Let
{\cal U}
be
{\cal P}
or
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
.
Let

h_{n}:(\overset{\circ}{T}_{n,{\rm zar}},{\cal K}_{T_{n}})\longrightarrow(\overset{\circ}{\cal U}_{\rm zar},{\cal K}_{\cal U})

g_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},n}:(\overset{\circ}{T}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},n,{\rm zar}},{\cal K}_{T_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},n}})\longrightarrow(\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}_{\rm zar},{\cal K}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}})

g^{(k)}_{n}:(\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{(k)}_{n{\rm zar}},{\cal K}_{T_{n}})\longrightarrow(D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})_{\rm zar},{\cal K}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})})

be natural morphisms
of ringed topoi
(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
.

For a coherent

{\cal K}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})}
-module
{\cal E}
,
set also

L^{(k){\rm conv}}({\cal E}):=j_{T^{(k)}*}\varphi^{(k)*}\{g^{(k)*}_{n}({\cal E})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\in((D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S)_{\rm conv}.

By (
3.6
) we have a complex

L^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})
of

{\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}
-modules.
By the log convergent Poincaré lemma ((
3.7
)),
we have a natural quasi-isomorphism

(6.6.3)

{\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}L^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}).

Similarly we have the following two quasi-isomorphisms:

(6.6.4)

{\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),

(6.6.5)

{\cal K}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}L^{(k){\rm conv}}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}).

The family

\{P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}_{k}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\}_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}

gives a subcomplex

P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}

of
\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}
.
By using the natural morphism

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}\longrightarrow{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
,
we can consider

P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}_{k}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}

(i\in{\mathbb{N}})

as an
{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}
-module.
Set

(6.6.6)

P^{D}_{k}L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}):=L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}_{k}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\quad(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

Since the derivative of
L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})
produces only log poles coming from

\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S}
,

P^{D}_{k}L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})

is stable under the derivative of

L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})
.

The following theorem (2) is
one of key theorems in this paper.
This is different from the corresponding theorem
in the log crystalline case (
[
NS1
, (2.2.17) (2)]
).

Theorem 6.7
.

(1)

For each
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
,
the natural morphism

(6.7.1)

{\cal K}_{T_{n}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{T_{n}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}

is injective.

(2)

The natural morphism

(6.7.2)

P^{D}_{k}L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\longrightarrow L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})

is injective.

Proof.

(1): (1) immediately follows from (
3.2
).

(2):
This is a local problem.
We may assume that there exist the cartesian diagrams
(
6.6.1
) and (
6.6.2
).
As in the proof of (
6.6
), we may assume that

{\cal P}=({\cal X},{\cal D}\cup{\cal Z})
,

{\cal R}=({\cal X},{\cal Z})

and
{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}=(\widehat{\cal X},\widehat{\cal Z})
.
Let
(U,T,\iota,u)
be an object of

{\rm Conv}((X,Z)/S)
.
We may also assume that
\overset{\circ}{T}
is affine.
Then we have a morphism
e\colon T\longrightarrow{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
over
S

such that the composite morphism

U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T\longrightarrow{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}

is equal to the composite morphism

U\longrightarrow(X,Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
.
Consider the composite immersion

(X,Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}\times_{S}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
.
Let
({\cal Q}^{\rm ex}\times_{S}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex})^{\rm ex}

(resp.
(T\times_{S}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex})^{\rm ex}
)
be the exactification
of the immersion

(X,Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}\times_{S}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}

(resp.
U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T\times_{S}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
).
By the proof of (
2.14
), we see that
the two induced morphisms

p_{i}\colon({\cal Q}^{\rm ex}\times_{S}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex})^{\rm ex}\longrightarrow{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}

(i=1,2)
by the two projections

{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}\times_{S}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}\longrightarrow{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
are formally smooth.
Let
r\colon(T\times_{S}{\cal Q})^{\rm ex}\longrightarrow{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}

be the second projection.
Then we have the following diagram (cf. the proof of (
3.5
)):

(6.7.3)

\begin{CD}\{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}(T\times_{S}{\cal Q})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}@>{\{q_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}>{}>(T\times_{S}{\cal Q})^{\rm ex}@>{r}>{}>{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}\\
@V{\{p^{\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}}V{}V\\
