T.\\
\end{CD}

Let
(U,T,\iota,u)
be an object of

{\rm Conv}((X,Z)/S)
.
Set

{\cal M}^{i}_{k}:=P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}_{k}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

(i,k\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
Then, by (
3.5.2
),

\{P^{D}_{k}L^{\rm conv}_{X/S}(\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\}_{T}=\varprojlim_{n}p^{\prime}_{n*}q_{n}^{*}r^{*}({\cal M}^{i}_{k}).

Because
\varprojlim_{n}
and
p^{\prime}_{n*}
are left exact
and because
q_{n}^{*}
is left exact by (
3.2
),
we have only to prove that the morphism

r^{*}({\cal M}^{i}_{k})\longrightarrow r^{*}({\cal M}^{i}_{k+1})
is injective.
For a strict log scheme
{\cal U}
over
{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
, denote

{\cal U}\times_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}(T\times_{S}{\cal Q})^{\rm ex}
by
{\cal U}_{T}
.
Then the desired injectivity is clear because

r^{*}({\cal M}^{i}_{k})=P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{T}}_{k}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{T}/T}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.
∎

Remark 6.8
.

Let
(U,T,\iota,u)
be an object of
{\rm Conv}((X,D\cup Z)/S)
.
We claim that the following pull-back morphism

(6.8.1)

\{{\rm coherent}~{\cal K}_{{\cal P}}{\textrm{-}}{\rm modules}\}\longrightarrow\{{\rm coherent}~{\cal K}_{(T\times_{S}{{\cal P}})^{\rm ex}}{\textrm{-}}{\rm modules}\}

is not exact in general.
Indeed, let the notations be as in (
3.4
).
Consider the case where
U=X
,
T={\cal P}
with the diagonal immersion

U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T\times_{S}{\cal P}
.
Then, as in (
3.4
),
we can see that the functor (
6.8.1
) is not exact in general.

By (
6.7
) (2), the family

\{P^{D}_{k}L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

of
{\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}
-modules
defines a filtration on the complex

L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})
.
Hence we obtain an object

(L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),\{P^{D}_{k}L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}})

in
{\rm C}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S})
.
For simplicity of notation, we denote it by

(L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),P^{D}).

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
{\cal E}
be a

{\cal K}_{\cal P}
-module.
Then there exists a canonical isomorphism

(6.9.1)

L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}({\cal E}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}b^{(k)}_{*}\varpi_{\rm zar}(D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}({\cal E})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}a^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}\varpi^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z).

Proof.

(
6.9
) immediately
follows from the definition of

\varpi^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)
.
∎

Proposition 6.10
.

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{\cal P}
is affine over
S
.
Then there exist the following quasi-isomorphisms

(6.10.1)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{D}}

\displaystyle L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}a^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}L^{(k){\rm conv}}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(D({\cal M}^{\rm ex})/S))[-k]

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longleftarrow}a^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))[-k]

in
D^{+}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S})
.

Proof.

Let
r
be a nonnegative integer such that

M_{X,x}/{\cal O}_{X,x}^{*}\simeq{\mathbb{N}}^{r}
.
By abuse of notation,
for
1\leq i\leq r
and
for any different
i_{0},\ldots,i_{k}

(1\leq i_{0},\ldots,i_{k}\leq r)
,
denote
D(M_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}})_{i}

and
\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{i_{0}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{i_{k}}

by
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{i}
and

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{i_{0}\cdots i_{k}}
, respectively.
Denote
\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k)}(M_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}})

and

\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{D}(M_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}))

by

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}
and

\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S})
, respectively.
Denote the natural local exact closed immersion

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{i_{0}\cdots i_{k}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}
by
b_{i_{0}\cdots i_{k}}
.
Let

b^{(k)}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})

be the natural morphism.
We have a natural immersion

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}

of (formal) schemes over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Because

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}=\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}

as topological spaces,
we can identify
\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S})

with
\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{0})
.

Identify the points of
\overset{\circ}{T}_{n}

with those of
\overset{\circ}{X}
.
Identify also the images of the points of
\overset{\circ}{X}

in
\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}
with the points of
\overset{\circ}{X}
.
Let
x
be a point of
\overset{\circ}{T}_{n}
.
Then, for a nonnegative integer
k
,
there exists the following Poincaré residue morphism

(6.10.2)

\displaystyle{\rm Res}^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}:

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}({\cal K}_{T_{n}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S})\owns\sigma\otimes d\log m_{i_{0}}\cdots d\log m_{i_{k-1}}\omega\longmapsto

\displaystyle\sigma\otimes b^{*}_{i_{0}\cdots i_{k-1}}(\omega)\otimes({\rm orientation}~(i_{0}\cdots i_{k-1}))

\displaystyle\in c^{(k)}_{n*}({\cal K}_{T_{n}^{(k)}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}))[-k])

\displaystyle(\sigma\in{\cal K}_{T_{n}},\omega\in\Omega^{\bullet-k}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S})

(c.f.
[
Nak2
, (4.4.1)]
).
We claim that this is an isomorphism.
Indeed, this is a local problem.
By replacing
{\cal P}
with
{\cal P}^{\rm loc,ex}

and
{\cal R}
with
{\cal R}^{\rm loc,ex}
, respectively,
we may assume that
the immersions
(X,D\cup Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}

and
(X,Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal R}
are exact.
Then, as in the existence of
the Poincaré residue isomorphism with respect to

{\cal P}/{\cal R}
(
[
Nak2
, (4.3)]
),
we have the following isomorphism

(6.10.3)

\displaystyle{\rm Res}^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}:\quad

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}({\cal K}_{T_{n}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S})={\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{{\cal P}/{\cal R}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}({\cal K}_{T_{n}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}/S})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}c^{(k)}_{n*}({\cal K}_{T_{n}^{(k)}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}))[-k]).

Here we have used (
6.1.3
) and (
6.6
) for
the equality in (
6.10.3
) and
the last isomorphism in (
6.10.3
), respectively.
Hence, by (
6.7
) (2) and (
3.5
),
we have the following equalities:

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{D}}L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})=L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}({\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})

\displaystyle\overset{L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}({\rm Res}^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}})}{=}L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(b_{*}^{(k)}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(D({\cal M})/S)))[-k].

By (
4.5
)
this complex is equal to

a^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}L^{(k){\rm conv}}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(D({\cal M})/S))[-k],

which is equal to

a^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}(L^{(k){\rm conv}}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))[-k]

by (
6.9
).

By (
3.7
) we obtain
the second quasi-isomorphism in (
6.10.1
).
∎

Remark 6.11
.

By using the (filtered) cohomological descent
(
[
NS1
, Lemma 1.5.1]
), the commutativity of
the diagram in [loc. cit., Lemma 1.3.4.1] and (
6.13
) below,
we can easily eliminate the assumption of the affineness
in (
6.10
).

For simplicity of notation, set

({\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S},P^{D}):=({\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S},\{{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}).

Proposition 6.12
.

Let
\beta_{n}\colon X_{n}\longrightarrow X
be a natural morphism
and
identify
(T_{n})_{{\rm zar}}
with
(X_{n})_{\rm zar}
.
Then

(6.12.1)

Ru^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S*}(L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),P^{D})=(\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n})({\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S},P^{D})

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))
.

Proof.

By the obvious relative version of

[
Sh2
, Corollary 2.3.4]
which is the log version of

[
O2
, Corollary 4.4]
,
the left hand side of (
6.12.1
)
is equal to

\displaystyle(u^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S*}L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),\{u^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S*}L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}))\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}).

The rest of the proof is the same as that of (
3.8
).
∎

The following is a log convergent analogue of

[
Nak2
, (4.8)]
:

Proposition 6.13
.

Let
{\cal V}^{\prime}
,
\pi^{\prime}
,
\kappa^{\prime}
and
S^{\prime}

be similar objects to
{\cal V}
,
\pi

\kappa
and
S

as in
§
2
, respectively.
Let

(X^{\prime},D^{\prime})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}`{\cal R}^{\prime}\quad{\rm and}\quad(X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal R}^{\prime}

be similar closed immersions to

(X,D)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}`{\cal R}\quad{\rm and}\quad(X,Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal R},

Let
{\cal P}^{\prime}
and
{\cal Q}^{\prime}

be similar log formal schemes to

{\cal P}
and
{\cal Q}
, respectively.
Let
{\cal M}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}
be the similar log structure
to
{\cal M}^{\rm ex}
.
Let

(6.13.1)

\begin{CD}(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}^{\prime}\\
@V{\epsilon_{(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime},Z^{\prime})/S}}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
(X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal R}^{\prime}\end{CD}

be a similar diagram to
(\ref{eqn:pvq})
over
a
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}^{\prime}
-scheme
S^{\prime}
.
Let
a^{\prime}{}^{(k)}\colon(D^{\prime(k)},Z|_{D^{\prime(k)}})\longrightarrow(X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})

be the natural morphism of log schemes over
S^{\prime}_{1}
.
Let
L^{\prime(k)}
be the log linearization functor for
coherent
{\cal K}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex})}
-modules.
Assume that there exists a morphism
from the commutative diagram
(
6.0.2
)
to
the commutative diagram
(
6.13.1
)
over
S\longrightarrow S^{\prime}
.
Let

{\mathfrak{g}}\colon(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex},{\cal M}^{\rm ex})\longrightarrow(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\prime\rm ex},{\cal M}{}^{\prime\rm ex})

and

{\mathfrak{g}}_{(X,Z)}\colon(X,Z)\longrightarrow(X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})

be the induced morphisms.
Assume that, for each point
x\in\overset{\circ}{\cal P}^{\rm ex}

and for each member
m
of the minimal generators
of
{\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{x}/{\cal O}^{*}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex},x}
,
there exists a unique member
m^{\prime}
of the minimal generators of

{\cal M}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}_{\overset{\circ}{g}(x)}/{\cal O}^{*}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex},\overset{\circ}{g}(x)}

such that
g^{*}(m^{\prime})=m
and such that the image of
the other minimal generators of

{\cal M}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}_{\overset{\circ}{g}(x)}/{\cal O}^{*}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex},\overset{\circ}{g}(x)}
by
g^{*}

are the trivial element of

{\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{x}/{\cal O}^{*}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex},x}
.
Let
b^{\prime}{}^{(k)}\colon D^{(k)}({\cal M}{}^{\prime\rm ex})\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}{}^{\prime\rm ex}

be the similar morphism to
b^{(k)}
.
Let
\{T^{\prime}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

(
resp.
\{T^{\prime}{}^{(k)}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty})

be the system of the log universal enlargements of
the immersion
(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\prime}

(
resp.
(D^{\prime}{}^{(k)},Z^{\prime}|_{D^{\prime}{}^{(k)}})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}),{\cal N}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}))

and
let
c^{\prime}{}^{(k)}_{n}\colon T^{\prime}{}^{(k)}_{n}\longrightarrow T^{\prime}_{n}

be the morphism induced by
b^{\prime}{}^{(k)}
.
Then the following diagram is commutative
:

(6.13.2)

\begin{CD}{\rm Res}:{\mathfrak{g}}_{(X,Z)_{\rm crys}*}L^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}({\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S})@>{\sim}>{}>\\
@A{}A{}A\\
{\rm Res}:L^{\rm conv}_{(X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}}({\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}/{\cal Q}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}/S^{\prime}})@>{\sim}>{}>\end{CD}

\begin{CD}{\mathfrak{g}}_{(X,Z)_{\rm crys}*}a^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}(L^{(k){\rm conv}}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(D/S;Z))[-k]\\
@A{}A{}A\\
a^{\prime}{}^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}(L^{\prime}{}^{(k){\rm conv}}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}{}^{\prime\rm ex})/S^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(D^{\prime}/S^{\prime};Z^{\prime}))[-k].\end{CD}

Proof.

We may assume that the immersions

(X,D\cup Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}

and

(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\prime}

are exact and closed.
Let
{\mathfrak{g}}_{n}\colon T_{n}\longrightarrow T^{\prime}_{n}
be
the induced morphism by the morphism
{\cal P}\longrightarrow{\cal P}^{\prime}
.
Then, as in
[
Nak2
, (4.8)]
,
we obtain the following isomorphism

(6.13.3)

\begin{CD}{\mathfrak{g}}_{n*}({\rm Res}^{{\cal P}/{\cal Q}}):{\mathfrak{g}}_{n*}({\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{{\cal P}/{\cal Q}}}({\cal K}_{T_{n}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}/S}))@>{\sim}>{}>\\
@A{}A{}A\\
{\rm Res}^{{\cal P}^{\prime}/{\cal Q}^{\prime}}:{\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{{\cal P}^{\prime}/{\cal Q}^{\prime}}}({\cal K}_{T^{\prime}_{n}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})@>{\sim}>{}>\end{CD}

\begin{CD}{\mathfrak{g}}_{n*}c^{(k)}_{n*}({\cal K}_{T_{n}^{(k)}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}))[-k])\\
@A{}A{}A\\
c^{\prime}{}^{(k)}_{n*}({\cal K}_{T^{\prime}{}^{(k)}_{n}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\prime{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}{}^{\prime\rm ex})/S^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm zar}(D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}))[-k]).\end{CD}

Hence we obtain (
6.13
)
by (
4.5.2
) and (
6.9.1
).
∎

7
(Bi)simplicial log convergent topoi

In this section we give the notations of
(bi)simplicial log convergent topoi and
(bi)simplicial Zariski topoi for later sections.

Let the notations be as in the beginning of
§
2
.
Let
Y=\underset{i\in I}{\bigcup}Y_{i}

be an open covering of
Y
, where

I
is a (not necessarily finite) set.
Set
Y_{0}:=\coprod_{i\in I}Y_{i}
and

Y_{n}:={\rm cosk}_{0}^{Y}(Y_{0})_{n}:=\underset{n~{\rm pieces}}{\underbrace{Y_{0}\times_{Y}\cdots\times_{Y}Y_{0}}}
.
Then we have the simplicial log scheme
Y_{\bullet}=Y_{\bullet\in{\mathbb{N}}}
over
Y
.
We also have the ringed topos

(({Y_{\bullet}/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}/S})
.
Let

{\pi}_{\rm conv}\colon({Y_{\bullet}/S})_{\rm conv}\longrightarrow({Y/S})_{\rm conv}

be the natural morphism of topoi.
The morphism

{\pi}_{\rm conv}
induces the following morphism

(7.0.1)

\pi_{\rm conv}\colon(({Y_{\bullet}/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}/S})\longrightarrow(({Y/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S})

of ringed topoi and a morphism of filtered derived categories:

(7.0.2)

R{\pi}_{{\rm conv}*}\colon{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}/S})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y/S}).

Assume that we are given another open covering

\{Y_{j}\}_{j\in J}
of
Y
.
Set
Y^{\prime}_{0}:=\coprod_{j\in J}Y_{j}

and

Y_{mn}:={\rm cosk}_{0}^{Y}(Y_{0})_{m}\times_{Y}{\rm cosk}_{0}^{Y}(Y^{\prime}_{0})_{n}
.
Then we have a bisimplicial log scheme

Y_{\bullet\bullet}
, a bisimplicial ringed topos

(({Y_{\bullet\bullet}/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet\bullet}/S})

and the following natural morphism of ringed topoi

(7.0.3)

\eta_{\rm conv}\colon(({Y_{\bullet\bullet}/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet\bullet}/S})\longrightarrow(({Y_{\bullet}/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}/S}).

We have the following two morphisms of
filtered derived categories:

(7.0.4)

R\eta_{{\rm conv}*}\colon{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet\bullet}/S})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}/S}),

(7.0.5)

R\eta_{m,{\rm conv}*}\colon{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y_{m\bullet}/S})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y_{m}/S})\quad(m\in{\mathbb{N}}).

By using the composite morphisms
f_{i}\colon\overset{\circ}{Y}_{i}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{Y}\overset{\overset{\circ}{f}}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{S}

(i\in I)
, we
also have an analogous simplicial ringed topos

({\overset{\circ}{Y}}_{\bullet{\rm zar}},f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal K}_{S}))
and
an analogous morphism

(7.0.6)

{\pi}_{\rm zar}\colon({\overset{\circ}{Y}}_{\bullet{\rm zar}},f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal K}_{S}))\longrightarrow({\overset{\circ}{Y}}_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))

of ringed topoi.
We have the following
three morphisms of filtered derived categories:

(7.0.7)

R{\pi}_{{\rm zar}*}\colon{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal K}_{S}))\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S})),

(7.0.8)

R\eta_{{\rm zar}*}\colon{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{\bullet\bullet}({\cal K}_{S}))\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal K}_{S})),

(7.0.9)

R\eta_{m,{\rm zar}*}\colon{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{m\bullet}({\cal K}_{S}))\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{m}({\cal K}_{S})).

Let
u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}\colon((Y/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S})\longrightarrow({\overset{\circ}{Y}}_{{\rm zar}},f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))

be the projection in (
2.0.3
).
We also have the following natural projections

(7.0.10)

u^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\bullet}/S}\colon(({Y_{\bullet}/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}/S})\longrightarrow({\overset{\circ}{Y}}_{\bullet{\rm zar}},f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal K}_{S})),

(7.0.11)

u^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\bullet\bullet}/S}\colon(({Y_{\bullet\bullet}/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet\bullet}/S})\longrightarrow({\overset{\circ}{Y}}_{\bullet\bullet{\rm zar}},f^{-1}_{\bullet\bullet}({\cal K}_{S})).

We have the following commutative diagrams:

(7.0.12)

\begin{CD}{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}/S})@>{R\pi_{\rm conv*}}>{}>{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y/S})\\
@V{Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\bullet}/S*}}V{}V@V{}V{Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}}V\\
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal K}_{S}))@>{R\pi_{\rm zar*}}>{}>{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))\end{CD}

and

(7.0.13)

\begin{CD}{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet\bullet}/S})@>{R\eta_{\rm conv*}}>{}>{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}/S})\\
@V{Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\bullet\bullet}/S*}}V{}V@V{}V{Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\bullet}/S*}}V\\
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{\bullet\bullet}({\cal K}_{S}))@>{R\eta_{\rm zar*}}>{}>{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal K}_{S})).\end{CD}

Assume that we are given the commutative diagram
(
2.0.4
) and open coverings

\{Y_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
Y
and

\{Y^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of

, respectively,
such that

induces a morphism

of log schemes.
Let

)
for

and

, respectively.

) for

and

, respectively.
Then the family
\{g_{i}\}_{i\in I}

induces the natural morphism

of simplicial log schemes over the morphism

.
Hence we obtain the following commutative diagram:

(7.0.14)

\begin{CD}{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}/S})@>{Rg_{\bullet{\rm conv}*}}>{}>{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y_{\bullet}^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})\\
@V{R{\pi}_{Y{\rm conv}*}}V{}V@V{}V{R{\pi}_{Y^{\prime}{\rm conv}*}}V\\
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y/S})@>{Rg_{{\rm conv}*}}>{}>{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}).\end{CD}

We also have the following commutative diagram

(7.0.15)

\begin{CD}{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal K}_{S}))@>{Rg_{\bullet{\rm zar}*}}>{}>{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{\prime}{}^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal K}_{S^{\prime}}))\\
@V{R{\pi}_{Y{\rm zar}*}}V{}V@V{}V{R{\pi}_{Y^{\prime}{\rm zar}*}}V\\
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))@>{Rg_{{\rm zar}*}}>{}>{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{\prime}{}^{-1}({\cal K}_{S^{\prime}})).\end{CD}

Let
M
be the log structure of

.
Let

be the sub log structure of

.

(resp.

(i\in I)
.

(7.0.17)

\epsilon_{{\rm conv}{\bullet\bullet}}\colon(({(Y_{\bullet\bullet},M_{\bullet\bullet})/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(Y_{\bullet\bullet},M_{\bullet\bullet})/S})\longrightarrow(({(Y_{\bullet\bullet},N_{\bullet\bullet})/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(Y_{\bullet\bullet},N_{\bullet\bullet})/S}).

We have the following commutative diagram

(7.0.18)

\begin{CD}(({(Y_{\bullet},M_{\bullet})/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(Y_{\bullet},M_{\bullet})/S})@>{\epsilon_{{\rm conv}\bullet}}>{}>(({(Y_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(Y_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})/S})\\

Here

and

are the morphisms of ringed topoi defined in (

);
we have written the symbols

and

in

We also have the following commutative diagram:

(7.0.19)

\begin{CD}(({(Y_{\bullet\bullet},M_{\bullet\bullet})/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(Y_{\bullet\bullet},M_{\bullet\bullet})/S})@>{\epsilon_{{\rm conv}\bullet\bullet}}>{}>(({(Y_{\bullet\bullet},N_{\bullet\bullet})/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(Y_{\bullet\bullet},N_{\bullet\bullet})/S})\\
@V{{\eta}_{M{\rm conv}}}V{}V@V{}V{{\eta}_{N{\rm conv}}}V\\
(({(Y_{\bullet},M_{\bullet})/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(Y_{\bullet},M_{\bullet})/S})@>{\epsilon_{{\rm conv}\bullet}}>{}>(({(Y_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(Y_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})/S})).\end{CD}

Here
{\eta}_{M{\rm conv}}

and
{\eta}_{N{\rm conv}}

are the morphisms of ringed topoi defined in (
7.0.3
).

Let

u^{\rm conv}_{(Y_{\bullet},L_{\bullet})/S}
and

be the projections in (

) and

) for
(Y,L)
, respectively.
We have the following equalities:

(7.0.20)

u^{\rm conv}_{(Y_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})/S}\circ\epsilon_{{\rm conv}\bullet}=u^{\rm conv}_{(Y_{\bullet},M_{\bullet})/S},\quad u^{\rm conv}_{(Y_{\bullet\bullet},N_{\bullet\bullet})/S}\circ\epsilon_{{\rm conv}\bullet\bullet}=u^{\rm conv}_{(Y_{\bullet\bullet},M_{\bullet\bullet})/S}

as morphisms of ringed topoi.

8
Weight-filtered convergent complexes and
p
-adic purity

Let
{\cal V}
,
\pi
,
S
and
S_{1}

be as in §
2
.
Let
f\colon(X,D\cup Z)\longrightarrow S_{1}

be a smooth scheme with a transversal relative SNCD
over
S
.
In this section we construct a filtered complex

(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S})
and prove
the
p
-adic purity (
1.1
).

Let
X=\bigcup_{i}X_{i}
be an affine open covering of
X
.
Set
D_{i}:=D|_{X_{i}}
,
Z_{i}:=Z|_{X_{i}}

and
(X_{0},D_{0}\cup Z_{0}):=(\coprod_{i}X_{i},\coprod_{i}(D_{i}\cup Z_{i}))
.
Set also

(X_{n},D_{n}\cup Z_{n}):={\rm cosk}_{0}^{X}(X_{0},D_{0}\cup Z_{0})_{n}

(n\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
Then we have the simplicial log scheme

(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})
over
S_{1}
.
Let

(8.0.1)

(X_{i},D_{i})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}`{\cal R}_{i}\quad{\rm and}\quad(X_{i},Z_{i})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal R}_{i}

be closed immersions into
formally log smooth log noetherian
p
-adic formal schemes
such that

\overset{\circ}{`{\cal R}}_{i}=\overset{\circ}{\cal R}_{i}
.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{\cal R}_{i}

is topologically of finite type over
S
.
Set
{\cal P}_{i}:=(\overset{\circ}{\cal R}_{i},M_{`{\cal R}_{i}}\oplus_{{\cal O}^{*}_{{\cal R}_{i}}}M_{{\cal R}_{i}})
.
Assume that
{\cal P}_{i}
is formally log smooth over
S
.
Then we have a natural closed immersion

(X_{i},D_{i}\cup Z_{i})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}_{i}
.
Set

`{\cal R}_{0}:=\coprod_{i}{`{\cal R}}_{i}
,

{\cal R}_{0}:=\coprod_{i}{\cal R}_{i}
,

{\cal P}_{0}:=\coprod_{i}{\cal P}_{i}
,

`{\cal R}_{\bullet}:={\rm cosk}_{0}^{S}(`{\cal R}_{0})
,

{\cal R}_{\bullet}:={\rm cosk}_{0}^{S}({\cal R}_{0})

and

{\cal P}_{\bullet}:={\rm cosk}_{0}^{S}({\cal P}_{0})
.
Then we have the following simplicial immersions

(8.0.2)

(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}`{\cal R}_{\bullet}\quad{\rm and}\quad(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal R}_{\bullet}

and the following commutative diagram:

(8.0.3)

\begin{CD}(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal R}_{\bullet}.\end{CD}

Note that

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}_{\bullet}=\overset{\circ}{\cal R}_{\bullet}
.
Let
(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

(resp.

(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal R}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}
)
be the exactification of
the the upper immersion (resp. the lower immersion)
in (
8.0.3
).
(Indeed, by the universality of the exactification,
we have the simplicial log schemes

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}
and
{\cal R}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}
.)
Let
(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(`{\cal R}_{\bullet})^{\rm ex}

be the exactification of the former immersion in (
8.0.2
).
Let
{\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

(resp.
{\cal N}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}
) be
the pull-back of the log structure of

(`{\cal R}_{\bullet})^{\rm ex}

(resp.
{\cal R}_{\bullet}^{\rm ex}
)
by the morphism
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow(`{\cal R}_{\bullet})^{\rm ex}

(resp.
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\cal R}_{\bullet}^{\rm ex}
).
Then we have the following equality:

(8.0.4)

M_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}={\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}\oplus_{{\cal O}^{*}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\cal N}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}.

Endow
\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

with the pull-back of the log structure of
{\cal R}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

and let
{\cal Q}_{\bullet}
be the resulting log scheme.
Then we have the natural simplicial exact immersion

(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}_{\bullet}

and we see that

{\cal Q}_{\bullet}={\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}=(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet},{\cal N}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})
.
We have the following commutative diagram

(8.0.5)

\begin{CD}(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}\\
@V{\epsilon_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}\\
\Big\|@V{}V{}V\\
(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal R}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}.\end{CD}

Let
\{(U_{\bullet n},T_{\bullet n})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be
the system of
the universal enlargements of the simplicial immersion

(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal R}_{\bullet}

over
S
.
Let
\beta_{\bullet n}\colon U_{\bullet n}\longrightarrow X_{\bullet}

be the natural morphism.
Identify
({T_{\bullet n}})_{\rm zar}

with
({U_{\bullet n}})_{\rm zar}
.
We have the following filtered complexes by (
6.7
):

\displaystyle(L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}),P^{D_{\bullet}}):=(L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}),\{L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S})\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}})

and

\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{\bullet n*}({\cal K}_{T_{\bullet n}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S},P^{D_{\bullet}}):=\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{\bullet n*}({\cal K}_{T_{\bullet n}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S},\{{\cal K}_{T_{\bullet n}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}).

Let
\pi_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}}

and
\pi_{\rm zar}

be the morphisms of ringed topoi in
(
7.0.1
) and (
7.0.6
), respectively,
for
Y=(X,Z)
.
We set

(8.0.6)

(C_{\rm conv}({\cal O}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}):=R\pi_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}((L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}),P^{D_{\bullet}}))

and

(8.0.7)

\displaystyle(C_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})

\displaystyle:=Ru^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S*}(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})

\displaystyle=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(\varprojlim_{n*}\beta_{\bullet n*}({\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{T}_{\bullet n}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S},P^{D_{\bullet}})).

Here we have used (
7.0.12
) and (
6.12.1
).
Let

a^{(k)}_{\bullet}\colon(D^{(k)}_{\bullet},Z|_{D^{(k)}_{\bullet}})\longrightarrow(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})

be the natural morphism of ringed topoi.

The following lemma has been proved in
[
NS1
, (1.3.4)]
:

Lemma 8.1

(
[
Nak2
, (1.3.4)]
)
.

For a morphism
f\colon({\cal T},{\cal A})\longrightarrow({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})
of ringed topoi,
the following diagram is commutative
:

(8.1.1)

\begin{CD}{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal A})@>{{\rm gr}_{k}}>{}>D^{+}({\cal A})\\
@V{Rf_{*}}V{}V@V{}V{Rf_{*}}V\\
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal A}^{\prime})@>{{\rm gr}_{k}}>{}>D^{+}({\cal A}^{\prime}).\end{CD}

Lemma 8.2

(
cf.
[
Nak2
, (4.14)]
)
.

Let
k
be a nonnegative integer. Then the following hold
:

(1)
For the morphism

g\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{n}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{n^{\prime}}

corresponding to a morphism
[n^{\prime}]\longrightarrow[n]
in
\Delta
,
the assumption in
(
6.3
)
is satisfied
for
Y=(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{n},{\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{n})

and

Y^{\prime}=(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{n^{\prime}},{\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{n^{\prime}})
.
Consequently there exists a morphism

g^{(k)}\colon D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{n})\longrightarrow D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{n^{\prime}})

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})
fitting into
the commutative diagram
(
6.3.1
)

for
Y=(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{n},{\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{n})
and

Y^{\prime}=(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{n^{\prime}},{\cal M}{}^{\rm ex}_{n^{\prime}})
.

(2)

The family

\{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{n})\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}

gives a simplicial scheme
D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})

with natural morphism

b^{(k)}_{\bullet}\colon D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

of simplicial schemes.

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of
[
Nak2
, (4.14)]
.
∎

The following is a key lemma for
(
8.4
) below:

Lemma 8.3
.

(8.3.1)

{\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{D}}C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})=a^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k){\rm log}}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))[-k].

Proof.

Let
a_{\bullet}\colon(D^{(k)}_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet}|_{D^{(k)}_{\bullet}})\longrightarrow(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})
be the natural morphism of
simplicial log schemes.
Let
L^{(k){\rm conv}}_{\bullet}

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})

be the log convergent linearization functor for
coherent

{\cal K}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})}
-modules.
Then, by (
8.2
) (2), (
6.9.1
) and (
6.13
),
we have the following isomorphism
in
D^{+}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S})
:

(8.3.2)

{\rm Res}:{\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{D_{\bullet}}}L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}a^{(k)\log}_{\bullet{\rm conv}*}(L^{(k){\rm conv}}_{\bullet}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(D_{\bullet}/S;Z_{\bullet}))[-k].

Hence, by (
8.1
), (
6.10.1
)
and the cohomological descent,
we obtain (
8.3.1
) as follows:

(8.3.3)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{D}}C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})

\displaystyle=R\pi^{\rm log}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}{\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{D_{\bullet}}}(L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})

\displaystyle=R\pi^{\rm log}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}a^{(k)\log}_{\bullet{\rm conv}*}(L^{(k){\rm conv}}_{\bullet}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(D_{\bullet}/S;Z_{\bullet}))[-k]

\displaystyle=R\pi^{\rm log}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}a^{(k)\log}_{{\bullet}{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(k)}_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet}|_{{D^{(k)}_{\bullet}}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}}(D_{\bullet}/S;Z_{\bullet}))[-k]

\displaystyle=a^{(k)\log}_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{{D^{(k)}}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k){\rm log}}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))[-k].

∎

Theorem 8.4
.

The filtered complexes

(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S})

and

(C_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))

are independent of the choice of the open covering of
X

and the immersions in
(
8.0.2
)
.

Proof.

(Though we can give the same proof of
(
8.4
) as that of
[
NS1
, (2.5.3), (2.5.4)]
,
we give a shorter proof of it here.)

Assume that we are given the immersions in (
8.0.2
).
Let
X^{\prime}_{0}
be the disjoint union of
another affine open covering of
X
.
Set
(X^{\prime}_{\bullet},D^{\prime}_{\bullet}):={\rm cosk}_{0}^{(X,D)}(X^{\prime}_{0},D^{\prime}_{0})

and
(X^{\prime}_{\bullet},Z^{\prime}_{\bullet}):={\rm cosk}_{0}^{(X,Z)}(X^{\prime}_{0},Z^{\prime}_{0})
.
Assume that we are given other immersions

(8.4.1)

(X^{\prime}_{\bullet},D^{\prime}_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}`{\cal R}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\quad{\rm and}\quad(X^{\prime}_{\bullet},Z^{\prime}_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal R}^{\prime}_{\bullet}

which are analogous to (
8.0.2
).
Let

(8.4.2)

\begin{CD}(X^{\prime}_{\bullet},D^{\prime}_{\bullet}\cup Z^{\prime}_{\bullet})@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
(X^{\prime}_{\bullet},Z^{\prime}_{\bullet})@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal R}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram of simplicial log schemes
which is analogous to (
8.0.3
).
Set
(X_{mn},D_{mn}\cup Z_{mn}):={\rm cosk}_{0}^{X}(X_{0},D_{0}\cup Z_{0})_{m}\times_{X}{\rm cosk}_{0}^{X}(X^{\prime}_{0},D^{\prime}_{0}\cup Z^{\prime}_{0})_{n}

(m,n\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
Then we have the natural bisimplicial log schemes

(X_{\bullet\bullet},D_{\bullet\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet\bullet})

with morphisms

(X_{\bullet\bullet},D_{\bullet\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet\bullet})\longrightarrow(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})
and

(X_{\bullet\bullet},D_{\bullet\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet\bullet})\longrightarrow(X^{\prime}_{\bullet},D^{\prime}_{\bullet}\cup Z^{\prime}_{\bullet})

with natural morphisms.
Set

{\cal P}_{\bullet\bullet}:={\cal P}_{\bullet}\times_{S}{\cal P}^{\prime}_{\bullet}

and
{\cal R}_{\bullet\bullet}:={\cal R}_{\bullet}\times_{S}{\cal R}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
.
Then we have the following diagram

(8.4.3)

\begin{CD}(X_{\bullet\bullet},D_{\bullet\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet\bullet})@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}_{\bullet\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
(X_{\bullet\bullet},Z_{\bullet\bullet})@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal R}_{\bullet\bullet}\end{CD}

such that there exist two natural morphisms
from this commutative diagram to
(
8.0.3
) and (
8.4.2
).
Let
{\cal Q}_{\bullet\bullet}={\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\bullet}

be the bisimplicial version of

{\cal Q}_{\bullet}={\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}
.
Let
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\bullet}

be the exactification of the immersion

(X_{\bullet\bullet},D_{\bullet\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}_{\bullet\bullet}
.
Then there exists an fs bicosimplicial sub log structure

{\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\bullet}
of

M_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\bullet}}

such that

(8.4.4)

M_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\bullet}}={\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\bullet}\oplus_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\bullet}}^{*}}M_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\bullet}},

such that the immersion

(X_{\bullet\bullet},D_{\bullet\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}_{\bullet\bullet}

induces an immersion

(X_{\bullet\bullet},D_{\bullet\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\bullet},{\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\bullet})
.
Let

R\eta_{{\rm conv}*}\colon{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet\bullet},Z_{\bullet\bullet})/S})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S})

be the morphism (
7.0.4
).
Then we have only to prove that

(8.4.5)

\displaystyle(L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),P^{D_{\bullet}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}R\eta_{{\rm conv}*}((L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet\bullet},Z_{\bullet\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),P^{D_{\bullet\bullet}})).

Let

R\eta_{m,{\rm conv}*}\colon{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X_{m\bullet},Z_{m\bullet})/S})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X_{m},Z_{m})/S})\quad(m\in{\mathbb{N}})

be the induced morphism by

R\eta_{{\rm conv}*}
.
It is well-known that there exists
a filtered flasque resolution of

(I^{\bullet\bullet\bullet},P)
of

(L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet\bullet},Z_{\bullet\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),P^{D_{\bullet\bullet}})

such that
(I^{m\bullet\bullet},P)
is a
filtered flasque resolution of

(L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{m\bullet},Z_{m\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{m\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),P^{D_{m\bullet}})

for each
m\in{\mathbb{N}}

(cf.
[
NS1
, (1.5.0.4)]
).
Here the third degree of
(I^{\bullet\bullet\bullet},P)

is the complex degree.
Hence, to prove (
8.4.5
),
we have only to prove that the morphism

(8.4.6)

\displaystyle(L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{m},Z_{m})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{m}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),P^{D_{m}}){\longrightarrow}R\eta_{m,{\rm conv}*}((L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{m\bullet},Z_{m\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{m\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),P^{D_{m\bullet}}))

is a filtered isomorphism.

Let
a^{(k)}_{\bullet\bullet}\colon(D^{(k)}_{\bullet\bullet},Z_{\bullet\bullet}|_{D^{(k)}_{\bullet\bullet}})\longrightarrow(X_{\bullet\bullet},Z_{\bullet\bullet})
be the natural morphism
of log schemes.
By (
8.3
) we have the following isomorphism

(8.4.7)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P^{D_{m}}}_{k}L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{m},Z_{m})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{m}/S})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}a^{(k)\log}_{m,{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(k)}_{m},Z|_{D^{(k)}_{m}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}}(D_{m}/S;Z_{m}))[-k].

On the other hand,
by the proof of (
8.2
) (1),
we have the simplicial scheme

D^{(k)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{m\bullet})

(m\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
As in (
8.3.3
), we have the following formula

(8.4.8)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P^{D_{m}}}_{k}R\eta_{m,{\rm conv}*}(L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{m\bullet},Z_{m\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{m\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}))

\displaystyle=a^{(k)\log}_{m,{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(k)}_{m},Z|_{D^{(k)}_{m}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}}(D_{m}/S;Z_{m}))[-k]

by replacing
R\pi_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}

and
\bullet
with
R\eta_{m,{\rm conv}*}

and
m\bullet
, respectively.
Hence the morphism (
8.4.6
) is
a filtered isomorphism.
∎

Definition 8.5
.

We call

(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})

(resp.

(C_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})
)
the
weight-filtered convergent complex

(resp. the
weight-filtered isozariskian complex
)
of
{\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}

with respect to

D
.

Proposition 8.6
.

There exists a canonical isomorphism

(8.6.1)

C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})\overset{\sim}{\longleftarrow}R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}).

Proof.

Let

(8.6.2)

\pi_{{\rm conv}}\colon(({(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S})\longrightarrow(({(X,D\cup Z)/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})

be the natural morphism in (
7.0.1
).
We have the following equalities:

\displaystyle C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})

\displaystyle=R\pi^{\rm log}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}(L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})

\displaystyle=R\pi^{\rm log}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S*}(L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}))

\displaystyle=R\pi^{\rm log}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S*}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S})

\displaystyle=R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S*}R\pi^{\rm log}_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S})

\displaystyle=R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}).

Here the first equality (resp. the second one,
the third one, the fourth one, the fifth one) follows
from the definition of

C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})

(resp. (
5.8.1
),
(
3.7
), (
7.0.18
),
the cohomological descent).
∎

Corollary 8.7
.

There exists the following spectral sequence

(8.7.1)

E^{-k,h+k}_{1}:=R^{h-k}f^{\rm conv}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k){\rm log}}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))\Longrightarrow

R^{h}f^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}).

Proof.

(
8.7
) immediately follows from
(
8.6
) and (
8.3.1
).
∎

Theorem 8.8

(
p
-adic purity, cf.
[
NS1
, (2.7.1)]
).

Let
k
be a nonnegative integer. Then

(8.8.1)

R^{k}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})=a^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k){\rm log}}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)).

Proof.

The “increasing filtration”

\{P^{D}_{k}C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

on
C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})
gives
the following spectral sequence

(8.8.2)

E_{1}^{-k,h+k}={\cal H}^{h}({\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{D}}C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}))\Longrightarrow{\cal H}^{h}(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})).

By (
8.6.1
)

{\cal H}^{h}(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}))=R^{h}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})

and by (
8.3.1
) we have the following equality:

(8.8.3)

{\cal H}^{h}({\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{D}}C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}))={\cal H}^{h-k}(a^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k){\rm log}}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))).

Hence
E_{1}^{-k,h+k}=0
for
h\not=k
.
Now we obtain (
8.8.1
) by the following equalities:

\displaystyle R^{k}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})

\displaystyle={\cal H}^{k}(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}))=E_{1}^{-k,2k}

\displaystyle=a^{(k)}_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k){\rm log}}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)).

∎

Remark 8.9
.

In this remark, we show (
1.3.2
) in general
as in
[
B1
, VI Lemme 1.2.2]
.

Let
Y
be a scheme over
S_{1}

and let
a
be a global section
of
\Gamma(Y,{\cal O}_{Y})
. Let
U
be the largest open subscheme of

Y
such that
a
is invertible on
U
and let

j\colon U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}Y
be the open immersion.
Then we would like to prove that,
for a coherent
{\cal K}_{U/S}
-module
E
,

(8.9.1)

R^{k}j_{{\rm conv}*}(E)=0\quad(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}).

Indeed, let
(V,T,\iota,u)
be an object of
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
.
Assume that
T
is affine.
Let
\widetilde{u^{*}(a)}\in\Gamma(T,{\cal O}_{T})
be a lift of
u^{*}(a)
.
Set
V_{U}:=V\times_{Y}U
and consider the open subscheme

T|_{V_{U}}
of
T
.
Then
T|_{V_{U}}
is the largest open subscheme of

T
such that
\widetilde{u^{*}(a)}

is invertible on
T|_{V_{U}}
.
Let
j_{T}\colon T|_{V_{U}}\longrightarrow T
be the open immersion.
Then

R^{k}j_{T*}(E_{(V_{U},T|_{V_{U}},\iota|_{V_{U}},u|_{V_{U}})})=0

for
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
.
As in [loc. cit., VI Lemme 1.2.1],

R^{k}j_{{\rm conv}*}(E)_{(V,T,\iota,u)}=R^{k}j_{T*}(E_{(V_{U},T|_{V_{U}},\iota|_{V_{U}},u|_{V_{U}})})
.
Consequently we obtain (
8.9.1
).

By the Leray spectral sequence for the functor

\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S}\colon({(X,D\cup Z)/S})_{\rm conv}\longrightarrow({(X,Z)/S})_{\rm conv}
and

f^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}\colon({(X,Z)/S})_{\rm conv}\longrightarrow{S}_{\rm zar}
,
we obtain the following spectral sequence

(8.9.2)

E^{st}_{2}:=R^{s}f^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S*}R^{t}\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})\Longrightarrow

R^{s+t}f^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}).

By (
8.8
),
(
8.9.2
) is equal to
the following spectral sequence

(8.9.3)

E^{st}_{2}=R^{s}f^{\rm conv}_{(D^{(t)},Z|_{D^{(t)}})/S*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(t)},Z|_{D^{(t)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(t)}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))\Longrightarrow

R^{s+t}f^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),

which is equal to (
8.7.1
) by renumbering
the
E_{2}
-term
E^{k,h-k}_{2}
by
E_{1}^{-k,h+k}
.

Now we give another description of

(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})
.
We need the following lemma whose proof is easy:

Lemma 8.10

(
[
NS1
, (2.7.2)]
).

Let
\tau
be the canonical filtration on a complex.
Let

f\colon({\cal T},{\cal A})\longrightarrow({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})

be a morphism of ringed topoi.
Then, for an object
E^{\bullet}
in

D^{+}({\cal A})
,
there exists a canonical morphism

(8.10.1)

(Rf_{*}(E^{\bullet}),\tau)\longrightarrow Rf_{*}((E^{\bullet},\tau))

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal A}^{\prime}).

Theorem 8.11

(
Comparison theorem).

Set

(E_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}):=(R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),\tau).

Then there exists a
canonical isomorphism

(8.11.1)

(E_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}).

In particular,

(8.11.2)

(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),\tau)=(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}).

Proof.

(The proof is the same as that of
[
NS1
, (2.7.3)]
.)
Consider the commutative diagram (
8.0.3
).
Then, as usual, there exists the following natural morphism
of filtered complexes of

{\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}
-modules:

(8.11.3)

(L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),\tau)\longrightarrow(L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),P^{D_{\bullet}}).

By (
8.10
) there exists a canonical morphism

(8.11.4)

(R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),\tau)\longrightarrow

R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}((L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),\tau)).

By composing (
8.11.4
) with the morphism

R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}((\ref{eqn:ltpd}))
,
we obtain the following morphism

(8.11.5)

(R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),\tau)\longrightarrow

R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}((L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),P^{D_{\bullet}}))

which is nothing but a morphism

(8.11.6)

(R\epsilon_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),\tau)\longrightarrow(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})

by (
5.8
).
To prove that the morphism (
8.11.6
) is a
filtered isomorphism,
it suffices to prove that the induced morphism

(8.11.7)

{\rm gr}_{k}^{\tau}R\epsilon_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})\longrightarrow{\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{D}}C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})\quad(k\in{\mathbb{Z}})

is an isomorphism.
By (
8.8
) we have the following equalities:

(8.11.8)

\displaystyle{\cal H}^{h}({\rm gr}_{k}^{\tau}R\epsilon_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}))

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}R^{k}\epsilon_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})&(h=k),\\
0&(h\not=k)\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a^{(k){\log}}_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k){\rm log}}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))&(h=k),\\
0&(h\not=k).\end{cases}

By (
8.8.3
),

{\cal H}^{h}({\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{D}}C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}))

is also equal to the last formulas in (
8.11.8
).
Hence the morphism (
8.11.6
)
is an isomorphism.

The rest we have to show is that
the morphism (
8.11.6
) is independent of
the choice of the commutative diagram (
8.0.3
).
Let the notations be as in the proof of (
8.4
).
By the cohomological descent we have the following commutative diagram:

(8.11.9)

\begin{CD}(R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),\tau)@>{}>{}>\\
@V{}V{}V\\
(R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}R\eta_{{\rm conv}*}L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet\bullet},Z_{\bullet\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),\tau)@>{}>{}>\end{CD}

\begin{CD}(R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),P^{D_{\bullet}})\\
\Big\|\\
R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}R\eta_{{\rm conv}*}(L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet\bullet},Z_{\bullet\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),P^{D_{\bullet\bullet}}).\end{CD}

Here we obtain the equality above by (
8.4.5
).
We see that, by the proof above,
the horizontal morphisms are isomorphisms.
Hence the vertical morphism above is an isomorphism.
We have proved the desired independence.
∎

Definition 8.12
.

We call

(E_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})

the
weight-filtered vanishing cycle convergent complex

of
(X,D\cup Z)/S

with respect to

D
.

Let
\pi^{\prime}
be another non-zero element of
the maximal ideal of
{\cal V}
.
Assume that
\pi{\cal V}\subset\pi^{\prime}{\cal V}
.
Set
S^{\prime}_{1}:=\underline{\rm Spec}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}/\pi^{\prime})
.
Then we have a natural closed immersion

S^{\prime}_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S_{1}
.
Set
(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime}):=(X,D\cup Z)\times_{S_{1}}S^{\prime}_{1}
.
Let
i\colon(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(X,D\cup Z)

and
i^{Z}\colon(X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(X,Z)

be the natural closed immersions.

Because
i^{Z}_{{\rm conv}*}
is exact ((
2.1
) (1)),
we have the following functor:

(8.12.1)

i^{Z}_{{\rm conv}*}\colon{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})/S})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}).

Proposition 8.13
.

(8.13.1)

i^{Z}_{{\rm conv}*}((E_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S}),P^{D^{\prime}}))=(E_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}).

Proof.

We have the following equalities:

\displaystyle i^{Z}_{{\rm conv}*}((E_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S}),P^{D^{\prime}}))

\displaystyle=i^{Z}_{{\rm conv}*}(R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime},Z^{\prime})/S*}({\cal K}_{(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S}),\tau)

\displaystyle=(R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S*}i_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S}),\tau)

\displaystyle=(R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),\tau)

\displaystyle=(E_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}).

Here the third equality follows from (
2.1.5
).
∎

Corollary 8.14
.

(1)

(8.14.1)

i^{Z}_{{\rm conv}*}((C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S}),P^{D^{\prime}}))=(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}).

(2)

(8.14.2)

\overset{\circ}{i}{}^{Z}_{*}((C_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S}),P^{D^{\prime}}))=(C_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}).

Proof.

(
8.14.1
) follows from (
8.11.1
)
and (
8.13.1
).
(
8.14.2
) follows from (
8.14.1
).
∎

The following has an application for the unipotent
\pi_{1}

as in
[
KH
]
:

Theorem 8.15
.

Let
{\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S})

be the category of
filtered positively-graded graded commutative dga’s
of
{\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}
-algebras
and let
{\rm D}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}))

be the localized category of

{\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S})

inverting the weakly equivalent isomorphisms
in
{\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S})

(
[
GM
, p. 145, p. 334]
)
.
Let

U_{\rm conv}\colon{\rm D}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}))\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S})
be
the natural forgetful functor.
Then there exists
a functorial filtered object

(\widetilde{C},\widetilde{P})\in{\rm D}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}))

such that

U_{\rm conv}((\widetilde{C},\widetilde{P}))\simeq(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})
.

Proof.

Let the notations be as in the beginning of this section.
Let
(X_{\bullet^{\prime}},Z_{\bullet^{\prime}})
be the constant simplicial
scheme defined by
(X,Z)
.
Let

R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv,TW}\bullet^{\prime}*}\colon{\rm D}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}))\longrightarrow{\rm D}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet^{\prime}},Z_{\bullet^{\prime}})/S}))

be the derived direct image of Thom-Whitney in

[
Nav
, §4]
.
Let

{\bf s}_{\rm TW}\colon{\rm D}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet^{\prime}},Z_{\bullet^{\prime}})/S}))\longrightarrow{\rm D}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}))

be the single complex of Thom-Whitney in [loc. cit., §3].
Then, by [loc. cit., (2.6)], we have only to set

(\widetilde{C},\widetilde{P}):={\bf s}_{\rm TW}R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv,TW}\bullet^{\prime}*}((L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}})).

∎

9
The functoriality of the weight-filtered
convergent and isozariskian complexes

Let
{\cal V}
,
K
and
\pi
be as in §
2

and let
S
,
S_{1}
and
(X,D\cup Z)

be as in §
6
.
In this section we prove the contravariant functoriality of

(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})

and
(C_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})

by using (
8.11
).

Let
{\cal V}^{\prime}
,
K^{\prime}
,
\pi^{\prime}
,
S^{\prime}
and
S^{\prime}_{1}

be analogous objects to

{\cal V}
,
K
,
\pi
,
S
and
S_{1}
, respectively.
Let

(9.0.1)

\begin{CD}S@>{u}>{}>S^{\prime}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\rm Spf}({\cal V})@>{}>{}>{\rm Spf}({\cal V}^{\prime})\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram of log
p
-adic

{\cal V}
-formal and log
p
-adic
{\cal V}^{\prime}
-formal schemes.
We assume that
u

induces a morphism
u_{1}\colon S_{1}\longrightarrow S^{\prime}_{1}

of schemes.
Let
(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})

be a smooth scheme
with transversal relative SNCD’s
over
S^{\prime}_{1}
. Let

(9.0.2)

\begin{CD}(X,D\cup Z)@>{g}>{}>(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{1}@>{u_{1}}>{}>S^{\prime}_{1}\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram of log schemes.
Assume that the morphism
g
induces

g_{(X,D)}\colon(X,D)\longrightarrow(X^{\prime},D^{\prime})
and

g_{(X,Z)}\colon(X,Z)\longrightarrow(X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})

.
Let

\epsilon_{\rm conv}\colon({(X,D\cup Z)/S})_{\rm conv}\longrightarrow({(X,Z)/S})_{\rm conv}

and

\epsilon^{\prime}_{\rm conv}\colon({(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}})_{\rm conv}\longrightarrow({(X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}})_{\rm conv}

be the
morphism of topoi
forgetting the log structure along
D
and
D^{\prime}
,
respectively.
Let

g_{{\rm conv}}\colon(({(X,D\cup Z)/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})\longrightarrow(({(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}})

be the morphism of log convergent ringed topoi induced by

.

Theorem 9.1

(
Functoriality).

Let the notations be as above.
Then the following hold
:

(1)
There exists a canonical
morphism

(9.1.1)

g^{*}_{(X,Z){\rm conv}}\colon(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}}),P^{D^{\prime}})\longrightarrow Rg_{(X,Z){\rm conv}*}(C_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}).

(2)
There exists a canonical
morphism

(9.1.2)

g_{\rm zar}^{*}\colon(C_{{\rm isozar}}({\cal K}_{(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}}),P^{D^{\prime}})\longrightarrow Rg_{{\rm zar}*}(C_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}).

Proof.

(1): (1) immediately sfrom (
8.11
).

(2): (2) immediately follows from (1) and the first equality in (
8.0.7
).
∎

Corollary 9.2
.

Let
E_{\rm ss}((X,D\cup Z)/S)

(
resp.
E_{\rm ss}((X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}))

be the spectral sequence
(
8.7.1
)

(
resp.
(
8.7.1
)
for

(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime})
.
Then the morphism
g^{*}_{\rm conv}

induces a morphism

(9.2.1)

g^{*}_{\rm conv}\colon u^{-1}E_{\rm ss}((X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime})\longrightarrow E_{\rm ss}((X,D\cup Z)/S)

of spectral sequences.

Proof.

The proof is straightforward.
∎

Let

a^{\prime}{}^{(k)}\colon(D^{\prime}{}^{(k)},Z^{\prime}|_{D^{\prime}{}^{(k)}})\longrightarrow(X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})

be the natural morphism.
By (
8.3.1
) and (
9.1
),
the morphism

g^{*}_{(X,Z){\rm conv}}

induces the following morphism

(9.2.2)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P^{D}}_{k}(g^{*}_{(X,Z){\rm conv}})\colon Ru_{(X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}*}a^{\prime}{}^{(k){\log}}_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal O}_{(D^{\prime}{}^{(k)},Z^{\prime}|_{D^{\prime}{}^{(k)}})/S^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(D^{\prime}/S^{\prime};Z^{\prime}))[-k]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow Ru_{(X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}*}a^{\prime}{}^{(k){\log}}_{{\rm conv}*}Rg_{D^{(k)}{\rm conv}*}({\cal O}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)\log}_{\rm conv}(D/S;Z))[-k].

Assume that
g
induces a morphism

g_{D^{(k)}}\colon(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})\longrightarrow(D^{\prime}{}^{(k)},Z^{\prime}|_{D^{\prime}{}^{(k)}})
for any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
In the following, we make the morphism

{\rm gr}^{P^{D}}_{k}(g^{*}_{(X,Z){\rm conv}})
in
(
9.2.2
) explicit in certain cases.

Assume that the following two conditions hold:

(9.2.3): there exists the same cardinality of
smooth components of
D
and
D^{\prime}
over

S_{1}
and

, respectively:

and

, respectively.

(9.2.4):
there exist positive integers

(\lambda\in\Lambda)

.

Set

Let

and

be the natural exact closed immersions.
Consider the following
direct factor of the morphism (

):

(9.2.5)

\displaystyle Ru_{(X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}*}(g^{*}_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}})\colon Ru_{(X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}*}a^{\prime}{}^{{\log}}_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}*}({\cal O}_{(D^{\prime}_{\underline{\lambda}},Z^{\prime}|_{D^{\prime}_{\underline{\lambda}}})/S^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(D^{\prime}/S^{\prime};Z^{\prime}))[-k]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow Ru_{(X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}*}a^{\prime}{}^{{\log}}_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}*}Rg_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}*}({\cal O}_{(D_{\underline{\lambda}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))[-k],

where

are

as defined in

, p. 81]
.

Proposition 9.3
.

Let the notations
and the assumptions be as above.
Let

g_{(D_{\underline{\lambda}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}}})}\colon(D_{\underline{\lambda}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}}})\longrightarrow(D^{\prime}_{\underline{\lambda}},Z^{\prime}|_{D^{\prime}_{\underline{\lambda}}})

.

in

)

(\prod_{j=1}^{k}e_{\lambda_{j}})Ru_{(X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}*}(a^{\prime}{}^{{\log}}_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}*}g^{*}_{(D_{\underline{\lambda}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}}}){\rm conv}})
for

for

Proof.

Definition 9.4
.

(1) We call

\{e_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{>0})^{\Lambda}

the
multi-degree
of
g

with respect to the decompositions of

\{D_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda}

and
\{D^{\prime}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda}
of
D
and
D^{\prime}

(
[
NS1
, (2.1.8)]
), respectively.
We denote it by

{\rm deg}_{D,D^{\prime}}(g)\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{>0})^{\Lambda}
.
If
e_{\lambda}
’s for all

\lambda
’s
are equal,
we also denote

e_{\lambda}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

by

{\rm deg}_{D,D^{\prime}}(g)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
.

(2) Assume that

e_{\lambda}
’s for all
\lambda
’s are equal.
Let
v\colon{\cal E}\longrightarrow{\cal F}

be a morphism of

{\cal K}_{S}
-modules.
Let
k
be a nonnegative integer.
The
k
-
twist

v(-k)\colon{\cal E}(-k;g;D,D^{\prime})\longrightarrow{\cal F}(-k;g;D,D^{\prime})

of
v
with respect to
g

is, by definition, the morphism

{\rm deg}_{D,D^{\prime}}(g)^{k}v\colon{\cal E}\longrightarrow{\cal F}
.

Corollary 9.5
.

Assume that
e_{\lambda}
’s for
all
\lambda
’s are equal.
Let
E_{\rm ss}((X,D\cup Z)/S)
be

E_{1}^{-k,h+k}((X,D\cup Z)/S)=R^{h-k}f^{\rm conv}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k){\rm log}}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))(-k;g;D,D^{\prime})

\Longrightarrow R^{h}f^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})

and let

be the obvious

.
Then there exists a morphism

(9.5.1)

g^{*}_{\rm conv}\colon u^{-1}E_{\rm ss}((X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime})\longrightarrow E_{\rm ss}((X,D\cup Z)/S)

of spectral sequences which induces the morphism

g^{*}_{\rm conv}\colon R^{h}f^{\rm conv}_{(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}*}({\cal K}_{(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}})\longrightarrow R^{h}f^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})
.

Proof.

(
9.5
) immediately follows
from (
9.3
).
∎

Let
F_{S_{1}}\colon S_{1}\longrightarrow S_{1}
be
the absolute Frobenius endomorphism of
S_{1}
.
Set

(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime}):=(X,D\cup Z)\times_{S_{1},F_{S_{1}}}S_{1}
.
The relative Frobenius morphism

F\colon(X,D\cup Z)\longrightarrow(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})

over
S_{1}
induces the relative
Frobenius morphisms

and

F^{(k)}\colon(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})\longrightarrow(D^{(k)^{\prime}},Z^{\prime}|_{D^{(k)^{\prime}}})
.
Let
a^{(k)}\colon(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})\longrightarrow(X,D\cup Z)

and

a^{(k)^{\prime}}\colon(D^{(k)^{\prime}},Z^{\prime}|_{D^{(k)^{\prime}}})\longrightarrow(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})

be the natural morphisms.
We define the
relative Frobenius action

\Phi_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S}\colon a^{(k)^{\prime}{\log}}_{{\rm conv}*}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(D^{\prime}/S;Z^{\prime})\longrightarrow F_{{\rm conv}*}a^{(k){\log}}_{{\rm conv}*}\varpi^{(k){\rm log}}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)

as the identity
under the natural identification

\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(D^{\prime}/S^{\prime};Z^{\prime})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}F^{(k){\log}}_{{\rm conv}*}\varpi^{(k){\rm log}}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z).

When
g
in (
9.0.2
)
is equal to the relative Frobenius

F\colon(X,D\cup Z)\longrightarrow(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})
,
we denote (
8.7.1
)+(the compatibility with Frobenius)
by the following:

(9.5.2)

\begin{split}E_{1}^{-k,h+k}((X,D\cup Z)/S)=&R^{h-k}f^{\rm conv}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S}\\
{}&\phantom{R^{h-k}f^{\rm conv}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S*}(}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k){\rm log}}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)))(-k)\\
\Longrightarrow{}&R^{h}f^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}).\end{split}

Definition 9.6
.

We call the sequence (
9.5.2
)
the
weight spectral sequence of

(X,D\cup Z)/S
(more precisely over
(S_{1},S)
)

with respect to

D
. If
Z=\emptyset
, then
we call it
the
weight spectral sequence of

(X,D)/S
.

By (
8.9.2
) we also
have the following Leray spectral sequence

(9.6.1)

E^{st}_{2}:=R^{s}f^{\rm conv}_{(D^{(t)},Z|_{D^{(t)}})/S*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(t)},Z|_{D^{(t)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(t)}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))(-t)

\Longrightarrow R^{s+t}f^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}).

.

Let the notations be as in
(
8.13
)
.

(

)
for

.

10
Boundary morphisms

In this section we define the log cycle class
in a log convergent cohomology for a smooth divisor which
intersects a log locus transversally
(cf.
[
Fa
, §2]
,
[
NS1
, (2.8)]
).
Then, as in
[
NS1
, (2.8)]
, we give a
description of the boundary morphism between
the
E_{1}
-terms of the spectral sequence
(
9.5.2
). Because the proof is the same as that of
[
NS1
, (2.8)]
, we omit the proofs in this section.

Let
S
and
S_{1}
be as in §
6
.
Let
f\colon(X,Z)\longrightarrow S_{1}

be a smooth scheme with a relative SNCD over
S_{1}
.
By abuse of notation,
we also denote by
f
the composite
morphism
(X,Z)\longrightarrow S_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S
.
Let
D
be
a smooth divisor on
X
which intersects

Z
transversally over
S_{1}
.
Let

a\colon(D,Z|_{D})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(X,Z)
be
the natural
closed immersion over
S_{1}
.
Let

a_{\rm zar}\colon D_{\rm zar}\longrightarrow X_{\rm zar}

be the induced morphism of Zariski topoi.
Let
a_{\rm conv}\colon(({(D,Z|_{D})/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(D,Z|_{D})/S})\longrightarrow(({(X,Z)/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S})

be also the induced morphism of
log convergent ringed topoi.

Let the notations be as
in the beginning of §
8
for the log scheme

(X,D\cup Z)
above.
Let
b_{\bullet}\colon(D^{(1)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}),{\cal N}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}_{\bullet}

be the natural simplicial immersion.
By using the Poincaré residue isomorphism
with respect to
D^{(1)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})
,
we have the following exact sequence:

(10.0.1)

0\longrightarrow\Omega_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}^{\bullet}\longrightarrow\Omega_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}^{\bullet}\overset{\rm Res}{\longrightarrow}b_{{\bullet}{\rm zar}*}(\Omega_{D^{(1)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})/S}^{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{\rm zar}(D^{(1)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})/S))[-1]\longrightarrow 0.

Let
L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}

(resp.
L^{\rm conv}_{(D_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet}|_{D_{\bullet}})/S}
)
be the log convergent linearization functor
with respect to the simplicial immersion

(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}_{\bullet}

(resp.
(D_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet}|_{D_{\bullet}})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(D^{(1)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}),{\cal N}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})
).
By (
4.5
),

L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}b_{\bullet{\rm zar}*}=a_{{\rm conv}\bullet*}L^{\rm conv}_{(D_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet}|_{D_{\bullet}})/S}
.
Hence we have the following exact
sequence by (
6.10
) and (
6.7
) (2):

(10.0.2)

0\longrightarrow L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}^{\bullet})

\longrightarrow a_{{\rm conv}\bullet*}(L^{\rm conv}_{(D_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet}|_{D_{\bullet}})/S}(\Omega_{D^{(1)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})/S}^{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}}(D_{\bullet}/S;Z_{\bullet}))[-1])\longrightarrow 0.

Recall the morphism

{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}}

of ringed topoi in §
8

and let

{\pi}_{(D,Z|_{D})/S{\rm conv}}

be an analogously defined morphism.
By applying
R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}
to (
10.0.2
),
we obtain the following triangle:

(10.0.3)

\longrightarrow R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm conv}*}L^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow

a_{{\rm conv}*}R{\pi}_{(D,Z|_{D})/S{\rm conv}*}(L^{\rm conv}_{(D_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet}|_{D_{\bullet}})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D^{(1)}({\cal M}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet})/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}}(D_{\bullet}/S;Z_{\bullet}))[-1]\overset{+1}{\longrightarrow}\cdots.

By (
3.7
), (
5.8
) and
by the cohomological descent,
we have the following triangle:

(10.0.4)

\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}\longrightarrow R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})\longrightarrow

a_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D,Z|_{D})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))[-1]\overset{+1}{\longrightarrow}\cdots.

Hence we have the boundary morphism

(10.0.5)

d\colon a_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D,Z|_{D})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))[-1]\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}[1]

in
D^{+}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S})
.
Hence we have the following morphism

(10.0.6)

d\colon a_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D,Z|_{D})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}[2]

in
D^{+}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S})
.
Set

(10.0.7)

G^{\rm conv}_{D/(X,Z)}:=-d.

and call
G^{\rm conv}_{D/(X,Z)}
the

log convergent Gysin morphism
of
D
.
Then we have the following cohomology class

(10.0.8)

\displaystyle c^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(D):=G^{\rm conv}_{D/(X,Z)}\in{\cal E}{\it xt}^{0}_{{\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}}(

\displaystyle a_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D,Z|_{D})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)),

\displaystyle{\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}[2]).

It is a routine work to prove that
G^{\rm conv}_{D/(X,Z)}

(and
c^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(D)
) depends only
on
(X,D\cup Z)/S
.
Since
\varpi^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)

is canonically isomorphic to
{\mathbb{Z}}
and since
there exists a natural morphism

{\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}\longrightarrow a_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D,Z|_{D})/S})
,
we have a cohomology class

\displaystyle c^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(D)

\displaystyle\in{\cal E}{\it xt}^{0}_{{\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S},{\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}[2])

\displaystyle=:\underline{\cal H}_{{\log}{\textrm{-}}{\rm conv}}^{2}((X,Z)/S).

As usual, if
Z=\emptyset
,
we denote
G^{\rm conv}_{D/(X,Z)}

and
c^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(D)
simply by

G^{\rm conv}_{D/X}
and
c^{\rm conv}_{X}(D)
,
respectively.

Remark 10.1
.

As in
[
NS1
]
we can also construct

c^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(D)
by using a
convergent vanishing cycle sheaf.

Proposition 10.2
.

Let
\pi^{\prime}
be a nonzero element of
\pi{\cal V}
.
Let
u\colon S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S
be a morphism of

p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-schemes.
Set
S^{\prime}_{1}:=\underline{\rm Spec}_{S^{\prime}}({\cal O}_{S^{\prime}}/\pi^{\prime}{\cal O}_{S^{\prime}})
.
Let
g\colon Y\longrightarrow S^{\prime}_{1}
be a smooth morphism of schemes
fitting into the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}Y@>{}>{}>X\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S^{\prime}_{1}@>{}>{}>S_{1}.\end{CD}

Set
E:=D\times_{X}Y
and
W:=Z\times_{X}Y
.
Assume that
E\cup W
is a transversal relative SNCD on
Y

over
S_{1}
.
Let
b\colon(E,W|_{E})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(Y,W)

be the natural closed immersion.
Then the image of
c^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S}(D)
in

(\ref{eqn:lcycls})
by the natural morphism

{\cal E}{\it xt}^{0}_{{\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}}(a_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D,Z|_{D})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)),{\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}[2])\longrightarrow

{\cal E}{\it xt}^{0}_{{\cal K}_{(Y,W)/S^{\prime}}}(b_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(E,W|_{E})/S^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}}(E/S^{\prime};W)),{\cal K}_{(Y,W)/S^{\prime}}[2])

is equal to
c^{\rm conv}_{(Y,W)/S^{\prime}}(E)
.

Finally we express the edge morphism
d_{1}^{\bullet\bullet}
of
(
9.5.2
) by the Čech-Gysin morphism.

Henceforth
D
denotes a (not necessarily smooth)
relative SNCD on
X
over
S_{1}

which meets
Z
transversally.
First, fix a decomposition

\{D_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
of
D

by smooth components of
D
over
S_{1}

[
NS1
, (2.1.8)]
and fix a total order on
\Lambda
.
Assume that

D_{\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{k-1}\}}=D_{\lambda_{0}}\cap\cdots\cap D_{\lambda_{k-1}}\not=\emptyset

(\lambda_{i}\not=\lambda_{j}

(i\not=j))
.
Set
\underline{\lambda}:=\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{k-1}\}

(\lambda_{0}<\cdots<\lambda_{k-1})
,

\underline{\lambda}_{j}:=\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\widehat{\lambda}_{j},\ldots,\lambda_{k-1}\}
,

D_{\underline{\lambda}}:=D_{\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{k-1}\}}
,
and
D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}:=D_{\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\widehat{\lambda}_{j},\ldots,\lambda_{k-1}\}}
for
k\geq 2

and
D_{\underline{\lambda}_{0}}:=X
.
Here
~\widehat{}~
means the elimination.
Then
D_{\underline{\lambda}}

is a smooth divisor on
D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}
over
S_{1}
.
Let

\iota^{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}_{\underline{\lambda}}\colon(D_{\underline{\lambda}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}}})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}})

be the closed immersion.
As in
[
NS1
, p. 81]
,
we have the following orientation sheaves

\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z):=\varpi_{\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{k-1}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)

and

\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z):=\varpi_{\lambda_{0}\cdots\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\cdots\lambda_{k-1}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)

associated to the sets
\{D_{\lambda_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{k}

and
\{D_{\lambda_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{k}\setminus\{D_{\lambda_{j}}\}
, respectively.
We denote by
(\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{k-1})

the local section giving a basis of

\varpi_{\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{k-1}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)
;
we also denote by
(\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{k-1})

the local section giving a basis of

\varpi_{\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{k-1}{\rm zar}}(D/S)
.
By (
10.0.7
) we have a morphism

(10.2.1)

\displaystyle G^{{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}{\rm conv}}_{\underline{\lambda}}:=G^{\rm conv}_{D_{\underline{\lambda}}/(D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}})}\colon

\displaystyle\iota^{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\log}}_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D_{\underline{\lambda}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\lambda_{j}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{(D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}})/S}[2].

We fix an isomorphism

(10.2.2)

\varpi_{\lambda_{j}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)

by the following morphism

(\lambda_{j})\otimes(\lambda_{0}\cdots\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\cdots\lambda_{k-1})\longmapsto(-1)^{j}(\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{k-1}).

We identify

\varpi_{\lambda_{j}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)
with

\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)

by this isomorphism.
We also have the following composite morphism

(10.2.3)

(-1)^{j}G^{{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}{\rm conv}}_{\underline{\lambda}}\colon\iota^{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\log}}_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D_{\underline{\lambda}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\iota^{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\log}}_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D_{\underline{\lambda}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\lambda_{j}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))\overset{G^{{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}{\rm conv}}_{\underline{\lambda}}\otimes 1}{\longrightarrow}

{\cal K}_{(D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)[2]

defined by

(10.2.4)

{\rm``}x\otimes(\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{k-1})\longmapsto(-1)^{j}G^{{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}{\rm conv}}_{\underline{\lambda}}(x)\otimes(\lambda_{0}\cdots\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\cdots\lambda_{k-1}){\rm"}.

The morphism (
10.2.4
) induces
the following morphism
of log convergent cohomologies:

(10.2.5)

(-1)^{j}G^{{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}{\rm conv}}_{\underline{\lambda}}\colon R^{h-k}f^{\rm conv}_{(D_{\underline{\lambda}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}}})/S*}({\cal K}_{(D_{\underline{\lambda}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))\longrightarrow

R^{h-k+2}f^{\rm conv}_{(D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}})/S*}({\cal K}_{(D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)).

If
D_{\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{k-1}\}}=\emptyset
, set

(-1)^{j}G^{{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}{\rm conv}}_{\underline{\lambda}}:=0
.

Denote by
a_{\underline{\lambda}}
(resp.
a_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}
)
the natural exact closed immersion

(D_{\underline{\lambda}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}}})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(X,Z)

(resp.
(D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(X,Z)
).

Proposition 10.3
.

Let

d_{1}^{-k,h+k}\colon E_{1}^{-k,h+k}\longrightarrow E_{1}^{-k+1,h+k}

be the boundary
morphism of
(
9.5.2
)
.
Set

G^{\rm conv}:=\sum_{\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{k-1}~|~\lambda_{i}\not=\lambda_{j}~(i\not=j)\}}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}(-1)^{j}G^{{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}{\rm conv}}_{\underline{\lambda}}
.
Then

d_{1}^{-k,h+k}=-G^{\rm conv}
.

11
Comparison theorem

In this section we compare
the weight-filtered isozariskian complex
of a certain
N
-truncated simplicial smooth scheme
with a certain
N
-truncated simplicial relative SNCD
and the weight-filtered zariskian complex defined in

[
NS1
]
and
[
Nak2
]
.
First we consider the non-filtered case.
The following has been proved in
[
Nak2
, (6.1)]
:

Lemma 11.1

(
[
Nak2
, (6.1)]
)
.

Let
Y_{\bullet}
be
a split simplicial fine log
(
formal
)
scheme
over a fine log
(
formal
)
scheme
Y
over
a fine log
(
formal
)
scheme
T
.
Then there exists
a fine split simplicial log
(
formal
)
scheme

Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}
with a morphism
Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow Y_{\bullet}

of simplicial log
(
formal
)
schemes over
Y

satisfying the following conditions
:

(1)

Y^{\prime}_{m}

(m\in{\mathbb{N}})
is the disjoint
union of affine open log
(
formal
)
subschemes
which cover
Y_{m}
and whose images in

Y
are contained in
affine open log
(
formal
)
subschemes of
Y
.

(2)
If
\overset{\circ}{Y}_{m}

(m\in{\mathbb{N}})

is quasi-compact, then the
number of the open subschemes in
(1)

can be assumed to be finite.

In particular, there exists a natural morphism

Y_{\bullet\bullet}\longrightarrow Y_{\bullet}

by setting

Y_{mn}:={\rm cosk}_{0}^{Y_{m}}(Y_{m}^{\prime})_{n}
.
Moreover, for each
