n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

Y_{\bullet n}
is split.

In [loc. cit.] we have called
the simplicial log (formal) scheme

Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}
satisfying
(1)
and
(2)
in
(
11.1
) the
disjoint union of the members of
an affine simplicial open covering
of
Y_{\bullet}/Y
;
we have called the bisimplicial scheme

Y_{\bullet\bullet}
in (
11.1
)
the
Čech diagram
of
Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}

over
Y_{\bullet}/Y
.

The following has been proved in
[
Nak2
, (6.3)]
:

Proposition 11.2

(
[
Nak2
, (6.3)]
)
.

(1)
Let
Y_{\bullet}\longrightarrow Z_{\bullet}

be a morphism of
split simplicial fine log
(
formal
)
schemes
over a morphism
Y\longrightarrow Z
of
fine log
(
formal
)
schemes
over a fine log
(
formal
)
scheme
T
.
Then there exist the disjoint unions of
the members of affine simplicial open coverings

Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}
and
Z^{\prime}_{\bullet}

of
Y_{\bullet}/Y
and
Z_{\bullet}/Z
,
respectively, which fit into
the following commutative
diagram
:

(11.2.1)

\begin{CD}Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}@>{}>{}>Z^{\prime}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
Y_{\bullet}@>{}>{}>Z_{\bullet}.\end{CD}

(2)
Let
Y_{\bullet}\longrightarrow Y\longrightarrow T
be as in

(
11.1
)
.
Let
Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}
and
Y^{\prime\prime}_{\bullet}

be two disjoint unions of the members of
affine simplicial open coverings of
Y_{\bullet}/Y
.
Then there exists a disjoint union of
the members of
an affine simplicial open covering
Y^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\bullet}

of
Y_{\bullet}/Y

fitting into the following
commutative diagram
:

(11.2.2)

\begin{CD}Y^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\bullet}@>{}>{}>Y^{\prime\prime}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}@>{}>{}>Y_{\bullet}.\end{CD}

Remark 11.3
.

Let
N
be a nonnegative integer.
The
N
-truncated versions of (
11.1
)
and (
11.2
) hold.

Let
{\cal V}
,
\pi
,
S
and
S_{1}

be as in §
2
.
Assume that
\pi{\cal V}=p{\cal V}
.
Let
f\colon Y_{\bullet\leq N}\longrightarrow S_{1}
be a fine

N
-truncated simplicial log scheme over
S_{1}
.
Then we have the log crystalline topos

({Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S})_{\rm crys}
of
Y_{\bullet\leq N}/(S,p{\cal O}_{S},[~])
,
where
[~]
is the canonical PD-structure on

p{\cal O}_{S}
.
Let

u^{\rm crys}_{Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S}\colon(({Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S})\longrightarrow((Y_{\bullet\leq N})_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))

be the canonical projection.

Theorem 11.4

(
Comparison theorem).

Assume that
\pi{\cal V}=p{\cal V}
.
Let
N
be a nonnegative integer. Let
Y_{\bullet\leq N}
be
a log smooth

N
-truncated simplicial log scheme over
S_{1}

which has the disjoint union
Y^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N}

of the members of an affine
N
-truncated simplicial
open covering of
Y_{\bullet\leq N}
.
Denote by

(11.4.1)

\Xi_{\bullet\leq N}\colon{\rm Isoc}_{\rm conv}(Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S)(:={\rm Isoc}_{\rm conv,zar}(Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S))\longrightarrow{\rm Isoc}_{\rm crys}(Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S)

the relative simplicial version of
the functor defined in
[
Sh1
, Theorem 5.3.1]

and denoted by
\Phi
in
[
loc. cit.
]
.
Let
E^{\bullet\leq N}
be an object of

{\rm Isoc}_{\rm conv}(Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S)
.
Then there exists a functorial isomorphism

(11.4.2)

Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S*}(E^{\bullet\leq N})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}Ru^{\rm crys}_{Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S*}(\Xi_{\bullet\leq N}(E^{\bullet\leq N})).

Proof.

Since
Y^{\prime}_{N}
is affine,
we have a closed immersion

Y^{\prime}_{N}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}^{\prime}(N)
into
a formally log smooth log noetherian formal scheme over
S
.
To construct a natural morphism

(11.4.3)

Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S*}(E^{\bullet\leq N})\longrightarrow Ru^{\rm crys}_{Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S*}(\Xi_{\bullet\leq N}(E^{\bullet\leq N})),

we need Tsuzuki’s functor
(
[
CT
, §11]
,
[
Ts
, (7.3.1)]
)
as follows.

Let
\Delta
be the standard simplicial
category; an object of
\Delta
is denoted by

[m]:=\{0,\ldots,m\}

(m\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
Let
{\cal C}
be a category which has
finite inverse limits.
Recall a simplicial object

\Gamma:=\Gamma^{\cal C}_{n}(X):=\Gamma^{\cal C}_{n}(X)^{\leq n}
in

{\cal C}

for an object
X\in{\cal C}
and

n\in{\mathbb{N}}

(
[
Ts
, (7.3.1)]
):
for an object
[m]
of
\Delta
, set

\Gamma_{{}{m}}:=\prod_{\delta\in{\rm Hom}_{\Delta}([n],[m])}X_{\delta}

with
X_{\delta}=X
;
for a morphism
\alpha\colon[m^{\prime}]\longrightarrow[m]

in
\Delta
,
\alpha_{\Gamma}\colon\Gamma_{m}\longrightarrow\Gamma_{m^{\prime}}
is defined to be the following:
“
(c_{\gamma})\longmapsto(d_{\beta})
” with
d_{\beta}=c_{\alpha\beta}
.
In fact, we have a functor

(11.4.4)

\Gamma^{\cal C}_{N}(?)\colon{\cal C}\longrightarrow{\cal C}^{\Delta}:=\{\text{simplicial objects in ${\cal C}$}\}.

Let
X_{\bullet\leq N}
be
an
N
-truncated simplicial object of
{\cal C}
and
let
f\colon X_{N}\longrightarrow Y
be a morphism in
{\cal C}
.
Then we have a morphism

(11.4.5)

X_{\bullet\leq N}\longrightarrow\Gamma^{\cal C}_{{}{N}}(Y)_{\bullet\leq N}

defined by the following commutative diagram:

(11.4.6)

\begin{CD}X_{m}@>{}>{}>\prod_{\delta\in{\rm Hom}_{\Delta}([N],[m])}Y_{\delta}\\
@V{X(\gamma)}V{}V@V{}V{\rm proj.}V\\
X_{N}@>{f}>{}>Y_{\gamma}=Y\end{CD}

for any morphism
\gamma\colon[N]\longrightarrow[m]
in
\Delta
.

Let
{\cal C}
be the category of fine log formal schemes over
S
.
Set

\Gamma{\cal Y}^{\prime}(N)_{\bullet}:=\Gamma^{\cal C}_{N}({\cal Y}^{\prime}(N))
.
Then we have an immersion

Y^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\Gamma{\cal Y}^{\prime}(N)_{\bullet\leq N}
.
Set
Y_{lm}:={\rm cosk}_{0}^{Y_{l}}(Y^{\prime}_{l})_{m}

(l,m\in{\mathbb{N}})

and

\Gamma{\cal Y}^{\prime}(N)_{lm}:={\rm cosk}_{0}^{S}(\Gamma{\cal Y}^{\prime}(N)_{l})_{m}

(l,m\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
Then we have an immersion

Y_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\Gamma{\cal Y}^{\prime}(N)_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}
.
Let

{\mathfrak{T}}(N)_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}=\{({\mathfrak{T}}(N)_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet})_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}

(resp.
{\mathfrak{D}}(N)_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}
)
be the system of the universal enlargements
(resp. the log PD-envelopes) of this immersion.
Since
a^{p}=p!a^{[p]}
for a local section
a
of the PD-ideal of

{\mathfrak{D}}(N)_{lm}
, we have a natural morphism

(11.4.7)

(g_{lm})_{k}\colon{\mathfrak{D}}(N)_{lm}\longrightarrow({\mathfrak{T}}(N)_{lm})_{k}\quad(0\leq\forall l\leq N,\forall m,\exists k)

(cf.
[
O2
, (7.6)]
, the proof of
[
Sh1
, Theorem 5.3.1]
).
Let
f(N)_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}
be
the structural morphism
\Gamma{\cal Y}^{\prime}(N)_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}\longrightarrow S
.
Let
({\cal E}_{\overset{\to}{{\mathfrak{T}}(N)}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}},\nabla_{T})

(resp.

({\cal E}_{{\mathfrak{D}(N)}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}},\nabla_{D})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
)
be the coherent crystal of

{\cal K}_{\overset{\to}{{\mathfrak{T}}(N)}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}}
-modules
(resp. the coherent

{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}(N)}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
-modules)
with integrable connection corresponding to

E^{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}

(resp.
\Xi_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}(E^{\bullet\leq N,\bullet})
).
The morphism (
11.4.7
) induces the following morphism

(11.4.8)

{\cal E}_{({\mathfrak{T}}(N)_{lm})_{k}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\Gamma{\cal Y}^{\prime}(N)_{lm}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\Gamma{\cal Y}^{\prime}(N)_{lm}/S}\longrightarrow

(g_{lm})_{k*}({\cal E}_{{\mathfrak{D}}(N)_{lm}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\Gamma{\cal Y}^{\prime}(N)_{lm}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\Gamma{\cal Y}^{\prime}(N)_{lm}/S}

(See also
[
O2
, Theorem 7.7]
.)
Let

\eta\colon(({Y}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet})_{\rm zar},f^{-1}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}({\cal O}_{S}))\longrightarrow(({Y}_{\bullet\leq N})_{\rm zar},f^{-1}_{\bullet\leq N}({\cal O}_{S}))

be the natural morphism of ringed topoi,
where
f_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}\colon Y_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}\longrightarrow S

and
f_{\bullet\leq N}\colon Y_{\bullet\leq N}\longrightarrow S

are the structural morphisms.
Set
(U(N)_{l\bullet})_{k}:=({\mathfrak{T}}(N)_{l\bullet})_{k}\times_{\Gamma{\cal Y}^{\prime}(N)_{l\bullet}}Y_{l\bullet}

with natural morphism

(\beta_{l\bullet})_{k}\colon(U(N)_{l\bullet})_{k}\longrightarrow Y_{l\bullet}
.
Identify
{{\mathfrak{T}}(N)_{l\bullet}}_{\rm zar}
with

{U(N)_{l\bullet}}_{\rm zar}
.
The morphism (
11.4.8
) induces the following morphism

(11.4.9)

R\eta_{*}\varprojlim_{k}R(\beta_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet})_{k*}({\cal E}_{({\mathfrak{T}}(N)_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet})_{k}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\Gamma{\cal Y}^{\prime}(N)_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\Gamma{\cal Y}^{\prime}(N)_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\longrightarrow

R\eta_{*}({\cal E}_{{\mathfrak{D}}(N)_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\Gamma{\cal Y}^{\prime}(N)_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\Gamma{\cal Y}^{\prime}(N)_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}_{\bullet\leq N}({\cal K}_{S}))
.
By (
3.8.1
), the crystalline analogue of
(
3.8.1
) (
[
Ka
, (6.4)]
)
and the cohomological descent,
the morphism (
11.4.9
)
turns out to be the following morphism

(11.4.10)

Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S*}(E^{\bullet\leq N})\longrightarrow Ru^{\rm crys}_{Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S*}(\Xi_{\bullet\leq N}(E^{\bullet\leq N})).

We claim that the morphism (
11.4.10
)
is independent of the choice of
an affine simplicial open covering of
Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S
.
Indeed, let
Y^{\prime\prime}_{\bullet\leq N}
be another affine simplicial open covering of
Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S
.
Then there exists an affine simplicial open covering

Y^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\bullet\leq N}
of
Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S
in (
11.2.2
).
Therefore we may assume that there exists a morphism

Y^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N}\longrightarrow Y^{\prime\prime}_{\bullet\leq N}
over
Y_{\bullet\leq N}
.
Then the rest of the proof of
the independence is a routine work.

By the proof of
[
Sh2
, Theorem (3.1.1)]

(see also the proof of
[
B2
, Proposition 1.9]
),
we see that the following morphism

(11.4.11)

Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y_{m}/S*}(E^{m})\longrightarrow Ru^{\rm crys}_{Y_{m}/S*}(\Xi_{m}(E^{m}))\quad(m\leq N)

is an isomorphism. Consequently
the morphism (
11.4.10
) is an isomorphism.

Finally we prove the functoriality of the morphism
(
11.4.2
).
Let

\begin{CD}Y_{\bullet\leq N}@>{g}>{}>Z_{\bullet\leq N}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{1}@>{}>{}>S^{\prime}_{1}\\
@V{\bigcap}V{}V@V{}V{\bigcap}V\\
S@>{}>{}>S^{\prime}\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram, where

S^{\prime}
is a fine log
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}^{\prime}
-scheme,
where
{\cal V}^{\prime}
is a complete discrete valuation ring
of mixed characteristics and
S^{\prime}_{1}

is a closed log subscheme
defined by the ideal sheaf
p{\cal O}_{S^{\prime}}
.
Then there exists the obvious
N
-truncated version of
the commutative diagram (
11.2.1
).
For any nonnegative integer
n
,
we also have the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}Y^{\prime}_{N}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal Y}^{\prime}(N)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
Z^{\prime}_{N}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal Z}^{\prime}(N),\end{CD}

where the horizontal morphisms are closed immersions
into formally log smooth log noetherian formal schemes over
S
and
S^{\prime}
.
Let
F^{\bullet\leq N}
be an object of

{\rm Isoc}_{\rm conv}(Z_{\bullet\leq N}/S)
with a morphism

g^{*}(F^{\bullet\leq N})\longrightarrow E^{\bullet\leq N}
in

{\rm Isoc}_{\rm conv}(Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S)
.
Using Tsuzuki’s functor,
we have the following commutative diagram

(11.4.12)

\begin{CD}Rg_{*}Ru^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S*}(E^{\bullet\leq N})@>{\sim}>{}>Rg_{*}Ru^{\rm crys}_{Y_{\bullet\leq N}/S*}(\Xi_{\bullet\leq N}(E^{\bullet\leq N}))\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
Ru^{\rm conv}_{Z_{\bullet\leq N}/S^{\prime}*}(F^{\bullet\leq N})@>{\sim}>{}>Ru^{\rm crys}_{Z_{\bullet\leq N}/S^{\prime}*}(\Xi_{\bullet\leq N}(F^{\bullet\leq N})).\end{CD}

We complete the proof of (
11.4
).
∎

Remark 11.5
.

(
11.4
) is an
N
-truncated simplicial
and relative version of Shiho’s comparison theorem
(
[
Sh2
, Theorem 3.1.1]
).
See also
[
Sh3
, Theorem 2.3.6]

for the relative version of [loc. cit.].

The following is a generalization of (
[
O2
, (7.9)]
) and
(
[
Sh2
, Corollary 3.1.1]
).

Corollary 11.6
.

(
We do not assume that
\pi{\cal V}=p{\cal V}
in this corollary.
)

Let
r
be a positive integer.
Set
\underline{\bullet}_{r}:=(\bullet\cdots\bullet)

(r
-points
)
.
Let
f_{\underline{\bullet}_{r}}\colon Y_{\underline{\bullet}_{r}}\longrightarrow S_{1}

be an
r
-simplicial fine log scheme over
S_{1}
.
Assume that

\overset{\circ}{f}_{\underline{\bullet}_{r}}\colon\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\underline{\bullet}_{r}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}_{1}

is proper.
Let
\underline{N}_{r}:=(N_{1},\ldots,N_{r})
be an element of

{\mathbb{N}}^{r}
. Endow
{\mathbb{N}}^{r}
with the following order
induced by the order of
{\mathbb{N}}:

(m_{1},\ldots,m_{r})\leq(n_{1},\ldots,n_{r})\underset{\rm def.}{\Longleftrightarrow}m_{i}\leq n_{i}

(1\leq\forall i\leq r)
.
Let
E^{\underline{\bullet}_{r}}
be an object of

{\rm Isoc}_{\rm conv}(Y_{\underline{\bullet}_{r}}/S)
.
Set

f^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\underline{\bullet}_{r}\leq\underline{N}_{r}}/S}:=f_{\underline{\bullet}_{r}\leq\underline{N}_{r}}\circ u^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\underline{\bullet}_{r}\leq\underline{N}_{r}}/S}
.
Then

Rf^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\underline{\bullet}_{r}\leq\underline{N}_{r}}/S*}(E^{\underline{\bullet}_{r}\leq\underline{N}_{r}})

is quasi-isomorphic to a strictly perfect complex of

{\cal K}_{S}
-modules.

Proof.

As in
[
O2
, (7.9)]
, we may assume that
{\cal V}={\cal W}

and that
\pi=p
.
By the comparison theorem (
11.4
)
for the constant simplicial case and
by the finite tor-dimension and the finite generation
of log crystalline cohomologies,

Rf^{\rm conv}_{(Y_{i_{1}\cdots i_{r}},M_{i_{1}\cdots i_{r}})/S*}(E^{i_{1}\cdots i_{r}})

has finite tor-dimension and coherent cohomology.
Set

E_{1}^{ij}:=R^{j}f^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\underline{\bullet}_{r-1}\leq\underline{N}_{r-1},i}/S*}(E^{\underline{\bullet}_{r}\leq\underline{N}_{r-1},i})

for
0\leq i\leq N_{r}
and
E_{1}^{ij}=0
for
i>N_{r}
.
Then we have the following spectral sequence

E_{1}^{ij}\Longrightarrow R^{i+j}f^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\underline{\bullet}\leq\underline{N}_{r}}/S*}(E^{\underline{\bullet}_{r}\leq\underline{N}_{r}}).

Truncating the cosimplicial degrees similarly,
we see that

Rf^{\rm conv}_{Y_{\underline{\bullet}_{r-1}\leq\underline{N}_{r-1},i}/S*}(E^{\underline{\bullet}_{r-1}\leq\underline{N}_{r-1},i})

has finite tor-dimension and finitely generated cohomology.
Hence (
11.6
) follows from (
11.5
) and
[
BO
, (7.15)]
.
∎

Next we give the comparison theorem stated
in the beginning of this section.
When we consider the log crystalline setting, we consider the canonical PD-structure
on
p{\cal O}_{S}
.

Let
N
be a nonnegative integer.
Let
(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})

be an
N
-truncated simplicial smooth scheme over
S~{\rm mod}~p

with
N
-truncated simplicial transversal relative SNCD’s

D_{\bullet\leq N}
and
Z_{\bullet\leq N}
on

X_{\bullet\leq N}/S~{\rm mod}~p
.
Let

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\rm crys}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}\colon

\displaystyle(({(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(({(X_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S})

be the morphism of ringed topoi forgetting the log structure
along
D_{\bullet\leq N}
.
Let
f_{\bullet\leq N}\colon X_{\bullet\leq N}\longrightarrow S
be the structural morphism.
Then, set

(11.6.1)

\displaystyle(E_{\rm crys}({\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}}):=

\displaystyle(R\epsilon^{\rm crys}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S*}({\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),\tau^{\bullet\leq N})

in

{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S})
.
Let
I^{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}
be a flasque resolution of

{\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}
.
Here the left degree of
I^{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}

is the
N
-truncated cosimplicial degree
corresponding to the
N
-truncated simplicial degree of
X_{\bullet\leq N}

and the second degree is the complex degree.
The filtered complex

(E_{\rm crys}({\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}})

is represented by

(\epsilon^{\rm crys}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S*}(I^{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}),\tau^{\bullet\leq N}).

Let

u^{\rm crys}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}\colon(({(X_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S})\longrightarrow({X_{\bullet\leq N}}_{\rm zar},f^{-1}_{\bullet\leq N}({\cal O}_{S}))

be the natural morphism of ringed topoi.
Set

(11.6.2)

\displaystyle(E_{\rm zar}({\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}}):=

\displaystyle Ru^{{\rm crys}}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S*}(E_{\rm crys}({\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}})

in

{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{\bullet\leq N}({\cal O}_{S}))
.
Let
(J^{\bullet\leq N,\bullet},\{J^{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}})

be a filtered flasque resolution of

(\epsilon^{{\rm crys}}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S*}(I^{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}),\tau^{\bullet\leq N})
.
The filtered complex

(E_{\rm zar}({\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}})

is represented by

u^{{\rm crys}}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S*}((J^{\bullet\leq N,\bullet},\{J^{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}))
.

Definition 11.7
.

We call

(E_{\rm crys}({\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}})

({\rm resp}.~(E_{\rm zar}({\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}}))

the
N
-truncated cosimplicial weight-filtered
vanishing cycle crystalline complex

(resp.
N
-truncated cosimplicial weight-filtered
vanishing cycle zariskian complex
)
of
(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S

with respect to

D_{\bullet\leq N}
.

For the time being we consider the constant case:
let
X
be a smooth scheme
over
S~{\rm mod}~p
and let
D
and
Z

be transversal relative SNCD’s on
X/S~{\rm mod}~p
.
In
[
NS1
, (2.5.7), (2.7.5)]

we have essentially proved the following
which is a special case of
(
11.9
) below:

Theorem 11.8

(
[
NS1
, (2.5.7), (2.7.5)]
).

Let
(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})_{\bullet\in{\mathbb{N}}}

be the Čech diagram of an affine open covering of

(X,D\cup Z)
with structural morphism

f_{\bullet}\colon(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})\longrightarrow S
.
Let

(11.8.1)

(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}`{\cal R}_{\bullet}\quad{\rm and}\quad(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal R}_{\bullet}

be immersions into
formally log smooth simplicial
log noetherian
p
-adic formal schemes over
S

such that

\overset{\circ}{`{\cal R}}_{\bullet}=\overset{\circ}{\cal R}_{\bullet}
.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{\cal R}_{\bullet}
is
topologically of finite type over
S
.
Let
{\cal P}_{\bullet}
and

{\cal Q}_{\bullet}={\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

be the simplicial log schemes in the beginning of

§
8
.
Let
{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}

be the log PD-envelope of the immersion

(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
(S,p{\cal O}_{S},[~])
.
Let
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

be the exactifcation of the immersion

(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}_{\bullet}
.
Let

\pi_{\rm zar}\colon(({X}_{\bullet})_{\rm zar},f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal O}_{S}))\longrightarrow({X}_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))

be a natural morphism of ringed topoi.
Then there exists the following functorial
isomorphism

(11.8.2)

\displaystyle(E_{\rm zar}({\cal O}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S},P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}})

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
.

(
Here the right hand side
is, by definition, equal to

(C_{\rm zar}({\cal O}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})

(cf.
[
NS1
, (2.5.7), (2.5.8)]
)
.
)

Here the functoriality means the functoriality for
the pair of the following three commutative diagrams

\begin{CD}(X,D\cup Z)@>{}>{}>(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{1}@>{}>{}>S^{\prime}_{1},\end{CD}

where
S
and
S^{\prime}
are in
(
9.0.1
)

and
(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})
is in
(
9.0.2
)
,

\begin{CD}(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet})@>{\subset}>{}>`{\cal R}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
(X^{\prime}_{\bullet},D^{\prime}_{\bullet})@>{\subset}>{}>`{\cal R}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\end{CD}

and

\begin{CD}(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal R}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
(X^{\prime}_{\bullet},Z^{\prime}_{\bullet})@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal R}^{\prime}_{\bullet},\end{CD}

over
u\colon S\longrightarrow S^{\prime}
in
(
9.0.1
)
,
where the four horizontal morphisms above are immersion
into formally log smooth simplicial
log
p
-adic formal schemes over
S

such that

`\overset{\circ}{\cal R}_{\bullet}=`\overset{\circ}{\cal R}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}

and

\overset{\circ}{\cal R}_{\bullet}=\overset{\circ}{\cal R}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
.

The following is
an
N
-truncated simplicial version of (
11.8
):

Theorem 11.9

(
cf.
[
Nak2
, (6.17)]
,
Explicit description of cosimplicial
weight-filtered vanishing cycle zariskian complex).

Let
N
be a nonnegative integer.
Assume that

(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S~{\rm mod}~p

has the disjoint union of the member of
an affine
N
-truncated
simplicial open covering of

(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S~{\rm mod}~p
.
Let

(X_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet},D_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N\bullet})

be the Čech diagram of
this affine
N
-truncated simplicial open covering.
Let

(11.9.1)

(X_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet},D_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}`{\cal R}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}\quad{\rm and}\quad(X_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet},Z_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal R}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}

be an
(N,\infty)
-truncated version of

(
11.8.1
)
.
Let

{\cal P}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}
,

{\cal Q}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}={\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}
,

{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}
and

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}

be the
(N,\infty)
-truncated versions of

{\cal P}_{\bullet}
,

{\cal Q}_{\bullet}={\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}
,

{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}
and

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

in
(
11.8
)
.
Let

\eta_{\rm zar}\colon(({X}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet})_{\rm zar},f^{-1}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}({\cal O}_{S}))\longrightarrow(({X}_{\bullet\leq N})_{\rm zar},f^{-1}_{\bullet\leq N}({\cal O}_{S}))

be a natural morphism of ringed topoi.
Then

(11.9.2)

(E_{\rm zar}({\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}})=

R\eta_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}/S},P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}})

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{\bullet\leq N}({\cal O}_{S}))
.

Proof.

The same proof as that of
[
Nak2
, (6.17)]
works.
∎

Let

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}\colon

\displaystyle(({(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(({(X_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S})_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S})

be the morphism of topoi forgetting the log structure
along
D_{\bullet\leq N}
.
Then, set

\displaystyle(E_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}}):=

\displaystyle(R\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S*}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),\tau^{\bullet\leq N})

in

{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S})
,
and

\displaystyle(E_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}}):=

\displaystyle Ru^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S*}(E_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}})

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{\bullet\leq N}({\cal K}_{S}))
.

Lemma 11.10
.

Let
{\cal V}^{\prime}
and
\pi^{\prime}
be as in
(
2.1
)
.
Assume that
{\cal V}^{\prime}={\cal V}
. Set
S^{\prime}_{1}:=S~{\rm mod}~\pi^{\prime}
.
Set
(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime}):=(X,D\cup Z)\times_{S_{1}}S^{\prime}_{1}
.
Then

(11.10.1)

\displaystyle(E_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}})=(E_{\rm conv}({\cal K}_{(X^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N},D^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N}}).

Consequently

(11.10.2)

\displaystyle(E_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}})=(E_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N},D^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N}}).

Proof.

The equality (
11.10.1
) immediately follows from (
2.1.5
).
∎

By (
8.11
), (
3.8
)
and the same proof as that of
(
11.9
)(=the proof of
[
Nak2
, (6.17)]
),
we obtain the following:

Theorem 11.11

(
Explicit description of
cosimplicial weight-filtered isozariskian complex).

Let the notations be as in
(
11.9
)
.
Let

(\{(U_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet})_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}},\{(T_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet})_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}})

be the system of the universal enlargements of
the immersion

(X_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet},D_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}
.
Let
\beta_{k}\colon(U_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet})_{k}\longrightarrow X_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0})

be the natural morphism.
Identify
((T_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet})_{k})_{{\rm zar}}
with

((U_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet})_{k})_{{\rm zar}}
.
Let
\eta_{{\rm zar}*}\colon((U_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet})_{k})_{{\rm zar}}\longrightarrow(U_{\bullet\leq N})_{{\rm zar}}

be the natural morphism of topoi.
Then

(11.11.1)

(E_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}})=

R\eta_{{\rm zar}*}\varprojlim_{k}R\beta_{k*}({\cal K}_{T_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet k}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}/S},P^{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}/{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\leq N,\bullet}})

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{\bullet\leq N}({\cal K}_{S}))
.

Corollary 11.12

(
Comparison theorem of weight-filtered
vanishing cycle isozariskian and zariskian complexes).

There exists a canonical isomorphism

(11.12.1)

(E_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(E_{\rm zar}({\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{\bullet\leq N}({\cal K}_{S}))
.

Proof.

By (
11.10
) we can replace
\pi
by
p
.
Hence we may assume that
p{\cal V}=\pi{\cal V}
.
By the explicit descriptions (
11.9.2
) and
(
11.11.1
),
the morphism (
11.4.7
) induces the morphism
(
11.12.1
).
Let
m\leq N
be a nonnegative integer.
For a nonnegative integer
k
, we have

(11.12.2)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P^{D_{m}}}_{k}E_{\rm isozar}({\cal K}_{(X_{m},D_{m}\cup Z_{m})/S})=

\displaystyle a^{(k)}_{*}Ru^{\rm conv}_{(D^{(k)}_{m},Z_{m}|_{D^{(k)}_{m}})/S*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(k)}_{m},Z_{m}|_{D^{(k)}_{m}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k){\rm log}}_{{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z))

and

(11.12.3)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P^{D_{m}}}_{k}E_{\rm zar}({\cal O}_{(X_{m},D_{m}\cup Z_{m})/S})=

\displaystyle a^{(k)}_{*}Ru^{\rm crys}_{(D^{(k)}_{m},Z_{m}|_{D^{(k)}_{m}})/S}({\cal O}_{(D^{(k)}_{m},Z_{m}|_{D^{(k)}_{m}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)\log}_{{\rm crys}}(D/S;Z))

by (
8.1.1
), (
8.11.8
) and
[
NS1
, (2.6.1.2), (2.7.3.2)]
.
By the relative version of Shiho’s comparison isomorphism

[
Sh2
, Corollary 2.3.9]

(or (
11.4.2
)), the right hand sides on
(
11.12.2
) and
(
11.12.3
) are isomorphic.
Hence the morphism (
11.12.1
)
is a filtered quasi-isomorphism.
By using (
11.2
) and the proof of (
11.4
),
we can prove, in a standard way, that
the isomorphism (
11.12.1
) is independent
of the choice of the affine simplicial open covering of

(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})

and the commutative diagram (
11.9.1
).
∎

Let
(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})
be
a smooth simplicial scheme
with transversal simplicial relative SNCD’s
over
S_{1}
.

Set
f^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S}:=f\circ u^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S}
.
To construct a weight spectral sequence of

R^{h}f^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S*}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S})

(h\in{\mathbb{N}})
,
let us recall the (multi)cosimplicial diagonal filtration
in
[
D2
]
and
[
Nak2
]
.

Let
({\cal T},{\cal A})
be a ringed topos.
Let
({\cal T}_{t},{\cal A}^{t})_{t\in{\mathbb{N}}}

be a constant simplicial ringed topos
defined by
({\cal T},{\cal A})
:

{\cal T}_{t}={\cal T}
,
{\cal A}^{t}={\cal A}
.
Let
M
be an object of

{\rm C}({\cal A}^{\bullet})
.
The object
M
defines a double complex

M^{\bullet\bullet}=(M^{t\bullet})_{t\in{\mathbb{N}}}
of
{\cal A}
-modules
whose boundary morphisms will be fixed
in (
11.12.4
) below.
Let us consider a single complex

{\bf s}(M)

with the following boundary morphism
(cf.
[
D2
, (5.1.9.1), (5.1.9.2)]
):

(11.12.4)

{\bf s}(M)^{n}=\bigoplus_{t+s=n}M^{ts}=\bigoplus_{t+s=n}M^{ts};

\displaystyle d(x^{ts})=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{t+1}(-1)^{i}\delta^{i}(x^{ts})+(-1)^{t}d_{M}(x^{ts})\;(x^{ts}\in M^{ts}),

where

d_{M}\colon M^{ts}\longrightarrow M^{t,s+1}

is the boundary morphism arising
from the boundary
morphism of the complex
M
and

\delta^{i}\colon M^{ts}\longrightarrow M^{ts}

(0\leq i\leq t+1)
is a
standard coface morphism.
Let
L
be the stupid
filtration on
{\bf s}(M)

with respect to the first index:

(11.12.5)

L^{t}({\bf s}(M))=\bigoplus_{t^{\prime}\geq t}M^{t^{\prime}\bullet}.

Next we consider the filtered version of the above.

Let
(M,P):=(M,\{P_{k}M\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}})

be a complex of increasingly filtered

{\cal A}^{\bullet}
-modules.
Then
(M,P)
defines an
(r+1)
-uple filtered complex

(\bigoplus_{t\geq 0,s}M^{ts},\{\bigoplus_{t\geq 0,s}P_{k}M^{ts}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}})

of
{\cal A}
-modules.

Let

\delta(L,P)
be the diagonal filtration of

L
and
P
on
{\bf s}(M)

(cf.
[
D2
, (7.1.6.1), (8.1.22)]
):

(11.12.6)

\displaystyle\delta(L,P)_{k}{\bf s}(M)

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{t\geq 0,s}P_{t+k}M^{ts}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t\geq 0}L^{t}({\bf s}(M))\cap{\bf s}(P_{t+k}M).

Then we have the following equality:

(11.12.7)

{\rm gr}_{k}^{\delta(L,P)}{\bf s}(M)=\bigoplus_{t\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r}}{\rm gr}^{P}_{t+k}M^{t\bullet}[-t].

Set
f^{\rm crys}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S}:=f\circ u^{\rm crys}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S}
.
Next let us construct a spectral sequence of

R^{h}f^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S*}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S})

(h\in{\mathbb{N}})

and compare the spectral sequence of

R^{h}f^{\rm crys}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S*}({\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

constructed in
[
Nak2
, §4]
.

Let
S_{\bullet}
be
the constant simplicial formal scheme defined by
S
.
Let

I^{\bullet\bullet}_{\rm conv}
be an injective resolution of

{\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S}
.
Let
(J^{\bullet\bullet},P)
be a filtered injective resolution of

(\epsilon^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S_{\bullet}*}(I^{\bullet\bullet}_{\rm conv}),\tau)

(
[
NS1
, (1.1.7)]
).
Set

(K^{\bullet\bullet},P):=u^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S_{\bullet}*}(J^{\bullet\bullet},P).

Let
\delta(L,P)
be the diagonal filtration of
L
and
P

on
{\bf s}(K^{\bullet\bullet})
.
Then, by (
8.8.1
), we have

(11.12.8)

\begin{split}{\cal H}^{h}({\rm gr}_{k}^{\delta(L,P)}{\bf s}(K^{\bullet\bullet}))&=\bigoplus_{t\geq 0}{\cal H}^{h}({\rm gr}^{P}_{t+k}J^{t\bullet}[-t])\\
&=\bigoplus_{t\geq 0}R^{h-2t-k}f^{\rm conv}_{(D_{t}^{(t+k)},Z|_{D^{(t+k)}_{t}})/S*}({\cal K}_{(D_{t}^{(t+k)},Z|_{D^{(t+k)}_{t}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\\
&\phantom{=\bigoplus_{t\geq 0}R^{h-2t-k}f^{\rm conv}_{(D_{t}^{(t+k)},Z|_{D^{(t+k)}}}}\varpi^{(t+k)}_{\rm conv}(D_{t}/S;Z_{t}))(-(t+k)).\end{split}

Hence we have the following
spectral sequence as in
[
Nak2
, (5.1.3)]
:

(11.12.9)

\begin{split}E_{1,{\rm conv}}^{-k,h+k}((X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S_{K})=&\bigoplus_{t\geq 0}R^{h-2t-k}f^{\rm conv}_{(D_{t}^{(t+k)},Z|_{D_{t}^{(t+k)}})/S*}\\
&\phantom{\bigoplus_{t\geq 0}R}({\cal K}_{(D_{t}^{(t+k)},Z|_{D_{t}^{(t+k)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(t+k)}_{\rm conv}(D_{t}/S;Z_{t}))\\
{}&\phantom{({\cal K}_{(D_{t}^{(t+k)},Z|_{D_{t}^{(t+k)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(t+k)}}(-(t+k))\\
{}&\Longrightarrow R^{h}f^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S*}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S}).\end{split}

In
[
Nak2
]
we have constructed
the following analogous spectral sequence:

(11.12.10)

\begin{split}E_{1,{\rm crys}}^{-k,h+k}((X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S)=&\bigoplus_{t\geq 0}R^{h-2t-k}f^{\rm crys}_{(D_{t}^{(t+k)},Z|_{D_{t}^{(t+k)}})/S*}\\
&\phantom{\bigoplus_{t\geq 0}R}({\cal O}_{(D_{t}^{(t+k)},Z|_{D_{t}^{(t+k)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(t+k)}_{\rm crys}(D_{t}/S;Z_{t}))\\
{}&\phantom{({\cal O}_{(D_{t}^{(t+k)},Z|_{D_{t}^{(t+k)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(t+k)}}(-(t+k))\\
{}&\Longrightarrow R^{h}f^{\rm crys}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S*}({\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S}).\end{split}

As a corollary of (
11.12
), we have the following:

Corollary 11.13
.

Assume that
(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})

has the disjoint union of an affine simplicial open covering
over
S
.
Then the spectral sequences

(\ref{ali:simppws})
and

(\ref{ali:opws})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

are canonically isomorphic.

Proof.

Fix
h\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Let
N
be a sufficiently large integer.
Then we have the spectral sequence
of

R^{h}f^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S*}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S})

which is analogous to
(
11.12.9
).
We also have the following spectral sequence of

R^{h}f^{\rm crys}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S*}({\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S})

which is analogous to (
11.12.10
).
Let

E_{r,{\rm conv}}^{-k,h+k}((X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S_{K})\quad(r\geq 1)

and

E_{r,{\rm crys}}^{-k,h+k}((X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S)\quad(r\geq 1)

be the
E_{r}
-terms of these spectral sequences, respectively.
Then

E_{r,{\rm conv}}^{-k,h+k}((X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S_{K})\simeq E_{r,{\rm crys}}^{-k,h+k}((X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S)

by (
11.12.1
).
Because
N
is sufficiently large,

E_{r,{\rm conv}}^{-k,h+k}((X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S_{K})\simeq E_{r,{\rm conv}}^{-k,h+k}((X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S_{K})
and

E_{r,{\rm crys}}^{-k,h+k}((X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S)\simeq E_{r,{\rm crys}}^{-k,h+k}((X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S)
.
Hence

E_{r,{\rm conv}}^{-k,h+k}((X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S_{K})\simeq E_{r,{\rm crys}}^{-k,h+k}((X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S_{K})
(cf.
[
Nak2
, (2.2)]
).
We complete the proof.
∎

Using (
11.13
) we obtain the following:

Theorem 11.14

(
E_{2}
-degeneration).

The spectral sequence

(\ref{ali:simppws})
degenerates at
E_{2}
if

X_{\bullet}
is proper over
S_{1}
and if

Z_{\bullet}=\emptyset
.

Proof.

In
[
Nak2
]
, by the same proof as that of

[
NS1
, (2.17.2)]
,
we have proved the
E_{2}
-degeneration of

(\ref{ali:opws})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

when
X_{\bullet}
is proper over
S_{1}
and
when
Z_{\bullet}=\emptyset
.
Hence (
11.14
) follows from
(
11.13
).
∎

Proposition 11.15

(
Base change theorem).

Let
{\cal V}^{\prime}
,
K^{\prime}
,
\pi^{\prime}
,
S^{\prime}
and
S_{1}^{\prime}

be as in
§
9
.
Let

(11.15.1)

\begin{CD}S^{\prime}@>{u}>{}>S\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\rm Spf}({\cal V}^{\prime})@>{}>{}>{\rm Spf}({\cal V})\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram of
p
-adic formal schemes.
Let
Y

(
resp.
Y^{\prime})

be a quasi-compact smooth scheme over

S_{1}

(
resp.
S^{\prime}_{1})

(
with trivial log structure
)
.
Let
N
be a nonnegative integer.
Let

f\colon(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})\longrightarrow Y

be a morphism from a smooth

N
-truncated simplicial scheme with
transversal relative
N
-truncated simplicial SNCD’s

D_{\bullet\leq N}
and
Z_{\bullet\leq N}

such that the morphism

\overset{\circ}{f}\colon X_{\bullet\leq N}\longrightarrow Y

is smooth, quasi-compact and quasi-separated.
Let

\begin{CD}(X^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N},D^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N})@>{g_{\bullet\leq N}}>{}>(X_{\bullet\leq N},D_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})\\
@V{f^{\prime}}V{}V@V{}V{f}V\\
Y^{\prime}@>{h}>{}>Y\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
(S^{\prime},p{\cal O}_{S^{\prime}},[~]^{\prime})@>{u}>{}>(S,p{\cal O}_{S},[~])\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram of

N
-truncated simplicial
(
log
)
schemes
such that the upper rectangle is cartesian,
where
[~]^{\prime}
and
[~]
are the canonical PD-structures on

p{\cal O}_{S^{\prime}}
and
p{\cal O}_{S}
, respectively.
Let

f_{(X_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})}\colon(X_{\bullet\leq N},Z_{\bullet\leq N})\longrightarrow Y\quad{\text{a}nd}\quad f_{(X^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N},Z^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N})}\colon(X^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N},Z^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N})\longrightarrow Y^{\prime}

be the induced morphisms by
f
and
f^{\prime}
, respectively.
Then the base change morphism

(11.15.2)

{\cal K}_{S^{\prime}}\otimes^{L}_{{\cal K}_{S}}(Rf^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S*}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),\delta(L,P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}}))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

(Rf^{\rm conv}_{(X^{\prime}_{\bullet},D^{\prime}_{\bullet}\cup Z^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N})/S*}({\cal K}_{(X^{\prime}_{\bullet},D^{\prime}_{\bullet}\cup Z^{\prime}_{\bullet\leq N})/S}),\delta(L,P^{D_{\bullet\leq N}}))

is an isomorphism in the filtered derived category

{\rm DF}({\cal K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

In
[
NS1
, (2.10.3)]

we have proved that the log crystalline version of
the morphism (
11.15.2
) for the constant case
is a filtered isomorphism.
Thus (
11.15
) immediately follows
from the comparison theorem (
11.12.1
) as in (
11.14
).
∎

Theorem 11.16

(
Künneth formula).

Let
h
be a nonnegative integer and
let
N
be a nonnegative integer satisfying
the following inequality in
[
Nak2
, (2.2.1)]
:

N>\max\{i+2^{-1}(h-i+1)(h-i+2)~|~0\leq i\leq h\}=2^{-1}(h+1)(h+2).

Let
Y
and

f^{i}\colon(X^{i}_{\bullet\leq N},D^{i}_{\bullet\leq N}\cup Z^{i}_{\bullet\leq N})\longrightarrow Y

(i=1,2)
be as in
(
11.15
)
.
Set
f_{3}:=f_{1}\times_{Y}f_{2}
,

X^{3}_{\bullet\leq N}:=X^{1}_{\bullet\leq N}\times_{Y}X^{2}_{\bullet\leq N}
,

D^{3}_{\bullet\leq N}:=(D^{1}_{\bullet\leq N}\times_{Y}X^{2}_{\bullet\leq N})\cup(X^{1}_{\bullet\leq N}\times_{Y}D^{2}_{\bullet\leq N})
and

Z^{3}_{\bullet\leq N}:=(Z^{1}_{\bullet\leq N}\times_{Y}X^{2}_{\bullet\leq N})\cup(X^{1}_{\bullet\leq N}\times_{Y}Z^{2}_{\bullet\leq N})
.
Set

\displaystyle(F^{\bullet}_{i},\delta_{i}):=

\displaystyle\{Rf_{(X^{i}_{{\bullet}\leq{N}},Z^{i}_{{\bullet}\leq{N}}){\rm conv}*}(E^{\log,Z^{i}_{{\bullet}\leq{N}}}_{\rm crys}({\cal O}_{(X^{i}_{{\bullet}\leq{N}},D^{i}_{{\bullet}\leq{N}}\cup Z^{i}_{{\bullet}\leq{N}})/S})),\delta({L},P^{D^{i}_{{\bullet}\leq{N}}})\}

\displaystyle(i=1,2,3).

Then, for an integer
k
,
there exists a canonical isomorphism

(11.16.1)

{\cal H}^{h}[(\delta_{1}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{S}}^{L}\delta_{2})_{k}\{(F^{\bullet}_{1},\delta_{1}){\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{S}}^{L}(F^{\bullet}_{2},\delta_{2})\}]\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal H}^{h}[(\delta_{3})_{k}(F^{\bullet}_{3},\delta_{3})].

Proof.

In
[
Nak2
, (5.10)]
we have proved
the analogous preweight-filtered Künneth formula
for the log crystalline case.
Thus (
11.16.1
) immediately follows from
the comparison theorem (
11.12.1
) as in (
11.14
).
∎

Theorem 11.17

(
Strict compatibility).

Assume that
{\cal V}^{\prime}={\cal V}
and
S^{\prime}=S

in
(
11.15
)
.
Let
f\colon(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet})\longrightarrow S_{1}

and
f^{\prime}\colon(X^{\prime}_{\bullet},D^{\prime}_{\bullet})\longrightarrow S_{1}

be proper smooth simplicial schemes
with relative SNCD’s over
S_{1}
.
Let

g\colon(X^{\prime}_{\bullet},D^{\prime}_{\bullet})\longrightarrow(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet})

be a morphism of simplicial log
schemes over
S_{1}
.
Let
h
be an integer.
Then the induced morphism

(11.17.1)

g^{*}\colon R^{h}f^{\rm conv}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet})/S*}({\cal K}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet})/S})\longrightarrow R^{h}f^{\rm conv}_{(X^{\prime}_{\bullet},D^{\prime}_{\bullet})/S*}({\cal K}_{(X^{\prime}_{\bullet},D^{\prime}_{\bullet})/S})

is strictly compatible with the weight filtration.

Proof.

In
[
NS1
, (2.18.2)]
we have proved
the strict compatibility of
g^{*}

with respect to the weight filtration in the case of
log crystalline cohomologies for the constant case.
In fact we obtain the strict compatibility
in the case of log crystalline cohomologies
for the simplicial case (cf.
[
Nak2
, (8.5)]
) by the same proof as that of [loc. cit.].
Now (
11.17
) follows from the comparison theorem
(
11.12.1
).
∎

12
Log convergent cohomologies
with compact support

In this section we give fundamental results for the log convergent cohomology sheaf
with compact support.
Because the proofs of the results are almost
the same as those in
[
NS1
, (2.11)]
, we omit several of them.

Let
S
be as in §
2
.
Let
Y
be a log smooth scheme over
S_{1}
.
Let
U
be a log open subscheme of
Y
.
Let
\iota\colon U\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal U}

be an immersion into a log smooth noetherian
log
p
-adic formal scheme over
S

which is topologically of finite type over
S
.
Let
\{T_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be
the system of the universal enlargements
of
\iota
.
Let
(Y/S)_{\rm Rconv}
be
a subtopos of
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)

whose objects are
T_{n}
’s for all
immersions
\iota
’s above.
Let

(12.0.1)

Q^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}\colon({Y/S})_{\rm Rconv}\longrightarrow(Y/S)_{\rm conv}

be a morphism of topoi such that

Q^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S}(E)
for an object
E\in(Y/S)_{\rm conv}

is the natural restriction of
E
.
Set

\overline{u}{}^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}:=u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}\circ Q^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}\colon({Y/S})_{\rm Rconv}\longrightarrow Y_{\rm zar}.

Lemma 12.1
.

Let
(U,{\cal U},\iota)
be as above.
Let
\beta_{n}\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U})\longrightarrow U
and

\beta_{n}(1)\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U}\times_{S}{\cal U})\longrightarrow U

be the natural morphisms.
Then the following sequence

(12.1.1)

u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(E)|_{U}\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n*}(E_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U})})\overset{\longrightarrow}{\longrightarrow}\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n}(1)_{*}(E_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U}\times_{S}{\cal U})})

is exact.

Proof.

(The proof is the obvious log convergent analogue of the proof
in
[
B1
, IV Proposition 2.3.2]
.)
To give a section of
u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(E)|_{U}
is
to give compatible sections
s_{T}
’s of
E_{(V,T,j,v)}
’s
for all objects
(V,T,j,v)
of
(Y/S)_{\rm conv}

with
v\colon V\longrightarrow U
: for any morphism

g\colon(V^{\prime},T^{\prime},j^{\prime},v^{\prime})\longrightarrow(V,T,j,v)
,
s_{T^{\prime}}=g^{*}(s_{T})
in
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
.
Hence we have the following natural morphism

u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(E)|_{U}\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n*}(E_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U})})\overset{\longrightarrow}{\longrightarrow}\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n}(1)_{*}(E_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U}\times_{S}{\cal U})})).

Conversely let
\{s_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be a section of

\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n*}(E_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U})})
such that
the pull-back of the two projections

{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U}\times_{S}{\cal U})\overset{\longrightarrow}{\longrightarrow}{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U})

are equal.
Let
(V,T,j,v)
of
(Y/S)_{\rm conv}

with
v\colon V\longrightarrow U
.
Then there exists a positive integer

n
such that
V\longrightarrow U
lifts to a morphism

T\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U})
.
Now the rest of the proof is a routine work.
∎

Corollary 12.2
.

Let
F
be a sheaf of sets in
(Y/S)_{\rm Rconv}
.
Let
(U,{\cal U},\iota)
be as in

(
12.1
)
.
Let
\beta_{n}\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U})\longrightarrow U
and

\beta_{n}(1)\colon{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U}\times_{S}{\cal U})\longrightarrow U

be the natural morphisms.
Then the following sequence

(12.2.1)

\overline{u}{}^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(F)|_{U}\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n*}(F_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U})})\overset{\longrightarrow}{\longrightarrow}\varprojlim_{n}\beta_{n}(1)_{*}(F_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U}\times_{S}{\cal U})})

is exact.

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of
[
B1
, IV Proposition 2.3.3]
:
we have only to use the formulas:

\overline{u}{}^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(F)=u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(Q^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(F))
,

Q^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(F)_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U})}=F_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U})}
and

Q^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(F)_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U}\times_{S}{\cal U})}=F_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{U,n}({\cal U}\times_{S}{\cal U})}
.
∎

Corollary 12.3
.

Let
E
be a sheaf of sets in
(Y/S)_{\rm conv}
.
Then there exists the following canonical isomorphism
:

u^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(E)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\overline{u}{}^{\rm conv}_{Y/S*}(Q^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S}(E)).

Proof.

By using (
12.1.1
) and (
12.2.1
),
the proof is the obvious log convergent analogue of the proof
in
[
B1
, IV Proposition 2.3.5]
; we omit the proof.
∎

Proposition 12.4
.

Any injective module in
(Y/S)_{\rm Rconv}

is
Q^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S}(I)
for an
injective module
I
in
(Y/S)_{\rm conv}
.

Proof.

The proof is the obvious log convergent analogue of the proof
in
[
B1
, IV Proposition 2.2.5]
; we omit the proof.
∎

The morphism

Q^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}
induces the following morphism of
ringed topoi

(({Y/S})_{\rm Rconv},Q^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S}({\cal K}_{Y/S}))\longrightarrow((Y/S)_{\rm conv},{\cal K}_{Y/S}),

which we denote by
Q^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}
again.
Consequently we have the following morphism
of ringed topoi:

\overline{u}{}^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}\colon(({Y/S})_{\rm Rconv},Q^{{\rm conv}*}_{Y/S}({\cal K}_{Y/S}))\longrightarrow(Y_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S})).

Then the following hold
:

Corollary 12.5
.

R\overline{u}_{Y/S*}Q^{*}_{Y/S}=Ru_{Y/S*}
.

Let
I_{Y}
be a quasi-coherent ideal sheaf of the log structure
M_{Y}
of
Y
.
Let
(U,T,\iota,u)
be an object of the log convergent site
{\rm Conv}(Y/S)
.
Let
M_{U}
and
M_{T}
be the log structures of
U
and
T
, respectively.
Because
\iota\colon U\longrightarrow T

is an exact closed immersion,

M_{T}/{\cal O}_{T}^{*}=M_{U}/{\cal O}_{U}^{*}

on
U_{\rm zar}=T_{\rm zar}
.
Hence local sections of

I_{Y}|_{U}
on
U
lift locally to local sections
m
’s of
M_{T}
.
We define an ideal sheaf

{\cal I}^{{\rm conv}}_{Y/S}\subset{\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}
by the following:

{\cal I}^{{\rm conv}}_{Y/S}(T)
=
the ideal generated by the image of
m
’s by
the following composite morphism

M_{T}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{T}\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{T}
.

Lemma 12.6
.

The sheaf
{\cal I}^{\rm conv}_{Y/S}

defines an isocrystal on
(Y/S)_{\rm Rconv}
.

Proof.

The question is local.
Let

(12.6.1)

\begin{CD}U^{\prime}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal U}^{\prime}\\
@V{\bigcap}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
U@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal U}\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram,
where
U^{\prime}
and
U
are log affine open subschemes of
Y
and
the horizontal morphisms are immersions into
log smooth log noetherian
p
-adic formal schemes over
S

which are topologically of finite type over
S
.
We may assume that the immersions are exact.
By
[
NS1
, (2.1.5)]
we may assume that

{\cal U}={\cal U}_{1}\times_{S}{\cal U}_{2}

and

{\cal U}^{\prime}={\cal U}^{\prime}_{1}\times_{S}{\cal U}^{\prime}_{2}
,
where
{\cal U}_{1}
and
{\cal U}^{\prime}_{1}
are
lifts of
U
and
U^{\prime}
over
S
, respectively,

{\cal U}_{2}\simeq\widehat{A}^{e}_{S}=\underline{\rm Spf}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{e}\})
and

{\cal U}^{\prime}_{2}\simeq\widehat{A}^{e^{\prime}}_{S}=\underline{\rm Spf}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}\{y^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,y^{\prime}_{e^{\prime}}\})

for some
e,e^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Then

{\cal O}_{T_{n}}=({\cal O}_{{\cal U}_{1}}\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{e}\}[t_{\underline{m}}\,|\,|\underline{m}|=n]]/(\underline{y}^{\underline{m}}-\pi t_{\underline{m}}))^{\widehat{}}

and

{\cal O}_{T^{\prime}_{n}}=({\cal O}_{{\cal U}^{\prime}_{1}}\{y^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,y^{\prime}_{e^{\prime}}\}[t^{\prime}_{\underline{m}}~|~|\underline{m}|=n]]/(\underline{y}^{\prime}{}^{\underline{m}}-\pi t^{\prime}_{\underline{m}}))^{\widehat{}}
.
Consider the following composite morphism

\displaystyle{\cal U}^{\prime}_{1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal U}^{\prime}_{1}\times_{S}{\cal U}^{\prime}_{2}\longrightarrow{\cal U}_{1}\times_{S}{\cal U}_{2}\longrightarrow{\cal U}_{1},

where the first morphism is defined by the zero section of

\underline{\rm Spf}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}\{y^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,y^{\prime}_{e^{\prime}}\})
and
the last morphism is the projection.
Let
I\longrightarrow I_{Y}
be a local chart of
I_{Y}
and
let
\langle I\rangle{\cal O}_{\cal U}
be an ideal sheaf of

{\cal O}_{\cal U}
generated by the images of
local sections of
M_{\cal U}
(by the structural morphism

M_{\cal U}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\cal U}
) which lift local sections of

{\rm Im}(I\longrightarrow I_{Y}|_{U})
.
By the lemma (
12.7
) below,
we have only to show that
the natural morphism

(12.6.2)

\displaystyle\langle I\rangle{\cal O}_{{\cal U}_{1}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal U}_{1}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal U}^{\prime}_{1}}\longrightarrow\langle I\rangle{\cal O}_{{\cal U}^{\prime}_{1}}

is an isomorphism. Hence we may assume that
{\cal U}
and
{\cal U}^{\prime}
are lifts of

U
and
U^{\prime}
, respectively.
Let
{\cal U}|_{U^{\prime}}
be the log open formal subscheme of
{\cal U}

whose underlying formal subscheme is isomorphic to
U^{\prime}
as a topological space.
Since the natural morphism
{\cal U}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\cal U}|_{U^{\prime}}
induces
the identity of
U^{\prime}
, this morphism is an isomorphism.
Since
{\cal U}|_{U^{\prime}}\longrightarrow{\cal U}
is an open immersion,
it is clear that the natural morphism

(12.6.3)

\displaystyle\langle I\rangle{\cal O}_{\cal U}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal U}}{\cal O}_{{\cal U}|_{U^{\prime}}}\longrightarrow\langle I\rangle{\cal O}_{{\cal U}|_{U^{\prime}}}

is an isomorphism.
It is also clear that
the natural morphism

(12.6.4)

\displaystyle\langle I\rangle{\cal O}_{{\cal U}|_{U^{\prime}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal U}|_{U^{\prime}}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal U}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\langle I\rangle{\cal O}_{{\cal U}^{\prime}}

is an isomorphism since the morphism

{\cal U}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\cal U}|_{U^{\prime}}
is an isomorphism.
We finish the proof.
∎

Lemma 12.7
.

Let
A
be a commutative ring with unit element and
let
B
,
C
be commutative
A
-algebras with unit elements.
Let
B^{\prime}

(
resp.
C^{\prime})
be
a commutative
B
-algebra
(
resp.
C
-algebra
)

with unit element.
Let
J
be an ideal of
B

Then there exists an isomorphism

((J\otimes 1)(B\otimes_{A}C))\otimes_{B\otimes_{A}C}(B^{\prime}\otimes_{A}C^{\prime})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(JB\otimes_{B}B^{\prime})\otimes_{A}C^{\prime}

fitting into the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}((J\otimes 1)(B\otimes_{A}C))\otimes_{B\otimes_{A}C}(B^{\prime}\otimes_{A}C^{\prime})@>{\sim}>{}>(JB\otimes_{B}B^{\prime})\otimes_{A}C^{\prime}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
B^{\prime}\otimes_{A}C^{\prime}=B^{\prime}\otimes_{A}C^{\prime}.\end{CD}

Proof.

We define a map from

((J\otimes 1)(B\otimes_{A}C))\otimes_{B\otimes_{A}C}(B^{\prime}\otimes_{A}C^{\prime})

to
(JB\otimes_{B}B^{\prime})\otimes_{A}C^{\prime}

by the following:

(jx\otimes y)\otimes(z\otimes w)\longmapsto(jx\otimes z)\otimes yw\quad(j\in J,x\in B,y\in C,z\in B^{\prime},w\in C^{\prime})

and we define a map from

(JB\otimes_{B}B^{\prime})\otimes_{A}C^{\prime}

to

((J\otimes 1)(B\otimes_{A}C))\otimes_{B\otimes_{A}C}(B^{\prime}\otimes_{A}C^{\prime})

by the following:

(jx\otimes y)\otimes z\longmapsto(jx\otimes 1)\otimes(y\otimes z)\quad(j\in J,x\in B,y\in B^{\prime},z\in C^{\prime}).

These maps are the inverse map of the other.
∎

Remark 12.8
.

The lemma (
12.6
) includes another
and more direct proof of
[
Tj
, Lemma 5.3]
.

Let the notations be as in §
6
.
Let
(U,T,\iota,u)
be an object of the
log convergent site

{\rm Conv}((X,D\cup Z)/S)={\rm Conv}((X,M({D\cup Z}))/S)
.
Set
M_{U}:=M({D\cup Z})|_{U}
and let

M_{T}
be the log structure of
T
.
Because

\iota\colon U\longrightarrow T
is an exact closed immersion,

M_{T}/{\cal O}_{T}^{*}=M_{U}/{\cal O}_{U}^{*}

on
U_{\rm zar}=T_{\rm zar}
.
Hence the defining equation of

D\cap U
on
U
lifts to a local section
m
of
M_{T}
.
We define an ideal sheaf

{\cal I}^{D,{\rm conv}}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}\subset{\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}

by the following:

{\cal I}^{D,{\rm conv}}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}(T)
=
the ideal generated by
the image of
m
by
the following composite morphism

M_{T}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{T}\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{T}
.

Definition 12.9
.

We call the higher direct image sheaf

R^{h}f_{(X,D\cup Z)/S*}({\cal I}^{D,{\rm conv}}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})

in
{S}_{\rm zar}
the

log convergent cohomology sheaf with compact support

with respect to

D
and denote it by

R^{h}f_{(X,D\cup Z)/S*,{\rm c}}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z;Z)/S})
.

Let
\{D_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda}
be
a decomposition of
D
by smooth
components of
D
.
Let the notations be as in §
10
.
The exact closed immersion

\iota^{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}_{\underline{\lambda}}\colon(D_{\underline{\lambda}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}}})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}})

induces the following morphism

(12.9.1)

(-1)^{j}\iota_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}^{\underline{\lambda}_{j}*}\colon{\cal K}_{(D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)\longrightarrow

\iota_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}*}^{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}({\cal K}_{(D_{\underline{\lambda}},Z|_{D_{\underline{\lambda}}})/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}(D/S;Z)

defined by

x\otimes(\lambda_{0}\cdots\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\cdots\lambda_{k-1})\longmapsto(-1)^{j}\iota_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}^{\underline{\lambda}_{j}*}(x)\otimes(\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{k-1}).

Set

(12.9.2)

\iota^{(k-1)*}_{\rm conv}:=\sum_{\{\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{k-1}~|~\lambda_{i}\not=\lambda_{l}~(i\not=l)\}}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}a_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}{\rm conv}*}\circ((-1)^{j}\iota_{\underline{\lambda}{\rm conv}}^{\underline{\lambda}_{j}*})\colon

a_{{\rm conv}*}^{(k-1)}({\cal K}_{(D^{(k-1)},Z|_{D^{(k-1)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-1)}_{\rm conv}(D/S;Z))\longrightarrow

a_{{\rm conv}*}^{(k)}({\cal K}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm conv}(D/S;Z)).

The composite morphism

\iota^{(k)*}_{\rm conv}\circ\iota^{(k-1)*}_{\rm conv}
vanishes.
The morphism
\iota^{(k-1)*}_{\rm conv}
is
independent of the choice of
the decomposition of

\{D_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda}
by smooth components of
D/S_{1}

as proved in
[
NS1
, (2.11.2)]
.

Lemma 12.10
.

There exists a morphism of topoi

\epsilon_{\rm Rconv}:({(X,D\cup Z)/S})_{\rm Rconv}\longrightarrow({(X,Z)/S})_{\rm Rconv}

fitting into the following commutative diagram of topoi
:

(12.10.1)

\begin{CD}({(X,D\cup Z)/S})_{\rm Rconv}@>{\epsilon_{\rm Rconv}}>{}>({(X,Z)/S})_{\rm Rconv}\\
@V{Q_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}}V{}V@V{}V{Q_{(X,Z)/S}}V\\
({(X,D\cup Z)/S})_{\rm crys}@>{\epsilon}>{}>({(X,Z)/S})_{\rm crys}.\end{CD}

Proof.

First we show the existence of
\epsilon_{\rm Rconv}
.
To show this, it suffices to see that, for an object

T:=(U,T,M_{T},\iota)\in((X,D\cup Z)/S)_{\rm Rconv}
,
the object
(U,T,N_{T},\iota)
constructed in
§
5
belongs to
((X,Z)/S)_{\rm Rconv}

Zariski locally on
T
because we can define the exact functor

\epsilon_{\rm Rconv}^{*}
in the same way as
\epsilon^{*}
in
[
NS1
, p. 85]
.
Let us assume that
T
is the enlargement obtained by a closed
immersion
i:(U,(D\cup Z)|_{U})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}({\cal U},M_{{\cal U}})
,
where
({\cal U},M_{{\cal U}})
is log smooth over
S
. Since the log
structure
M_{{\cal U}}
is defined on the
Zariski site of
{\cal U}
, we have a factorization

(U,(D\cup Z)|_{U})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}({\cal U}^{\prime},M_{{\cal U}^{\prime}})\longrightarrow({\cal U},M_{{\cal U}})

of
i
Zariski locally on
{\cal U}

such that the first morphism is an exact closed immersion and that
the second morphism is log étale.
Then
T
is the log PD-envelope of the first
morphism. Hence we may suppose that
i
is an exact closed immersion. Then, by

[
NS1
, (2.1.5)]
,
we may assume that
i
is an admissible closed immersion

(U,(D\cup Z)|_{U})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}({\cal U},{\cal D}\cup{\cal Z})
.
In this case, the log structure
N^{\rm inv}_{T}
on
T
is nothing but the
pull-back of the log structure on
{\cal U}
defined by
{\cal Z}
.
Hence
(T,N^{\rm inv}_{T})
is the log enlargement obtained by
the exact closed immersion
(U,Z|_{U})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}({\cal U},{\cal Z})
.
Hence
(U,T,N^{\rm inv}_{T},\iota,\delta)
belongs to

((X,Z)/S)_{\rm Rconv}
Zariski locally on
T
.
Now it is clear that
we have the morphism
\epsilon_{\rm Rconv}
of topoi. It is easy to see
that we have the commutative diagram (
12.10.1
).
∎

Lemma 12.11
.

Let the notations be as in
(
12.10
)
.
Then the following natural morphism of functors

Q^{*}_{(X,Z)/S}R\epsilon_{*}\longrightarrow R\epsilon_{{\rm Rconv}*}Q^{*}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}

for
{\cal O}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}
-modules
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We say that, for an
{\cal O}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}
-module
F
,

F
is parasitic if
F_{T_{n}}=0
for all
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
,
where
\{T_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
is the system of the universal enlargements
of any immersion
\iota\colon(U,D\cup Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal U}

into a log smooth noetherian
log
p
-adic formal scheme over
S

which is topologically of finite type over
S
.
As in
[
NS1
, (2.11.5)]
, we have to prove that
for any parasitic
{\cal O}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}
-module

F
of
((X,D\cup Z)/S)_{\rm crys}
,

R^{q}\epsilon_{*}(F)
is also parasitic for any
q\geq 0
.

We may assume that there exists a closed immersion

\iota:(U,Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal U}

into an affine log smooth scheme over
S
, where
(U,Z)
is an open log subscheme of

(X,Z)
. Let
\{T_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be the system of the universal enlargements
of any immersion
\iota
.
On the other hand, let
us take a closed immersion
\iota^{\prime}:(U,D\cup Z)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}
into an affine log scheme which is log smooth over
S
.
Then, for any
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, we have the closed immersion

\iota_{m}:(U,(D\cup Z)|_{U})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal U}\times_{S}{\cal Y}^{m}

induced by
\iota\circ(\epsilon|_{U})
and
\iota^{\prime}
. Let
\{T(m)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be the
system of the closed immersion
\iota_{m}
over
S
.
By the log convergent version of
[
B1
, V 1.2.5]
,
we have the following equalities:

(R^{q}\epsilon_{*}(F))_{T_{n}}=R^{q}f_{(U,(D\cup Z)|_{U})/T_{n}*}(F)=R^{q}(\iota\circ(\epsilon|_{U}))_{*}\text{{\rm\v{C}A}}(F)_{n},

where
\text{{\rm\v{C}A}}(F)_{n}=F_{T(\bullet)_{n}}
is
the Čech-Alexander complex of
F
(cf.
[
B1
, V 1.2.3]
). Since

F
is parasitic, we have
F_{T(m)_{n}}=0
for any
m
.
Hence
(R^{q}\epsilon_{*}(F))_{T_{n}}=0
.
∎

Theorem 12.12
.

Let
\epsilon_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S}\colon({(X,D\cup Z)/S})_{\rm conv}\longrightarrow({(X,Z)/S})_{\rm conv}
be
the morphism of ringed topoi
forgetting the log structure along
D

((
5.1
))
. Set

(12.12.1)

\displaystyle E_{{\rm conv},c}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}):=(

\displaystyle{\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm conv}(D/S;Z)\overset{\iota^{(0)*}_{\rm conv}}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle a^{(1)}_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(1)},Z|_{D^{(1)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{\rm conv}(D/S;Z))\overset{\iota^{(1)*}_{\rm conv}}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle a^{(2)}_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(2)},Z|_{D^{(2)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2)}_{\rm conv}(D/S;Z))\overset{\iota^{(2)*}_{\rm conv}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots).

Then there exists
the following canonical isomorphism in

D^{+}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}):

(12.12.2)

Q^{*}_{(X,Z)/S}R\epsilon_{(X,D\cup Z,Z)/S*}({\cal I}^{D,{\rm conv}}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}Q^{*}_{(X,Z)/S}E_{{\rm conv},c}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}).

Proof.

(By using (
5.8
),
the proof is the same as that of

[
NS1
, (2.11.3)]
.)
Assume that we are given
the commutative diagram (
8.0.3
) for
(X,D\cup Z)
.
Let

b^{(k)}_{\bullet}\colon{\cal D}^{(k)}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\cal X}_{\bullet}
be the natural morphism.
Let
\{(U_{\bullet n},T_{\bullet n})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be
the system of
the universal enlargements of the simplicial immersion

(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}_{\bullet}

over
S
. Note that the notation
\{(U_{\bullet n},T_{\bullet n})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

denotes the different objects in this section and §
8
.
By (
12.6
) we have the following connection

\displaystyle({\cal I}^{D}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S})_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{\bullet n}}\longrightarrow({\cal I}^{D}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S})_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{\bullet n}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}/S}.

Let
{\cal F}^{\bullet}

be the isocrystal on
(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S

corresponding to the integrable log connection

\varprojlim_{n}({\cal I}^{D}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{\bullet n}}\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}({\cal I}^{D}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{\bullet n}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}/S})
.
By taking the exactification of the immersion

(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}_{\bullet}
,
we may assume that
{\cal P}_{\bullet}=({\cal X}_{\bullet},{\cal D}_{\bullet}\cup{\cal Z}_{\bullet})
,
where
{\cal D}_{\bullet}
and
{\cal Z}_{\bullet}
are transversal SNCL on

{\cal X}_{\bullet}/S
such that
{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}=({\cal X}_{\bullet},{\cal Z}_{\bullet})
.
Then we obtain the following:

(12.12.3)

\displaystyle\phantom{\,\,\,\overset{=}{\longrightarrow}}Q^{*}_{(X,Z)/S}R\epsilon_{*}({\cal I}^{D}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})

\displaystyle\overset{=}{\longrightarrow}Q^{*}_{(X,Z)/S}R\epsilon_{*}R\pi_{(X,D\cup Z)/S{\rm conv}*}\pi^{-1}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S{\rm conv}}({\cal I}^{D}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})

\displaystyle\overset{=}{\longrightarrow}R\epsilon_{{\rm Rconv}*}R\pi_{(X,D\cup Z)/S{\rm Rconv}*}Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S}\pi^{-1}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S{\rm conv}}({\cal I}^{D}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})

\displaystyle\overset{=}{\longleftarrow}R\epsilon_{{\rm Rconv}*}R\pi_{(X,D\cup Z)/S{\rm Rconv}*}Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S}({\cal F}^{\bullet})

\displaystyle\overset{=}{\longleftarrow}R\pi_{(X,Z)/S{\rm Rconv}*}Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}R\epsilon_{\bullet*}({\cal F}^{\bullet})

\displaystyle\overset{=}{\longrightarrow}R\pi_{(X,Z)/S{\rm Rconv}*}Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}R\epsilon_{\bullet*}L^{\rm UE}_{(X_{\bullet},D_{\bullet}\cup Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet}/S}(\log({\cal Z}_{\bullet}-{\cal D}_{\bullet})))

\displaystyle\overset{=}{\longleftarrow}R\pi_{(X,Z)/S{\rm Rconv}*}Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}L^{\rm UE}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet}/S}(\log({\cal Z}_{\bullet}-{\cal D}_{\bullet}))).

By the same argument as that in
[
DI
, (4.2.2) (a), (c)]
,
the following sequence

(12.12.4)

0\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet}/S}(\log({\cal Z}_{\bullet}-{\cal D}_{\bullet}))\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(0)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S)

\overset{\iota_{\bullet,{\rm zar}}^{(0)*}}{\longrightarrow}b^{(1)}_{{\bullet}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal D}_{\bullet}^{(1)}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{\bullet}|_{{\cal D}^{(1)}_{\bullet}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(1)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S))\overset{\iota_{\bullet,{\rm zar}}^{(1)*}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots

is exact. Here we define
\iota_{\bullet,{\rm zar}}^{(k)*}

similarly as for
\iota^{(k)*}_{\rm conv}
.
Hence

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet}/S}(\log({\cal Z}_{\bullet}-{\cal D}_{\bullet}))

is quasi-isomorphic
to the single complex of the following double complex

(12.12.5)

{\footnotesize{\begin{CD}\cdots @>{}>{}>\cdots\\
@A{d}A{}A@A{-d}A{}A\\
\Omega^{2}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(0)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S)@>{\iota_{\bullet,{\rm zar}}^{(0)*}}>{}>b^{(1)}_{{\bullet}*}(\Omega^{2}_{{\cal D}_{\bullet}^{(1)}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{\bullet}|_{{\cal D}_{\bullet}^{(1)}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(1)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S))\\
@A{d}A{}A@A{-d}A{}A\\
\Omega^{1}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(0)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S)@>{\iota_{\bullet,{\rm zar}}^{(0)*}}>{}>b^{(1)}_{{\bullet}*}(\Omega^{1}_{{\cal D}_{\bullet}^{(1)}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{\bullet}|_{{\cal D}_{\bullet}^{(1)}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(1)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S))\\
@A{d}A{}A@A{-d}A{}A\\
{\cal O}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(0)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S)@>{\iota_{\bullet,{\rm zar}}^{(0)*}}>{}>b^{(1)}_{{\bullet}*}({\cal O}_{{\cal D}_{\bullet}^{(1)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(1)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S))\end{CD}}}

{\footnotesize{\begin{CD}@>{}>{}>\cdots @>{}>{}>\cdots\\
@A{d}A{}A\\
@>{\iota_{\bullet,{\rm zar}}^{(1)*}}>{}>b^{(2)}_{{\bullet}*}(\Omega^{2}_{{\cal D}_{\bullet}^{(2)}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{\bullet}|_{{\cal D}_{\bullet}^{(2)}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(2)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S))@>{\iota_{\bullet,{\rm zar}}^{(2)*}}>{}>\cdots\\
@A{d}A{}A\\
@>{\iota_{\bullet,{\rm zar}}^{(1)*}}>{}>b^{(2)}_{{\bullet}*}(\Omega^{1}_{{\cal D}_{\bullet}^{(2)}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{\bullet}|_{{\cal D}_{\bullet}^{(2)}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(2)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S))@>{\iota_{\bullet,{\rm zar}}^{(2)*}}>{}>\cdots\\
@A{d}A{}A\\
@>{\iota_{\bullet,{\rm zar}}^{(1)*}}>{}>b^{(2)}_{{\bullet}*}({\cal O}_{{\cal D}_{\bullet}^{(2)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(2)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S))@>{\iota_{\bullet,{\rm zar}}^{(2)*}}>{}>\cdots.\end{CD}}}

We claim that the following sequence

(12.12.6)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow

\displaystyle Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}L^{\rm UE}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet}/S}(\log({\cal Z}_{\bullet}-{\cal D}_{\bullet})))\longrightarrow

\displaystyle Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}L^{\rm UE}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\hskip 128.0374pt\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(0)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S))\overset{Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\iota_{\bullet,{\rm zar}}^{(0)*})}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}L^{\rm UE}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(b^{(1)}_{{\bullet}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal D}_{\bullet}^{(1)}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{\bullet}|_{{\cal D}^{(1)}_{\bullet}})

\displaystyle\hskip 128.0374pt\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(1)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S)))\overset{Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\iota_{\bullet,{\rm zar}}^{(1)*})}{\longrightarrow}\cdots

is exact. Indeed, the question is local and
we have only to prove that the sequence
(
12.12.6
) for
\bullet=m
is exact for a fixed
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
That is,
we have only to prove that the following sequence

(12.12.7)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{mn}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal X}_{m}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{m}/S}(\log({\cal Z}_{m}-{\cal D}_{m}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{mn}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal X}_{m}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{m}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{m})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(0)}({\cal D}_{m}/S)

\displaystyle\overset{\iota_{m,{\rm zar}}^{(0)*}}{\longrightarrow}{\cal K}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{mn}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal X}_{m}}}b^{(1)}_{m*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal D}_{m}^{(1)}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{m}|_{{\cal D}^{(1)}_{m}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(1)}({\cal D}_{m}/S))\overset{\iota_{m,{\rm zar}}^{(1)*}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots

is exact.
We may have cartesian diagrams
(
6.6.1
) and (
6.6.2
) for the SNCD

{\cal D}_{m}\cup{\cal Z}_{m}
on
{\cal X}_{m}
;
we assume that

{\cal D}_{m}
(resp.
{\cal Z}_{m}
)
is defined by an equation

x_{1}\cdots x_{t}=0
(resp.
x_{t+1}\cdots x_{s}=0
).
Set
{\cal J}_{m}:=(x_{d+1},\ldots,x_{d^{\prime}}){\cal O}_{{\cal X}_{m}}
.
Set
{\cal X}^{\prime}_{m}:=\underline{\rm Spec}_{{\cal X}_{m}}({\cal O}_{{\cal X}_{m}}/{\cal J}_{m})

and
{\cal X}^{\prime\prime}:=\underline{\rm Spec}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}[x_{d+1},\ldots,x_{d^{\prime}}])
.
Let
{\cal D}^{\prime}_{m}
(resp.
{\cal Z}^{\prime}_{m}
) be
the closed subscheme of

{\cal X}^{\prime}_{m}
defined by an equation
x_{1}\cdots x_{t}=0

(resp.
x_{t+1}\cdots x_{s}=0
).
Let
b^{\prime}{}^{(k)}_{m}

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0})
and

\iota^{\prime}{}^{(k)*}_{m,{\rm zar}}

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})

be analogous morphisms to
b^{(k)}_{m}
and

\iota_{m,{\rm zar}}^{(k)*}
, respectively, for
{\cal X}^{\prime}_{m}
,

{\cal D}^{\prime}_{m}
and
{\cal Z}^{\prime}_{m}
.
Then we have an exact sequence

(12.12.8)

0\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}^{\prime}_{m}/S}(\log({\cal Z}^{\prime}_{m}-{\cal D}^{\prime}_{m}))\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}^{\prime}_{m}/S}(\log{\cal Z}^{\prime}_{m})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(0)}({\cal D}^{\prime}_{m}/S)

\overset{\iota^{\prime}{}^{(0)*}_{m,{\rm zar}}}{\longrightarrow}b^{\prime}{}^{(1)}_{\!\!m*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal D}_{m}^{\prime}{}^{(1)}/S}(\log{\cal Z}^{\prime}_{m}|_{{\cal D}^{\prime}{}^{(1)}_{m}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(1)}({\cal D}^{\prime}_{m}/S))\overset{\iota^{\prime}{}^{(1)*}_{m,{\rm zar}}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots.

As in the proof of (
2.12
) (2),
we see that

\displaystyle({\cal O}_{S}[[x_{d+1},\ldots,x_{d^{\prime}}]][t_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r},|\underline{m}|=n]/(x^{\underline{m}}-\pi t_{\underline{m}}))^{\widehat{}}

is a flat
{\cal O}_{S}
-module.
Hence to apply the tensor product

\displaystyle\widehat{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{S}}({\cal O}_{S}[[x_{d+1},\ldots,x_{d^{\prime}}]][t_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r},|\underline{m}|=n]/(x^{\underline{m}}-\pi t_{\underline{m}}))^{\widehat{}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal X}^{\prime\prime}}}\Omega^{q}_{{\cal X}^{\prime\prime}/S}\quad(q\in{\mathbb{N}})

to the exact sequence (
12.12.8
) preserves the exactness.
Because

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{mn}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal X}_{m}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{m}/S}(\log({\cal Z}_{m}-{\cal D}_{m}))

\displaystyle\simeq\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}^{\prime}_{m}/S}(\log({\cal Z}^{\prime}_{m}-{\cal D}^{\prime}_{m}))\otimes_{{\cal O}_{S}}{}({\cal O}_{S}[[x_{d+1},\ldots,x_{d^{\prime}}]][t_{\underline{m}}~|~\underline{m}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{r},|\underline{m}|=n]/(x^{\underline{m}}-\pi t_{\underline{m}}))^{\widehat{}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal X}^{\prime\prime}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}^{\prime\prime}/S}

and because the similar formulas for

{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{T}}_{mn}}\otimes_{{\cal X}_{m}}b^{(k)}_{m*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal D}_{m}^{(k)}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{m}|_{{\cal D}^{(k)}_{m}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(k)}({\cal D}_{m}/S))

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})
hold,
we have the exactness of (
12.12.7
).

By (
4.5
) and (
12.12.6
),
we have the following quasi-isomorphism

(12.12.9)

\displaystyle Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}L^{\rm UE}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet}/S}(\log({\cal Z}_{\bullet}-{\cal D}_{\bullet})))

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\{Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}L^{\rm UE}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(0)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}a^{(1){\log}}_{\bullet{\rm conv}*}L^{\rm UE}_{(D^{(1)}_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet}|_{D^{(1)}_{\bullet}})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal D}_{\bullet}^{(1)}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{\bullet}|_{{\cal D}^{(1)}_{\bullet}}))

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(1)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S)),-d)\longrightarrow\cdots\}.

Applying the direct image

R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm Rconv}*}

to (
12.12.9
),
we obtain the following isomorphism:

(12.12.10)

R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm Rconv}*}Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}L^{\rm UE}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet}/S}(\log({\cal Z}_{\bullet}-{\cal D}_{\bullet})))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\{R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm Rconv}*}Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}L^{\rm UE}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(0)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S))\longrightarrow

\displaystyle(R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm Rconv}*}Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}a^{(1){\log}}_{\bullet{\rm conv}*}

\displaystyle L^{\rm UE}_{(D^{(1)}_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet}|_{D^{(1)}})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal D}_{\bullet}^{(1)}/S}(\log({\cal Z}_{\bullet}|_{{\cal D}_{\bullet}^{(1)}}))

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(1)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S),-d)\longrightarrow\cdots\}.

By (
12.12.3
) and (
6.9.1
),
the isomorphism (
12.12.10
) is nothing
but an isomorphism (
12.12.2
).

We have to show that the isomorphism
(
12.12.2
) is independent of the datum (
8.0.3
).

Let the notations be as in §
7
for the data (
8.0.3
)’s.
Let

\displaystyle R\eta_{{\rm Rconv}*}\colon{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet\bullet},Z_{\bullet\bullet})/S}(

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet\bullet},Z_{\bullet\bullet})/S}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}({\cal O}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}))

be a morphism of filtered derived categories as similarly defined in
in §
7
.
Then we have the following commutative diagram by
the cohomological descent:

\tiny{\begin{CD}R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm Rconv}*}Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}L^{\rm UE}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet}/S}(\log({\cal Z}_{\bullet}-{\cal D}_{\bullet})))@>{\sim}>{}>\hskip 7.3612pt\hskip 7.3612pt\hskip 7.3612pt\\
@V{}V{}V\\
R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm Rconv}*}R\eta_{{\rm Rconv}*}Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet\bullet},Z_{\bullet\bullet})/S}L^{\rm UE}_{(X_{\bullet\bullet},Z_{\bullet\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet\bullet}/S}(\log({\cal Z}_{\bullet\bullet}-{\cal D}_{\bullet\bullet})))@>{\sim}>{}>\hskip 7.3612pt\hskip 7.3612pt\hskip 7.3612pt\end{CD}}

\tiny{\begin{CD}\{R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm Rconv}*}Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}L^{\rm UE}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(0)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S))\longrightarrow\\
@V{}V{}V\\
\{R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm Rconv}*}R\eta_{{\rm Rconv}*}Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet\bullet},Z_{\bullet\bullet})/S}L^{\rm UE}_{(X_{\bullet\bullet},Z_{\bullet\bullet})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\bullet\bullet}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{\bullet\bullet})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(0)}({\cal D}_{\bullet\bullet}/S))\longrightarrow\end{CD}}

\tiny{\begin{CD}\begin{aligned} (R({\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm Rconv}}Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet})/S}a^{(1){\log}}_{\bullet{\rm conv}})_{*}L^{\rm UE}_{(D^{(1)}_{\bullet},Z_{\bullet}|_{D^{(1)}})/S}(&\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal D}_{\bullet}^{(1)}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{\bullet}|_{{\cal D}_{\bullet}^{(1)}})\\
&\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(1)}({\cal D}_{\bullet}/S)),-d)\longrightarrow\cdots\}\end{aligned}\\
@V{}V{}V\\
\begin{aligned} (R{\pi}_{(X,Z)/S{\rm Rconv}*}R\eta_{{\rm Rconv}*}Q^{*}_{(X_{\bullet\bullet},Z_{\bullet\bullet})/S}a^{(1){\log}}_{\bullet\bullet{\rm conv}*}&L^{\rm UE}_{(D^{(1)}_{\bullet\bullet},Z_{\bullet\bullet}|_{D^{(1)}})/S}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal D}_{\bullet\bullet}^{(1)}/S}(\log{\cal Z}_{\bullet\bullet}|_{{\cal D}_{\bullet\bullet}^{(1)}})\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm zar}^{(1)}({\cal D}_{\bullet\bullet}/S)),-d)\longrightarrow\cdots\}.\end{aligned}\end{CD}}

Hence the isomorphism (
12.12.2
) is independent of the data
(
8.0.3
)’s.
∎

Let

P^{D}_{\rm c}:=\{P^{D,k}_{\rm c}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

be the stupid filtration on

E_{{\rm conv},c}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})
:

P^{D,k}_{\rm c}E_{{\rm conv},c}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})=a^{(\bullet\geq k){\log}}_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(\bullet\geq k)},Z|_{D^{(\bullet\geq k)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(\bullet\geq k)\log}_{\rm conv}(D/S;Z)).

Then, by (
12.12
),
we have a filtered complex

(E_{{\rm conv},c}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}_{\rm c})\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S})
.

Definition 12.13
.

We call

(E_{{\rm conv},c}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}_{\rm c})

the
weight-filtered vanishing cycle convergent complex
with compact support
of
{\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}

(or
(X,D\cup Z)/S
)
with respect to
D
.
Set

(E_{{\rm isozar},c}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}_{\rm c}):=Ru^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S*}(E_{{\rm conv},c}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}_{\rm c}).

We call

(E_{{\rm isozar},c}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}_{\rm c})

the
weight-filtered vanishing cycle isozariskian complex
with compact support
of
{\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}

(or
(X,D\cup Z)/S
) with respect to
D
.

By the definition of

(E_{{\rm isozar},c}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}_{\rm c})
,
there exists the following canonical isomorphism
in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S})):

(12.13.1)

\displaystyle E_{{\rm isozar},c}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\{Ru^{\rm conv}_{(X,Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)\log}_{\rm conv}(D/S;Z))\longrightarrow

\displaystyle a^{(1)}_{{\rm zar}*}(Ru^{\rm conv}_{(D^{(1)},Z|_{D^{(1)}})/S*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(1)},Z|_{D^{(1)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)\log}_{\rm conv}(D/S;Z)),-d)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\cdots\}.

Remark-Definition 12.14
.

Because the notation for the right hand side
of (
12.13.1
) is only suggestive,
we have to give the strict definition of it;
