we can give it as in
[
NS1
, (2.11.8)]
.

Corollary 12.15
.

E_{{\rm isozar},c}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})=Ru^{\rm conv}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S*}({\cal I}^{D,{\rm conv}}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})
.

By applying
Rf_{*}
to both hand sides of
(
12.13.1
) (cf. (
12.14
)),
we have the following canonical isomorphism

(12.15.1)

\displaystyle Rf_{(X,D\cup Z)/S*,{\rm c}}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z;Z)/S})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\{Rf_{(X,Z)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,Z)/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(0)\log}(D/S;Z))

\longrightarrow(Rf_{(D^{(1)},Z|_{D^{(1)}})/S*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(1)},Z|_{D^{(1)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(1)\log}(D/S;Z)),-d)\longrightarrow\cdots\}.

Next we give the filtered base change theorem,
the filtered Künneth formula and
the filtered Poincaré duality of
the log convergent cohomology sheaf
with compact support.

In
[
NS1
, (2.11)]
we have set

\displaystyle E_{\rm zar,c}({\cal O}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}):=

\displaystyle Ru_{(X,Z)/S*}({\cal O}_{(X,Z)/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(0)\log}(D/S;Z))

\longrightarrow(Ru_{(D^{(1)},Z|_{D^{(1)}})/S*}({\cal O}_{(D^{(1)},Z|_{D^{(1)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(1)\log}(D/S;Z)),-d)\longrightarrow\cdots\}.

Let
P^{D}
be the stupid filtration on

E_{\rm zar,c}({\cal O}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})
.

Theorem 12.16

(
Comparison isomorphism).

There exists a canonical isomorphism

(12.16.1)

(E_{\rm isozar,c}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(E_{\rm zar,c}({\cal O}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))
.

Proof.

By (
11.10.2
) we may assume that
\pi{\cal V}=p{\cal V}
.
By using the morphism (
11.4.7
) for
the constant simplicial case
and the explicit descriptions (
11.9.2
) and
(
11.11.1
) for the constant simplicial case,
we immediately obtain (
12.16
) by (
11.12
).
∎

Proposition 12.17
.

Assume that
f\colon X\longrightarrow S_{1}
is proper.
Then

(Rf_{(X,D\cup Z)/S*,{\rm c}}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z;Z)/S}),P^{D}_{\rm c})

is a filteredly strictly perfect complex in the sense of
[
NS1
, (2.10.8)]
.

Proof.

(
12.17
) follows from (
12.16
) and
[
NS1
, (2.11.12)]
.
∎

Proposition 12.18
.

Let
Y
be a quasi-compact smooth scheme over

S_{1}

(
with trivial log structure
)
.
Let
f\colon(X,D\cup Z)\longrightarrow Y
be
a morphism of log schemes such that

\overset{\circ}{f}\colon X\longrightarrow Y
is smooth,
quasi-compact and quasi-separated
and such that
D\cup Z
is a relative SNCD
over
Y
.
(
In particular,
D\cup Z
is
also a relative SNCD on
X
over
S_{1}.)

Let
f_{(X,Z)}\colon(X,Z)\longrightarrow Y

be the induced morphism by
f
.
Let

\begin{CD}(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})@>{g}>{}>(X,D\cup Z)\\
@V{f^{\prime}}V{}V@V{}V{f}V\\
Y^{\prime}@>{h}>{}>Y\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S^{\prime}_{1}@>{}>{}>S_{1}\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram such that
the upper rectangle is cartesian,
where
Y^{\prime}
is a quasi-compact smooth scheme over
S^{\prime}_{1}
.
Then the following hold
:

(1)

The natural morphism

(12.18.1)

{\cal O}_{S^{\prime}}\otimes^{L}_{{\cal O}_{S}}Rf_{(X,Z)/S*}(E_{{\rm conv},c}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}_{\rm c})\longrightarrow

R{f^{\prime}}_{(X^{\prime},Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}*}(E_{{\rm conv},c}({\cal K}_{(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S}),P^{D^{\prime}}_{\rm c})

is an isomorphism.

(2)

There exists a natural isomorphism

(12.18.2)

{\cal O}_{S^{\prime}}\otimes^{L}_{{\cal O}_{S}}Rf_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal I}^{D}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S})\longrightarrow R{f^{\prime}}_{{\rm conv}*}({\cal I}^{D^{\prime}}_{(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})/S^{\prime}})

which is compatible with the isomorphism

(
12.18.1
)
.

Using the filtered complex

(E_{\rm conv,c}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z)/S}),P^{D}_{\rm c})
,
we have the following spectral sequence

(12.18.3)

E_{1,{\rm c}}^{k,h-k}((X,D\cup Z)/S)=R^{h-k}f_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S*}({\cal K}_{(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})/S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm conv}^{(k){\log}}(D/S;Z))

\Longrightarrow R^{h}f_{(X,D\cup Z)/S*,{\rm c}}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z;Z)/S}).

Definition 12.19
.

We call the spectral sequence (
12.18.3
)
the
weight spectral sequence of

(X,D\cup Z)/S

with respect to

D

for the log convergent cohomology
with compact support
. If
Z=\emptyset
, then
we call (
12.18.3
)
the
weight spectral sequence of

(X,D)/S

for the log convergent cohomology
with compact support
.

Let
P^{D,\bullet}_{\rm c}
be the filtration on

R^{h}f_{(X,D\cup Z)/S*,{\rm c}}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z;Z)/S})

induced by the spectral sequence
(
12.18.3
).
Since
P^{D,\bullet}_{\rm c}
is a decreasing filtration,
we also consider the following increasing filtration

P^{D}_{{\bullet},{\rm c}}
:

(12.19.1)

P^{D}_{h-{\bullet},{\rm c}}R^{h}f_{(X,D\cup Z)/S*,{\rm c}}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z;Z)/S})=P^{D,\bullet}_{\rm c}R^{h}f_{(X,D\cup Z)/S*,{\rm c}}({\cal K}_{(X,D\cup Z;Z)/S}).

Proposition 12.20
.

The boundary morphism

d_{1}^{k,h-k}\colon E_{1,c}^{k,h-k}((X,D\cup Z)/S)\longrightarrow E_{1,c}^{k+1,h-k}((X,D\cup Z)/S)

is equal to

\iota^{(k){\log}*}_{\rm conv}
.

Proposition 12.21
.

Let
u
be the morphism in
(
9.0.1
)
.
Let
u_{1}\colon S_{1}\longrightarrow S^{\prime}_{1}
be the induced morphism by
u
.
Let
(X,D\cup Z)
and
(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})

be smooth schemes
with relative SNCD’s over
S_{1}
and
S^{\prime}_{1}
, respectively.
Let

(12.21.1)

\begin{CD}(X,D\cup Z)@>{g}>{}>(X^{\prime},D^{\prime}\cup Z^{\prime})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{1}@>{u_{1}}>{}>S^{\prime}_{1}\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram of log schemes
such that the morphism
g
induces morphisms

g^{(k)}\colon(D^{(k)},Z|_{D^{(k)}})\longrightarrow(D^{\prime}{}^{(k)},Z^{\prime}|_{D^{\prime}{}^{(k)}})

of log schemes over
u_{1}

for all
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Then the isomorphism
(\ref{eqn:18.10.?})

and the spectral
sequence
(\ref{eqn:spcsz})
are
functorial with respect to

g_{\rm conv}^{*}
.

Theorem 12.22

(
Künneth formula).

Let the notations be as in
(
11.16
)

for the constant case.
Then the following hold
:

(1)
Set

H_{i.{\rm c}}:=Rf_{(X_{i},Z_{i}){\rm conv}*}(E^{\log,Z_{i}}_{{\rm conv},{\rm c}}({\cal K}_{(X_{i},D_{i}\cup Z_{i})/S}),P^{D_{i}}_{\rm c}).

Then there exists a canonical isomorphism

(12.22.1)

H_{1,{\rm c}}\otimes_{{\cal K}_{Y/S}}^{L}H_{2,{\rm c}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}H_{3,{\rm c}}.

(2)
The isomorphism
(
12.22.1
)

is compatible
with the base change isomorphism.

Proof.

By (
11.10.2
) we may assume that
\pi{\cal V}=p{\cal V}
.
This follows from (
12.16
) and
[
NS1
, (2.11.19)]
.
∎

Theorem 12.23

(
Poincaré duality).

Assume that
X/S
is projective and that
the relative dimension of
X/S
is
of pure dimension
d
.
Then there exists a perfect pairing

(12.23.1)

R^{h}f^{\rm conv}_{(X,D)/S*,{\rm c}}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S})\otimes R^{2d-h}f^{\rm conv}_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S})\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{S}(-d),

which is strictly compatible
with the weight filtration. That is,
the natural morphism

(12.23.2)

R^{h}f_{(X,D)/S*,{\rm c}}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S})\longrightarrow{\cal H}{\it om}_{{\cal K}_{S}}(R^{2d-h}f_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal K}_{(X,D)/S}),{\cal K}_{S}(-d))

is an isomorphism of
weight-filtered convergent
F
-isocrystals on

S/V
.

Proof.

By (
11.10.2
) we may assume that
\pi{\cal V}=p{\cal V}
.
Now this theorem follows from
the comparison theorems (
11.12
), (
12.16
) and

[
NS1
, (2.19.1)]
.
∎
